# Exploritaire development baseline

- Anthro Hero sprite design (Eric, 2026-11-27): use the approved animal-forward dressed turnaround for standing, walking, and lowered ready sprites across world and isometric views. Follow the anatomy, wardrobe, and soft watercolor guidance in [ART.md](./ART.md#animal-forward-painterly-hero).

- Village world travel: in `village.html` and `?iso`, selected kin and parties walk along the shortest available route between tile centers. Routes skirt water, locked fog, and occupied building and landmark tiles. The Curious Sapling, unlocked biomes, and den worksites can be destinations for their existing interactions, which open on arrival. Reduced motion resolves the same route immediately.

- Village scene titles (standing rule): use Aspen Grove's shared title treatment by default. Set the name in a warm serif with gold italic emphasis, a fading gold underline, and a soft shadow directly over the scene. Show the scene name without instructional copy unless explicitly requested.

- Village scene depth (standing rule): follow Aspen Grove's foreshortening for painted relief. Anchor props to the ground in staggered depth bands, with smaller and softer objects near the horizon and larger objects toward the foreground. Vary the spacing and size within bands. Keep puzzle cards readable and interactive over the scenery.

- Village Simple Den inspiration: take Hero to the Curious Sapling to open its seven-column, four-row infinifill tableau. The Simple Den Inspiration Card starts four cards deep in column five. Playing it reveals the holocard. Drag the revealed card onto Hero's foundation to add it to the fanned hand, then drag it onto eligible open land to start a den worksite.

- Village opening map: Hero starts five squares south of the Curious Sapling at `(0, -5)`. The home camera frames the opening position. The bottom-right Hollow and Kin selector is removed. Interact with a wanderer at the gate to open the Kin panel.

- Tableau receipt flights (standing rule): every accepted tableau receipt flies the source card to the receiving foundation. Use the same flight for tap, drag, keyboard, and auto-solve with uniform card scaling and immediate rules resolution. A following receipt may finish the previous flight early. Respect reduced motion and clean up on scene changes. Stock resets without a tableau source and rejected moves do not create receipt flights.

- Puzzle foundation sizing: foundation cards and their drop targets share the displayed width and height of tableau cards at every supported viewport size. Fit portraits, ranks, and stamina indicators inside that frame.

- Village den progression: place the Simple Den Inspiration Card on an empty plot and deploy a kin with Dig. Its authored tableau has seven columns and four cards per column, replenished from beneath after each receipt. Excavate 24 soil. On the first workday, the opening ranks and stock can only clear 14 surface soil. Resting on the ground preserves progress, opens the deeper stock ranks at dawn, and leaves the worker one AP short of a full refill. Dig receipts cost daylight only and remain legal at zero AP. Drawing from stock resets the foundation and costs the worker one AP from their stamina pool plus the usual draw time. A finished Simple Den houses one kin and initially assigns that slot to its digger. Its Improved Den Inspiration Card applies to the same site for three wood and adds a three-layer excavation and a second resident slot. Eight hours of overnight rest grants assigned kin +1 daily max AP and +1 max stamina from a Simple Den or +2 max stamina from an Improved Den.

- Sunny Hedge uses leaf cover: picking a berry brushes aside the leaves on the top berry cards of orthogonally adjacent bushes. Covered cards show a leaf glyph and their rank. They become playable when uncovered.

- Village AP and stamina: only drawing a stock card to replace a foundation costs AP. Each successful draw spends one AP from the kin's shared stamina pool across biomes. Opening deals, tableau receipts, found-card receipts and collecting wood cost no AP. At zero AP a kin can still receive legal cards, including through Ctrl auto-solve. Daylight costs and overnight recovery follow each puzzle's rules.

- Village receipt daylight: each accepted receipt advances the village clock by an estimate of the work it represents. Allow 3 minutes for investigating the Curious Sapling, 4 for gathering berries, 6 for cutting into a tree (including found-card receipts), 8 for loosening stone, and 12 for clearing a section of excavation. This is first-pass tuning. If a receipt uses the remaining daylight, keep its progress and end the day at dusk. Rejected receipts spend no time.

- Aspen Grove found cards: each tree has a seeded one-in-three chance to drop a rank card when it lands. Pick it up separately from the wood. Found cards persist in Hollow's Aspen Grove reserve and can be played onto a matching foundation for the usual damage at no AP cost. Ctrl includes them in its legal sequences.

- Tableau Ctrl auto-solve (standing rule, Eric, 2026-11-25): every deployed tableau puzzle must map either Ctrl key to auto-solve. Tap Ctrl to play exactly the first receipt of the best known legal sequence. Hold Ctrl to continually replan and play optimal legal receipts until the last Ctrl is released, the puzzle completes, or legal moves run out. Use the ordinary receipt path and respect pause, manual input, focus loss, and scene changes. Stock draws and turn advance remain manual.

- Village map and Aspen Grove (Eric, 2026-11-25): an unlocked biome can be inspected without deploying kin. Left-drag empty map space to group the enclosed kin. Click an individual portrait within a party to highlight it, then drag that portrait to split it out. Aspen Grove has 18 selectable aspens in three rows, with smaller trees in the background and slight size variation within each row. Existing tree deals and damage carry over.

- Desktop Hero pointer behavior (Eric, 2026-11-24): Hero follows a toy during an active drag and fetches it after release. Ordinary pointer movement never makes Hero chase the pointer.

- Desktop Hero toybox return (Eric, 2026-11-24): click the toybox while Hero is carrying an item to send her back to stow it. The box closes after she returns the item.

- Desktop Flurry (Eric, 2026-11-24): opening the toybox releases Flurry with her World Tree idle expressions and motion. Drag her out to start a playful chase between desktop platforms. Her falling snow gathers on the same surfaces Hero can use and melts over about five seconds after mouse activity. Click the toybox to recall Flurry and any item Hero is carrying, then close the lid. After release, Flurry may bring an existing Edge window forward and select an open Google Calendar tab only when Desktop Hero’s Window manipulation setting permits it. Disabled is the default.

- Desktop Flurry silhouette and chase (Eric, 2026-11-24): draw Flurry face-on as a round 2D sprite at every angle. During free flight she generally keeps a lead on Hero, with occasional short circuits around her before flitting away again.

- Desktop browse-and-chase refinement (Eric, 2026-11-24): Hero can dig down through broad browser control ledges to reach lower controls and Calendar events. Flurry is 25% smaller and keeps a visible gap above Hero's head when passing close, using a side pass if the display edge leaves no room overhead.

- Desktop Flurry vertical motion (Eric, 2026-11-24): approach Hero's clearance height with eased steering. Keep a gentle bob and in-plane banking during flight, with no vertical snapping when Hero enters or leaves her path.

- Desktop Flurry monitor travel (Eric, 2026-11-24): during free flight, visit all available displays, crossing shared edges where possible. Linger near a destination platform and give Hero that route target so she can follow on reachable terrain.

- World Tree portal arrival (Eric, 2026-11-24): `portals.html?tree` spawns Hero at (4.426587655513873, 57.143042928323396), heading 3.145254700920247, and flies directly to the captured ground-level shot in `world1111Layout.ts`. Skip and replay use the same route. Reduced motion lands directly in the captured shot. Hold the landed camera until movement or camera input. Book page 1111 retains its own arrival.

- Woods `?combat` Meteo cinematic (Eric, 2026-11-23): casting a completed Meteo card invokes the existing Woods star-pan, constellation, meteor flight, impact and camera-return sequence. Apply Hand + Board damage once at impact, avoid the legacy encounter damage handler, and hold combat input until the camera returns. Keep reduced-motion support and the option to skip the animation.

- Woods `?combat` holocards and camera (Eric, 2026-11-23): contain artwork within card borders at rest; mouse hover may extend only the art by 10% on each horizontal side. Use high-contrast phase placeholders with distinct filled styling and a receipt-fill glow; fills and combat actions cue gentle cinematic focus/zoom without moving gameplay targets. Reduced motion suppresses camera/slot animation. Reuse the existing battle free-camera controller and castle gestures: drag orbit, right/Shift drag pan, wheel/pinch zoom; manual WASD pan and Q/E height. Manual input takes ownership until Cinematic/Reframe is selected. Keep camera controls available before a hand opens; cinematic framing clears scenery and leaves both kin visible above the supply. Living actors and their HP plates can always open their ability hands for inspection, while energy/cooldowns still disable unaffordable actions. On the cover screen, lift the left ability dock within the arena so it does not cover Hero.


- Woods `?combat` energy (Eric, 2026-11-23): each actor has an independent energy pool. Hero and Mochi each start with 3 energy and refill their own pool each turn without carryover. Spending Hero energy never spends Mochi energy, and vice versa. Fast Swipes and Dig cost 1; Fira and Sticky Paws cost 2; Meteo costs 3. Starting/swapping a channel pays once, while continuing channels, receipts, wildfire and casting a completed spell cost no additional energy. Cancelling does not refund energy. Utilities charge only on a successful swap/theft, alongside their existing cooldowns. Derive pool size and costs from centralized defaults plus pool bonuses, actor-specific global cost adjustments and per-ability adjustments for future abilities/modifiers; clamp derived values at zero. Legacy Fire/Firaga cost 1/3. This tuning is provisional for playtesting.

- Woods `?combat` actor ability hands (Eric, 2026-11-23): show the supply as soon as the actor ability hand opens, before spending energy or choosing an ability. Remove the selector button; eligible Hero/Mochi actors glow with an outer stroke and clicking an actor fans out that actor's abilities. Hero has Fira, Meteo and Dig (swap any exposed top card with any second-from-top column card, 2-turn cooldown). Mochi has Fast Swipes and Sticky Paws (choose and steal a receipt from the enemy's active ability into the player's hand, reopening its requirement, 4-turn cooldown). Utility cooldowns start only on a successful action and do not replace an active channel.

- Woods `?combat` status plates (Eric, 2026-11-23): anchor the far team's HP UX above its actor. Keep status plates cozy and compact, combining HP and stagger in one meter housing and showing weaknesses as element icons with accessible names instead of text.

- Woods `?combat` ability docks (Eric, 2026-11-23): enemy ability cards dock in the upper-right corner; player ability cards share the lower-left dock above the supply. Keep enemy HP and stagger on its actor plate.

- Woods `?combat` ability selection (Eric, 2026-11-23): choose abilities from the card/hand flyout. Remove idle channeling panes and their choose-spell/ability buttons; show only active ability cards as receipt targets above the supply.

- Playing card proportions (standing rule): Enforce a 63:88 width-to-height ratio for every card object across Exploritaire. Landscape cards use 88:63. Apply this to faces, backs, hands, tableaus, holocards, foundations, world cards, previews, and drag and animation states. Every new puzzle must size the visible kin foundation and its card drop target to the card frame, including foundations that display a portrait, rank, or stamina. Set the ratio on the card itself in CSS or geometry and scale it uniformly. When space is tight, resize or rearrange the layout and fit the artwork inside the frame. Verify the ratio at supported viewport sizes and orientations.

- Galaxy Z Fold 7 support (standing rule, Eric, 2026-11-23): every UI change must work and be checked at desktop sizes (1280×720 to about 1912×914) and on the Galaxy Z Fold 7: unfolded landscape (about 884×797 CSS px), unfolded portrait (about 797×884) and the cover screen in portrait (about 412×915). Nothing may overlap, clip or need page scrolling at those sizes, cards keep 63:88, text stays at 16px or more, and drag and tap both work. Rearrange the layout (stack trays, move controls beside the board) rather than shrinking text. This replaces the earlier desktop-only focus; cover-screen landscape is not a target yet. Browser checks still follow the standing validation limits below.

- Woods `?combat` stagger break and Har Megiddo (Eric, 2026-11-23): stagger replaces the intent-stealing idea. Damage fills the enemy's stagger gauge; a full gauge staggers it, which breaks any spell it is casting and makes it lose its next action. Cards already placed in a broken spell spill onto the table and can be played as receipts like any other card. On its first turn the Khadroot starts casting Har Megiddo. Its three phases require 4/K, 2/8/J, and J/Q/K/A, respectively. Only the active phase accepts receipts and its ranks can be filled in any order. Har Megiddo casts when its final phase completes. A layout button cycles the supply between the 7-column tableau, the flip deck and the 4-column board + hand (`?supply=tableau|flip|board`).

- Ability casting readiness (Eric, 2026-11-23): Once all requirements are complete, player and enemy abilities can be played immediately. An ability has a build-up state between completion and play only when explicitly flagged. Khadroot can play Har Megiddo as soon as its final phase is complete.

- Combat hand board channel presentation: choose abilities from the hand selector. The dock only shows active channels, using holocards with separate art, text and pip parallax layers. Finite spells show one pip per required receipt and light them as receipts clear; Fast Swipes shows its ongoing level and count. The channel lanes, board, hand and turn controls share a responsive dock sized for the unfolded Galaxy Z Fold7 in either orientation.

- Woods target-first spell deals (Eric, 2026-11-21): drop an ability onto its field target to lock it and open a dealt 3×3 over that actor. Fade scenery into the starfield while keeping the live actors, a single foundation rank and power visible. All spell deals offer at least one receipt. Khadroot resistance limits Bite to 1–3 potential receipts; fire weakness gives Meteo at least 10. Evaluate finite directional backfill. Cast manually at any power or automatically after the final legal receipt. This replaces the full-field foundation pick and pre-charge sky flight.

- Combat Woods direct start and spell casting (Eric, 2026-11-21): the explicit `?combat` link starts in Woods with the landed camera. All Woods abilities can cast at base power with zero receipts; cornerstones and Meteo paint transfer are visual, and each receipt still adds one power. Meteo applies damage on impact.

- Standalone 3×3 team-up (Eric, 2026-11-20): open directly on eight grid cards around an empty foundation with five distinct ranks in hand, represented by Mochi, Hero, Demi, Banks and River. Eligible hand cards with the longest known grid sequence glimmer, including ties. Team-up follows the current sequenced rank (including A/K wrap), returns the previous hand anchor tapped, and replaces the current foundation rank. Each legal grid receipt gives the active kin 1 AP, which persists across team-ups and turns. Controls sit beside the grid and the hand fans out beneath the center foundation. End turn restores all five hand cards for a fresh foundation choice with the grid preserved. This standalone mode uses the hand in place of stock and keeps directional backfill.

- City free table (Eric, 2026-11-20): rebuild `book.html?city` as one open, card-stacking table instead of trays/panels. Every piece (den, kin, resource cards, token piles, face-down zones, enemies, recipes, buildings) is a loose card you drag anywhere; stacking acts: kin on a resource starts golf, on a zone scouts it, on an enemy joins its party (Fight in the enemy's header); piles on piles discover recipes, piles on a recipe build it, berries on the Drying Rack dry them. The table pans and zooms: pinch to zoom and three fingers to pan on touch, wheel to zoom and drag empty table to pan with a mouse, plus a Fit button. The table must stay testable on phones (portrait and landscape); gather and battle screens scale to fit. This supersedes the rail/bench/ledger layout in the entry below and is an exception to desktop-only checks.

- City building first pass (Eric, 2026-11-20): `book.html?city` is a persistent board-game table (wood, felt, brass, tokens) with a Reset button. Drag kin onto resource cards to start golf; a 7-column / 3×3 grid toggle switches layouts, and a paid foundation carries across a mid-game switch. Receipts become tokens; every foundation, including the first, costs that kin 1 stamina, and each kin has its own stamina, appetite and HP. Rest feeds kin from stored food and refills stamina; unfed kin wake malnourished (-2 max stamina, -25% max HP). Start with Hero and Mochi and the Den Clearing; scout zones for stamina, fight dark slimes and khad roots to rescue kin and win recipe cards, and discover other recipes by combining token stacks. Combat is Rally and Break (receipts chip and stagger the target and earn AP; a break skips the enemy turn; khad roots add a Deep Breath-style Thorn Whip intent); starting a fight costs each kin 1 stamina, turns do not. Combat uses 7 columns only for now. Values are provisional first-pass guesses. Proposal: docs/research/city-slice-proposal.md; code: src/city/.

- Handgrid opening chain (Eric, 2026-11-18): each four-card hand in the Portals handgrid preview offers at least one opener with four consecutive legal tableau plays using dealt cards, including directional backfill. The opener itself is excluded from the four plays. Use seeded deal filtering with a bounded fallback.

- Starfield grid fit and transparent wash (Eric, 2026-11-16): fit all 80 Woods spell field cards into the available starfield without scrolling or clipping; use a wide 16-by-5 deal and resize with the viewport. Keep entity, hand and casting controls clear. Use the scenery-window transparent watercolor wash with readable rank plaques so live stars remain visible through card faces; keep the current foundation opaque over collected cards. Preserve selection, receipts and casting rules. The Woods star grid has no stock card, stock count, draw or stock-reselect action (Eric, 2026-11-16). This supersedes the minimum-scale scrolling field above.

- Entity-owned ability selection and larger stargrid (Eric, 2026-11-16): the first `?combat` hand contains Lustro, Flurry and Hero entity cards, with no Star or Bite ability card. Tap or grab an entity to pin it on screen and fan only its abilities. Lustro grants Meteo (Fireball renamed, mechanics unchanged); Hero grants Bite; Flurry exposes scaffolds for the existing ice kit. Only ability cards may drop onto a stargrid foundation. Double the full stargrid from 40 to 80 cards and double authored card dimensions; keep large tiles readable through bounded scrolling and retain the shared 3×3 play/stock/refill model. Preserve holo/lenticular presentation, pause, reduced motion and cast rules. Flurry effects are temporary dev scaffolds; details and limitations: docs/design/woods-entity-abilities.md.

- Lustro and lenticular spell hand (Eric, 2026-11-16): replace the Fireball hand card in `?combat` with Lustro, a squat upright flame sticker with separate expressive eyes/brows/mouth and no rosy cheeks. Preserve inherited fire damage, receipt power, four-cornerstone gate, paint transfer and tracked meteor effect. All four hand cards adopt the established shared parallax/lenticular card presentation with pointer/gyro spring, foil, independently layered art, global optics controls, static low/reduced-motion behavior and pause. Keep labels clear and native selection, drag/foundation targeting and casting unchanged. Art and reusable expression inventory: docs/art/lustro-v1.md.

- Flurry hand card (Eric, 2026-11-16): replace the Woods spell test’s Blizzard card with Flurry, using her existing painted ice body and content face. Flurry takes that slot’s frost damage, slow effect, cold constellation, receipt power and hostile targeting rules. This replaces the Blizzard test sibling; it does not grant or change world sticker ownership.

- Meteor cast camera and target colors (Eric, 2026-11-16): committing Fireball pans the real camera up to the earned, lit constellation with no watercolor fill, holds briefly, then launches the shader meteor small and far away. Track its descent with the camera and grow its apparent size by distance until it strikes the actual target; apply Fireball damage and impact feedback on arrival. Return smoothly to the saved exploration camera. Eligible actors use a white silhouette stroke; only a dragged ability over its eligible actor turns that stroke hostile red or support blue. Preserve transparent hit regions, four-receipt gate, card painting, power hooks, pause, reduced motion and cleanup. This supersedes the fixed-camera screen-space cast and always-colored target strokes.

- Shader Fireball casting and power hooks (Eric, 2026-11-16): use the supplied polar-noise sphere plus tapered steam/flame cylinders with isolated bloom as Fireball’s actual cast effect. Fly from the remembered starfield location down into the selected target and time impact feedback to arrival. Keep watercolor art for the card/constellation paint transfer. Scale visual size, trail, bloom, embers and grandeur with earned receipts, with independent authoring and live tuning hooks; tuning never changes gameplay damage or the four-receipt gate. Preserve pause, reduced motion, quality budgets, scene cleanup and the existing renderer. Details: docs/design/fireball-shader-effect.md.

- Fireball constellation paint transfer (Eric, 2026-11-16): selecting a foundation replaces the powered Fireball card’s painted fill with a traced stroke. When an accepted receipt leaves the current tableau without another legal card receipt, wait for earned starlight to settle, etch the ability’s sketch behind its stars using the shared 40-frame ink-spread mask, then paint in its original artwork. Move that completed artwork from the sky into the Fireball card, etching then painting the card. Keep the source painting unchanged, pause the sequence with gameplay, snap under reduced motion, and preserve receipt power, the four-receipt cast gate and manual stock/casting. A new foundation begins another hollow card; this is a Fireball art treatment, with the other dev spell symbols unchanged.

- Stardust cornerstone reveal (Eric, 2026-11-16): show all four authored cornerstones subdued immediately after foundation selection. The first accepted receipt ignites its star immediately. Subsequent earned stars ignite after bright sparkling celestial lines travel from established lit stars, etching the shape in stardust; use radiant star cores, halos and ignition flourishes. Accelerate with faster receipt cadence and pending receipts so visuals catch up without blocking gameplay. Preserve receipt/power rules, pause, reduced motion, and independent later motifs. This supersedes hiding unlit cornerstone points; future/unearned connections remain absent.

- Hand drag visibility (Eric, 2026-11-16): while dragging an ability from the hand to the star grid, hide the rest of the hand and render the dragged card at 25% of its original dimensions with partial transparency. Clearly distinguish only the valid card currently targeted to become the foundation. Restore the hand and clear targeting on cancellation; preserve pointer capture and existing cast targeting.

- Spell foundation placement (Eric, 2026-11-16): grabbing or arming a hand ability shimmers the starfield and reveals the full card field. Drop the ability on a valid foundation (or select that cell with tap/keyboard) to start the shared puzzle on that exact card. Preview and cancellation award no receipts or power. An accepted drop sends a radial ripple from its actual position, jostling and shimmering stars/cards; respect pause and reduced motion. Preserve the four-receipt cast gate, actor-free charging and target silhouettes. This supersedes constellation preview/drop targeting.

- Spell placement and silhouette targets (Eric, 2026-11-16): remove the visible “Drop an ability into the stars” oval/prompt. Grabbing a hand card reveals that ability's four-cornerstone star shape; dragging/releasing over the highlighted constellation activates the shared tableau. No active receipt stars or power are awarded by previewing. Cancellation clears the preview; keyboard/tap placement retains an invisible accessible constellation target. Actual actor targeting must use a thin red hostile / blue support stroke of the live geometry or painted alpha silhouette, including on hover/focus/drag. HTML hit regions stay fully transparent and never draw an opaque fill, ellipse or bounding-box outline. Preserve the four-receipt cast gate and actor-free charging.

- Cornerstone constellation charging (Eric, 2026-11-16; Woods spell dev slice): drag an ability from the hand onto the starfield to reveal its constellation; keep combatants out of the hand/charging cosmos. The first four accepted receipts light four authored cornerstone stars. Fireball uses the annotated leading tip, far tail, upper shoulder and lower bowl, in that order. Require all four receipts before casting. Each visible prefix is a complete simpler composition: single tip, tip-to-tail axis, shoulder triangle, then complete four-star base. Additional receipts enrich that established shape with independent closed local motifs, interior stars and clusters; never require a long sequential outline or draw connections to future/unlit nodes. Give every ability its own four-cornerstone layout. Choose target returns smoothly to the actual Woods actors for a charged-card drag/cast; Back to stars resumes the same charge/tableau. Preserve +1 power per receipt, manual casting, shared stellar rules and Ctrl tap/hold. This supersedes zero-receipt casting, actor-drop charging, combatants in the starfield, and the earlier twenty-point perimeter scaffold.

- Woods astral spell thin slice (Eric, 2026-11-16; temporary dev test): in `portals.html?combat`, a centered bottom card-fan button opens Fireball, Blizzard, Bite and Star using the established ability-hand fan. Fade Woods/portal scenery into a vast cosmos while preserving Hero and Khadroot as target actors. Hostile drags highlight Khadroot red; support drags highlight Hero blue. Dragging an ability onto its eligible actor begins a smooth upward sky flight and opens the shared stellar tableau, including best-card shimmer, fully opaque foundation, stock reselect and Ctrl tap-one/hold-continuous behavior. Accepted receipts add +1 spell power without chip damage or automatic casts. Fireball starts at 1 fire damage; illuminate its leading tip first, far tail second, then distinctive silhouette landmarks. At any progress, including zero receipts, drag the charged ability directly onto its eligible actor to cast. Return restores exploration and the prior camera. This reintroduces only explicit spell testing; the legacy encounter, OTT toggle, world grid, Navi, AP and enemy turns remain disabled. Blizzard slows with frost damage, Bite deals physical damage and Star restores Hero's health as simple test siblings; these are scaffolding, not production balance.

- Khadroot scenery prop (Eric, 2026-11-16): retain a decorative, idly animated Khadroot at its former Woods encounter position (x=3.3, z=-7.3, original 2.5 m scale). Its presence must not arm encounters, combat awareness, attacks, HP panels or combat controls. This supersedes hiding the Khadroot entirely in exploration-only Woods.

- Terrestrial exploration-only reset (Eric, 2026-11-16): `portals.html?combat` now presents Woods exploration without enemy, battle HUD, tableau/foundations, Navi guidance, familiars or combat action controls. Disable encounters and world-card interaction. Remove the O/OTT toggle and the post-fly-in OTT handoff; retain the low arrival camera and normal exploration camera controls. Stellar tableau iteration continues independently under `?stellarcombat`.

- Stellar stock reselect (Eric, 2026-11-16): clicking stock settles the current star chain, consumes one stock card, and reopens the full current field for a fresh foundation selection. Place the drawn value in the old foundation cell; preserve the remaining ranks, stock and refill progress. Shimmer the best new starting location and disable auto-play until a foundation is chosen. Start the next star chain on that choice, without creating a phantom stock star or ending the night merely because the old folded tableau is blocked. Stellar uses dense refill queues to reconstruct the full field consistently; standalone 3xgrid defaults remain unchanged.

- Stellar tableau exploration (Eric, 2026-11-16): iterate on `portals.html?stellarcombat` as a noncombat test of tableau work in the stars. Replace fairy guidance with a sparkling shimmer on the strongest starting foundation, then the best next legal sequence card, using the shared solver. The current foundation face must be fully opaque over its collected-card stack. Remove the Eclipse opponent, HP/strike UI, receipt damage, attacks and damage effects/sounds while preserving star travel, chains, constellation creation and musical progression. Ctrl tap plays exactly one best card; holding Ctrl continuously replans until release/exhaustion, without stock draws or turn advance. Preserve manual stock play, quality controls, reduced motion and restart. These changes apply to stellarcombat; the stopped terrestrial camera work remains untouched.

- Single-step Ctrl and card hierarchy (Eric, 2026-11-16): a short Ctrl tap plays exactly one best legal card, even when a longer sequence exists. Holding Ctrl continues best moves until release/exhaustion, with no stock draws or turn advance. The auto button follows the same semantics. Remove corner numbers from the main nine side-grid cards; retain one large cream center value on a nearly opaque dark plaque. Keep backfill values small and differentiate playable-card borders from unavailable ones, reserving Navi's gold glow for her recommendation. Preserve the watercolor scenery wash. This supersedes tap-to-complete-a-sequence.

- Glass tableau UX (Eric, 2026-11-16): use the same scenery-window watercolor-glass treatment as Proto field cards for side-grid faces/backfill. Show the live world through each card with subtle pigment wash and high-contrast cream ranks on dark plaques. Scenery windows must exclude other UI card ink so overlapping backfill stays readable; retain the frameless layout and existing interactions.

- Ctrl sequence/hold behavior (Eric, 2026-11-16): tapping either Ctrl plays one planned best known sequence, paced through canonical receipts. Holding Ctrl continuously replans best moves until the last held Ctrl is released or no legal tableau move remains. Exhaustion stays stopped until a fresh press; never draw stock or end the turn automatically. Keep pointer auto-play equivalent, and preserve pause/blur/scene/manual-action cancellation. This supersedes continuous autoplay across turns/stock draws.

- Tableau-only play presentation (Eric, 2026-11-16): while the side tableau is in use, hide and disable all diegetic card surfaces, including Hero's foundation tile. Move restores the floor for foundation selection. Remove the Draw stock UI button; preserve the existing model/stock state. This supersedes retaining the visible world foundation and stock control during side-frame play.

- Quick foundation confirmation (Eric, 2026-11-16): mouse double-click or touch double-tap on the same valid floor candidate confirms placement directly, including during Move. A single click/tap retains the preview and check/X controls. Require two deliberate taps within 500 ms; drags, pinches, cancellation, focus loss, pause and camera takeover break the pair. Keep normal receipt clicks unchanged.

- Combat Woods receipt attacks disabled (Eric, 2026-11-16; temporary): tableau receipts still change rank, collect cards and earn capped AP/Momentum, but do not trigger chip strikes, stagger or receipt-triggered follow-up attacks. Keep explicit abilities, Move/end-turn behavior and other Proto scenarios unchanged. This supersedes the inherited receipt chip-hit behavior for the live world grid.

- Frameless side tableau (Eric, 2026-11-16): remove the dark green surround, outer border and panel shadow from the combat grid UX. Keep the cards, backfill, stock control and existing placement/spacing.

- Side-frame combat tableau (Eric, 2026-11-16): after Hero confirms a foundation and lands, extract the live 3×3 tableau and one directional backfill layer into an interactive felt UX frame to the side of the cinematic world view. Keep Hero's foundation tile in the world; play neighboring cards and stock through the same model/canonical receipts from the frame. Navi follows recommended UI cards while the frame is active and returns to Hero when none remain. Move dismisses the frame, smoothly returns to OTT, unfolds the preserved floor and permits confirmed selection/jump to a new foundation, then extracts that tableau again. Preserve cards, alternating refill, AP/turn rules, familiar controls and world/camera state. Keep the frame readable, scrollable on constrained screens, and animate extraction/dismissal with reduced-motion support. This supersedes world-only card selection during folded play.

- Navi sticker dev test (Eric, 2026-11-16; temporary scaffolding): add a checked-by-default Navi checkbox immediately left of Move. Instantiate the existing non-Proto Navi orb/four-wing appearance in world space. Before foundation selection (including Move), flutter around the pickable foundation with the strongest immediate known sequence. After confirmation, continuously guide to the next card in that sequence; when no legal tableau receipt remains, return to Hero's foundation. Share the grid's bounded known-card solver with auto-play, including A/K wrap and alternating corner backfill, without consuming cards or predicting random refill. Keep the guide optional, scene-scoped, paused with gameplay and respectful of reduced motion. This is a dev toggle, not a granted/purchased production sticker ability.

- Foundation landing cinematic (Eric, 2026-11-16): confirming an initial or Move foundation flies the camera out of OTT to a centered rear view of Hero facing the enemy party in the distance. Drive that flight from the actual Hero jump clock and duration so both movements finish together, then hold the landed composition during folding/play. Preserve smooth camera takeover, O/Move tactical access, pause and reduced motion; previewing a candidate must not start either movement.

- Combat Woods tactical selection polish (Eric, 2026-11-15): the return portal card clears the view after passing into Woods. Fly smoothly between the arrival/rear camera and OTT, including the opening handoff and Move; reduced motion snaps. Mouse hover previews the prospective foundation, eight neighbors and directional backfill using the source grid roles, dimming unused floor cards. Tapping a valid candidate pins that preview and shows connected check/X controls to its left/right; only check commits Hero's jump and any Move turn cost. X dismisses the candidate. Keep hover, touch, cancellation and manual camera takeover safe.

- Combat Woods opening tactical view (Eric, 2026-11-15): after the opening fly-in finishes, switch to the Hero-centered OTT camera for the first foundation selection. Apply the same handoff to skipped/replayed arrivals and reduced-motion entry. Retain the low rear arrival composition during the fly-in and as the view restored with O; subsequent manual camera input remains available. This supersedes holding the low rear view after arrival.

- Folding 3×3 and Move trial (Eric, 2026-11-15): replace free-roaming drawn paths in Combat Woods with the shared `3xgrid.html` mechanics. Keep the existing floor grid, coordinates and card state for selection. After Hero jumps and lands, animate the floor cards into the central foundation plus eight neighbors, with one visible top/bottom or left/right backfill strip for each applicable direction; deeper cards remain hidden and corner refill alternates vertical/side as in the source. Other floor cards vanish as folding completes. Use animated travel, flips and fade-outs for folding in, and reverse them when unfolding. Add Move immediately left of auto-play. Move unfolds the current field in OTT; choosing a different valid interior card sends Hero there immediately, establishes its existing value as the new foundation and ends the player turn without AP/chip receipt rewards. Preserve values, stock and refill progress instead of redealing. Hero stays at the central foundation during ordinary receipts. Escape cancels pending Move without spending a turn; pause, leaving the scene and focus loss clean up selection. Retain reduced motion, fixed world coordinates, terrain/depth and the source standalone defaults. This supersedes the drawn-path trial and permanent full-floor visibility during folded play.

- Drawn world-tableau paths and Hero alignment (Eric, 2026-11-15): Hero stands on the chosen tile's centerline, just above its middle printed value in OTT, including the initial K8 spawn. Use the registered tile stand point rather than an arbitrary position within the tile. Mouse/finger drag previews a glowing, terrain-fitted path through edge- or corner-adjacent cards of neighboring rank, with A/K wrapping. Paths can cross the whole floor; the foundation follows the path rather than staying confined to the original eight neighbors. Tracing backward erases the last step; do not revisit other path tiles. Preview awards nothing. Release queues paced, revalidated canonical receipts and actual Hero jumps; refill each vacated floor tile so coverage remains complete. Cancel unfinished input/queued steps on pointer cancellation, outside release, Escape, focus loss, pause, leaving the scene, enemy turn or battle completion; preserve already committed receipts. Keep alternate-button camera controls and multi-touch handoff. This is a world-only trial; standalone 3xgrid defaults remain unchanged.

- Combat Woods coordinates and camera presets (Eric, 2026-11-15): use fixed floor coordinates A–R by 1–14, with column letters along the grid's right axis and increasing rows along its down axis. Small printed coordinates and diagnostics identify floor tiles independently of changing card IDs/ranks; use `Woods:K8` when a scene-qualified identifier is needed. Start Hero just outside the return portal at the registered centerline stand point on K8, facing the enemy, with the supplied low rear camera composition as the default. Place the camera to keep the nearby portal outside the frame. O toggles a true vertical view centered above the live Hero at the reference height 17.594658803912374 m and FOV 60, retaining the reference's horizontal screen-up direction. O again restores the prior view relative to Hero; overhead follows movement/jumps and permits zoom. Ignore repeats, modified shortcuts, text editing, paused UI and other scenes. Retain manual camera takeover, replay/direct-entry consistency, and actual floor cards. This supersedes the prior full-clearing opening overview.

- Clearing-wide card floor (Eric, 2026-11-15): densely tile the entire Combat Woods floor within the innermost tree ring. Cards are 2 m wide at their established aspect ratio, with narrow .012 m seams and perimeter geometry clipped to the actual ring boundary. This supersedes the initial 33% reduction and .4 m spacing. Keep the floor fully tiled after choosing a foundation and after receipts: eight neighboring tiles remain playable while directional backfill shifts through its existing floor cells and replenishes the far end. Preserve the shared standalone 3xgrid defaults, actual Hero landing, terrain conformity, camera-independent world positions, and existing foundation panels/UX buttons. Batch the carpet geometry and frame the full clearing on entry.

- Combat Woods diegetic grid (Eric, 2026-11-15, clarified): the shared `3xgrid.html` cards are world-space surfaces mapped to the actual floor topography, never a screen-space UX grid. Reduce card dimensions by 33%, retain screen-space foundation panels and combat UX buttons, and choose an interior starting foundation. Keep that selected card's world position as its eight neighbors and alternating directional stacks form the playable grid; retain A/K wrapping, stock draws and default infinite refill. The actual world Hero jump-flips to the selected floor card and stands there at her normal physical scale. Keep adjacent printed ranks readable through precise rank-surface peek-through when her sprite overlaps them, preserving scenery depth. Use a floor-readable overview on entry instead of the former near-level shoulder shot; preserve manual orbit/zoom. Camera and viewport changes must not relocate cards. Share the source engine. Starting selection/stock establish rank; legal receipts retain canonical AP/hit effects. This supersedes the earlier screen-space grid implementation and prior tableau-removal trial.

- Combat Woods presentation (Eric, 2026-11-15): remove the visible tableau cards and turn/instruction text from the `?combat` scene. This supersedes the earlier visible screen-space tableau rail requirement.

Keep pause, editing fields, leaving combat and focus-loss input cleanup safe; releasing a hold stops further automatic plays. Familiar descriptions and actions remain in normal document flow, readable without overlap or horizontal scrolling at narrow/short viewports.

- Direct controller holocard entry (Eric, 2026-11-13): deliberately walking, crawling or jumping into a portal card with the left stick enters its paired world, from either face and throughout its usable vertical aperture, without requiring Y or a click. Only motion toward the nearby actual aperture arms that exact card. Ordinary point-and-click routes still avoid unselected membranes; proximity, sideways movement, moving away and other floors never trigger travel. Keep Y/click approach routes and paired return cards.

- Portals acrobatics and crawling (Eric, 2026-11-13): reverse a running direction and jump during the short pivot window for the Mario-style side flip, including after the initial skid slows. Crouch + jump (LT + B, or C + Space) launches a high backward somersault with a modest backward drift; keeping crouch held must not immediately ground pound. Release and press crouch again to request an aerial pound. Grounded crouch movement uses authored low, bent-limb crawl poses at a uniform physical scale across low/high/rear/overhead views; never squash ordinary walking sprites. Explicit traversal poses take priority over combat awareness.

- Controller sprint/jump swap (Eric, 2026-11-13): B jumps (hold for jump height); hold A while moving for immediate manual sprint, releasing A returns to ordinary analog walk/run. X retains airborne dive. A confirms and B goes back in paused menus. Keep LB zoom, right-stick camera and keyboard/route sprint rules. This supersedes the earlier controller A-jump mapping.

Share swept, axis-sliding movement clearance with navigation; preserve solid wood, placed props, water, slopes and deliberately unselected portal membranes.

- Controller rear framing (Eric, 2026-11-13): meaningful controller use adopts the supplied close rear view: a centered boom directly behind Hero, about 4.5 m away, with a gentle .13 rad downward pitch and the existing 60° lens. Merely connecting a controller or stick drift must not move the camera. Adopt the rendered camera before the change, retain terrain clearance, and let right-stick orbit and LB zoom adjust the view without resetting it every frame. Reacquire the preset when returning from mouse camera use; preserve an explicitly selected free camera and paused Settings behavior.

- Controller zoom modifier (Eric, 2026-11-13): while exploring with a standard controller, hold LB and use left-stick Y for slow analog zoom (up in, down out) in follow and free camera. Her default yaw follows Hero, with her normal slow spin, curious roll and deliberate playful upside-down interlude. Require transparent margins around the full ears/paws in every packed exposure. See docs/art/hero-world-refresh-v3.md. The game pauses only by explicit Pause/Resume controls, Escape or controller Start/Menu; window blur, tab changes and controller connection changes must not pause it automatically. Input cleanup on focus loss remains separate from the world clock.

Never enter sitting, prone, yawning or rolling idles in threat range. A pre-lunge bow lowers her chest/forelegs while hind legs stay planted and her rump stays high; it does not trigger an attack. Use threat-distance hysteresis and restore relaxed exploration only after leaving range or the enemy is defeated. Warm the four combat atlases before the first world frame and retain view/physical-scale calibration. Combat Woods arrival now lands on the supplied closer, lower rear shoulder composition: Hero foreground, Khadroot ahead, near-level aim. This supersedes the initial high overview while keeping the portal flight and held handoff.

Fly the camera through the actual grove-side Combat Woods card using the paired portal transform, then settle above/behind Hero with both actors framed. Match the subject's current atlas crop, mirror and actual plane bounds; normal depth/fog still occludes these effects behind scenery. Do not add bloom, lights or capture passes. Calibrate each exploration clip/view against the existing Hero neutral footprint, using one uniform scale across all four action frames; never size a new actor by its sprite-cell dimensions or independently fit every exposure. Extended climbing postures use the normal body scale rather than being shrunk to standing height.

Keep five mirrored yaw views at both low/high camera elevations plus world-aligned overhead. Random new idles yield immediately to movement; reduced motion suppresses them. Climbing requires an explicitly registered supported route, never arbitrary wall phasing or an invented landing. Move all former config options and World Studio into Settings, with Display, Controller, Paint, Ground, Objects and Camera tabs switched by LB/RB. Support controller focus, activation, numeric/range/select/checkbox adjustments and name editing while retaining mouse/keyboard. World editing remains grove-only; preserve validated disk saves, drafts and undo. Ground attacks and extra Mario moves are not implemented by this input mapping.

Proximity or an ordinary route passing a card must never teleport Hero. Page 1111 adds a fixed Combat Woods card at the supplied Hero ground coordinates (-0.0963792005082889,75.95259342773834), rotated 180 degrees from its initial placement (yaw π−0.755) to face the game-start camera, with a paired return card. The woods-side exit is rotated 180 degrees (yaw π), so Hero emerges toward decreasing Z into the clearing, safely outside Khadroot’s contact range. Reuse the actual painted combat/tableau woods and existing Khadroot rig. Arrival only explores: no battle state, combat clock, attack or tableau is created. A grounded player-driven approach into Khadroot’s 2.2 m contact range opens the real Khadroot encounter, carrying Flurry. Retreat keeps the session encounter state and requires leaving contact range before rearming; victory removes the enemy. Keep portal return travel available before engagement and World Studio editing limited to the grove.

Fill the former masonry footprint with actual painted, supported ground. Double the clearing’s first outgoing ramp width from 1.725 m to 3.45 m about its center, preserving grade/elevation and updating the same geometry used by navigation. Treat walkable ramp contact, including its crest, as floor support while retaining walls, undersides, clearance and per-step height checks. This explicit interlude alone may invert her; reduced motion disables it and holds her neutral. Preserve click-to-approach, local storm and persistent rewards. Around the dark slime, mix irregular black/burgundy pigments into the actual ground and nearby grass, with localized dark red/black rising miasma and sparse red flecks. Keep all effects depth/fog occluded, GPU-batched, quality bounded and still under reduced motion. Preserve pages 1110 and 120 independently.

Reduced motion holds a fixed orientation. Keep sticker placement/inspection cards readable and their existing interaction model intact.

A shared weighted random rig drives world and loose-sticker faces with varied holds and blink timing, including occasional double blinks. Blink only the eye region so the current mouth/blush remains stable. Applied sticker prints stay neutral; reduced motion holds a neutral face. Use one runtime atlas rather than separate GPU textures per expression, preserve the authored ice body, and retain normal alpha-aware world picking.
