Use smooth randomized held glances, gentle tilts and a small face-layer offset to express curiosity and interest. Surround her with a localized softly luminous snowfall column rising about 26 m, so it can act as a discovery landmark from farther away. Keep snowflake glow soft, terrain/depth/fog occluded, with bounded GPU batches (384 Low/768 otherwise), no added lights/bloom/capture passes or per-frame particle-buffer uploads. Keep the sticker itself in normal alpha-aware prop picking; snowfall must not intercept navigation clicks. Reduced motion retains a filled still storm and neutral face; existing click-to-approach, collection and duplicate-free inspection remain intact.

- Point-to-click sticker approach (Eric, 2026-11-11): clicking a world sticker such as Flurry uses the ordinary prop picking and safe navigation path. If Hero is outside interaction reach, walk her to reachable supported ground beside the sticker and automatically collect/inspect upon grounded entry into reach. Nearby clicks interact immediately. Replaced, cancelled or blocked routes must not retain a queued pickup. Keep sticker inventory persistent and duplicate-free, drag gestures intact, and preserve normal terrain/obstacle navigation and camera behavior.

- Persistent Flurry world presence (Eric, 2026-11-11): keep page 1111’s authored floating Flurry visible after collection, including for existing saved inventories and after authoring moves. Nearby clicks collect only when no reward exists; subsequent clicks inspect/reopen the existing reward without resetting an attachment or granting another sticker. The inventory button remains available independently of world proximity.

- Page 1111 exact arrival and Flurry reposition (Eric, 2026-11-11): use the supplied follow-camera telemetry for normal arrival/replay/direct entry: Hero (4,74), yaw 1.026, pitch .426, boom 12.5 m, FOV 60; its camera position is (14.136102932819126,9.162338405286881,79.23710183546828). Keep the cleared approach and settled handoff. Move Flurry independently of the slime so her floating center aligns with the new screenshot crosshairs, using the supplied camera projection and existing 2.5 m hover height. Flurry reward modals must render without visible scrollbars; prevent expanded decorative snowfall from creating layout overflow, fit the intro controls within short viewports, and retain scroll/touch/keyboard access to longer effect lists.

- Flurry world pickup and unique vertical enhancements (Eric, 2026-11-11): float the authored Flurry sticker above page 1111’s marked encounter patch. Collect her by clicking while Hero is within 6 m; open the sticker reward modal, retain unplaced rewards for later inspection, and persist progression separately from World Studio authoring. The collected reward follows Hero into musical combat. Divide the sticker portrait into equal vertical bands, one per supported enhancement in the displayed effect-row order. The sticker center selects exactly one band even when its silhouette spans a boundary; highlight only that band and its matching ability row. Confirmation persists and activates only that enhancement. Preserve ten-percent front coverage, edge wrapping, pointer/keyboard placement and confirmation/cheer. Older bundled attachments remain compatible; newly selected enhancements survive party continuation and scenario links without remapping as abilities change. This supersedes the earlier “all effects apply together” placement rule for new attachments.

- Page 1111 ground encounter and camera inspection (Eric, 2026-11-11): add an idle authored watercolor slime at the screenshot crosshairs and lower/reposition the initial approach to that ground patch. Arrival settings in world1111Layout.ts now use the supplied exact camera telemetry; Flurry has an independent screenshot-projected position. Preserve cleared flight and follow handoff. Camera telemetry opens a selectable JSON flyout with refresh and optional clipboard copy; blocked clipboard access must leave the text available for manual copying. Add Darker Autumn as a selectable, saved darker tint of Autumn Wood across mixed paint and grass; it changes pigments rather than restoring scene darkness.

- Direct-arrival World Studio copy (Eric, 2026-11-11): page 1111 copies page 1110’s painted daylight grove and complete tooling with independent disk settings and browser drafts. Begin its fly-in at the exact world-camera pose exposed after Believe’s i-dot has been passed (1.2 seconds into the cleared world approach); omit the exterior book/opening/portal sequence entirely. Replay uses the same starting pose, Skip follows the same cleared route, and arrival retains the shared follow-camera handoff. Page 1110 keeps its existing opening.

- Bright localized atmosphere and smoke option (Eric, 2026-11-11): disable page 1110's night/darkness treatment, vignette, blanket player-fog darkening and whole-sky/whole-tree height veil. Use neutral daylight, terrain-following pale ground mist, and localized fog at perimeter trees and the waterfall; leave the central tree readable at every height. Add a saved World Studio Ground option for smoke wisps, with density, rise and swirl controls, based on https://patch.gg/patch/particle-physics. Its WebGPU-only smoke is adapted to bounded WebGL buoyant/cooling/swirl tracers, not installed as an SPH simulation. Preserve depth occlusion, reduced-motion stills, quality caps and page 120's independent atmosphere. Older recipes without smoke settings remain valid.

- Complete mixed-paint surfaces (Eric, 2026-11-11): at stroke coverage 1, page 1110's mixed-paint method covers the entire rendered ground topography, including slopes, walls, rims and ground outside walking/planting areas. Use continuous world-anchored, overlapping normalized brush mixtures on the actual surface; random detail-stroke counts and Low-quality budgets must never expose unpainted holes at full coverage. Apply the same surface method to Yggdrasil's trunk/branches/roots in brown shades and its main foliage in greens, overriding the earlier gold/russet canopy choice. Retain Autumn Wood ground/litter styling, existing water, mist, non-emissive tree surfaces and luminous climbers/fruit.

- Autumn Wood mixed-paint configuration (Eric, 2026-11-11): page 1110's mixed/pinned-paint method defaults to Autumn Wood instead of the cool Aurora-like grove look. Use the supplied Oil Paint autumn reference's gold/russet foliage, warm oak bark, ochre leaf litter, violet-brown shadows and muted straw/olive ground cover. Keep saved settings, reset defaults and the palette selector consistent. Preserve page 120's independent treatment, existing water/mist, light effects and non-emissive bark/canopy.

- World Paint Studio copy (Eric, 2026-11-10): `book.html?page=1110` copies page 120's World Tree exploration, cleared fly-in and controls as an independent paint/tooling experiment. Adapt https://patch.gg/blendi/oil-paint for surface-pinned directional marks using art.md's watercolor palette/edges, and https://patch.gg/patch/soil-and-grass for instanced curled grass and paintable growth/wetness. Keep page 120's presentation independent. Provide live paint/ground controls, dry-ground tree/rock placement, transform handles and numeric editing, undo/redo, camera shot capture/revisit, validated import/export, draft recovery and explicit revision-checked disk persistence in `src/data/worldStudio1110.json`. Bind grass to the rendered terrain triangles and include authored props in navigation/arrival clearance; never save Hero or session progression as authoring. Preserve water, fog, non-emissive bark/canopy, luminous climbers/fruit, reduced motion and bounded rendering. Sources and licenses: `docs/research/world-studio-1110.md`.

- World Tree flight clearance and waterfall visibility (Eric, 2026-11-10): page 120's initial approach follows a precomputed terrain- and obstacle-cleared route using the rendered painted-tree footprints, modeled limbs/crowns/fruit and rock bounds. Clear the camera's near-plane footprint, not just its center; test complete segments and rounded corners, avoid unchecked spline cuts, and preserve the exact shared follow endpoint. Normal flight and accelerated Skip use the same route. Falling water and waterfall wisps must use the scene's fog density/depth transmission so they cannot appear ahead of adjacent geography; retain the upper altitude veil and depth occlusion.

- Natural World Tree with luminous climbers (Eric, 2026-11-10): remove all bioluminescent growth shading from page 120's bark and main canopy. Keep their natural watercolor paint. Weave separate ivy/vines along the modeled trunk, roots and branches; only these climbers may bioluminesce. Greatly increase floating wispy lights, including the lower grove. Fruit emits broken multicolored spectral edge particles that peel away from its silhouette, using the supplied outlined-shape reference as effect direction. Preserve bounded shared batches, frozen reduced-motion behavior, mist/depth occlusion and existing navigation. This supersedes the tree-wide luminous bark/canopy treatment.

- Waterfall particle flow (Eric, 2026-11-10): use the supplied gravity-driven canvas emitter method for page 120's two waterfall drops: varied round-capped downward strokes, fading trails and impact bubbles. Adapt it to the river's shared watercolor pigment palette, broad washes, feathered edges and pooled boundaries under art.md. Bound particle/bubble pools, texture resolution and update cadence; use the game clock, retain a filled static reduced-motion view, terrain/mist occlusion and the existing water-sprite lighting. The supplied container CSS is reference-demo scaffolding, not a page overlay.

- Dark painted grove (Eric, 2026-11-10): page 120 uses shorter distance-fog visibility and darker ambient light, soil and mist so luminous fruit and river lights stand out. Replace surrounding volumetric trees with the existing painted watercolor tree assets in static shared batches; preserve the central modeled Yggdrasil, its fourfold scale, vines and fruit. Retain terrain-following dynamic ground mist and the dense upper waterfall veil.

- Ubiquitous drifting basin mist (Eric, 2026-11-10): spread ground mist throughout page 120, following the floor and terraces rather than clustering only near the waterfall. Slowly moving coherent density fields form thicker pockets and thinner gaps across broad overlapping banks. Keep some mist near Hero with a gentler clearing, retain the dense upper bank and static reduced-motion form, and animate on the GPU without per-frame instance uploads or extra capture passes.

- Visible basin and upper waterfall mist (Eric, 2026-11-10): page 120 retains readable low drifting mist in the basin and a dense upper bank. The upper 20% of the 48 m waterfall (38.4 m upward), source terrain and everything above it should be almost fully obscured. Start the transition at 30 m. Apply the height veil to ordinary materials, custom waterfall shaders and luminous particles, and haze the empty sky too; do not rely solely on distance fog or a ground-height profile that vanishes at the crest. Keep the area near Hero readable and honor reduced motion.

- World Tree ground-cover treatment (Eric, 2026-11-10): page 120 ground clutter uses proto.html tableau/combat's actual woodland radial-dab paint, feathered pigment islands, painted leaf litter and crossed upright grass atlas. Replace solid polygon clovers and outlined bank-map soil with those shared materials. Fit islands to terrain slopes, exclude water/steep walls, and instance the cover in bounded quality-dependent batches. Preserve soft alpha edges and open walking areas; do not substitute faceted leaf polygons for painted ground cover.

- World Tree basin redesign (Eric, 2026-11-10): replace page 120's isolated extracted cascade with a reference-led two-stage waterfall, elevated source pool, plunge ledges, rock columns and mossy shelves. Enclose the lower scene in an irregular roughly 220×212 m basin, using the supplied castle GLB footprint proportions at the existing waterfall scale. Move the fourfold world tree rearward into the back terrain; keep the lower approach traversable, river downstream motion, fog and camera controls. Side terrain must have modeled depth, not flat panels. This supersedes the earlier requirement to use the extracted GLB waterfall itself on page 120; retain its archive and provenance.

- Original waterfall and camera hotkey (Eric, 2026-11-10): reuse the original castle inlet's two-stage waterfall mesh on page 120, retaining its authored topology and UVs when repositioned. The supplied castle_waterfall.glb is archived under sources/book/castle-waterfall; extract its Shape_258 cascade with original indices/UVs and exclude Shape_259 (the moat). In play mode, L toggles free camera once per keypress. Free-camera keyboard movement is twice its previous base speed, or four times that base while Shift is held. Ignore camera hotkeys while typing or using modal/puzzle controls.

- World Tree river/paint revision (Eric, 2026-11-10): Page 120 shares the chapter/page detail panels and full configuration including camera telemetry. Permit a 0.7 m close camera boom and steeper upward viewing. Scale only the central world tree by four; vary luminous fruit colors, strengthen fog, and remove inherited castle lamp shading. Apply the established authored watercolor wash textures to wood, foliage, clover and soil. A curving river comes from a visible streaming waterfall on the left, hugs the rear roots closely enough to submerge some, and flows out to the right; pigment motion follows downstream river distance. Preserve dry ground in front of Hero and the tree.

- Book world routes and World Tree (Eric, 2026-11-10): castle scenes use book.html?page=117, 118 and 119; legacy numbered documents redirect while preserving query parameters. Page 120 is a separate natural reinterpretation with a huge rooted, spreading world tree, hanging vines, glowing berries and ethereal river lights included in water reflections. Its river flows beside the tree from a waterfall rather than surrounding it. Remove castle structures, bridge puzzle, bridge boards, card packs and castle portals from page 120. Expand surrounding trees and ground vegetation using shared instanced batches; retain Hero, point-to-move and close camera controls.

- Intimate travel camera and floor guides (Eric, 2026-11-09): Page 119 defaults to a close 7.5 m camera boom. Ordinary point-to-move journeys retain the player's current boom, yaw and lens and follow Hero closely without automatic landmark pullbacks; preserve manual camera control and the explicitly authored bridge-crossing shot. Quest sparkle guides belong to actual supported ground/floor geometry, following slopes and stopping at voids/water/vertical discontinuities; never arc through the air or rise to floating packs.

- World quest sparkle guides and wood adornments (Eric, 2026-11-09): connect Hero to available quest objects using gently flowing violet/blue halos, warm star cores and sparkling motes; on page 119 guide each uncollected Wood Plank pack. Retire a guide when its pickup starts and hide guides during blueprint editing, arrivals and other zones. Use a bounded shared particle batch with depth occlusion, no new lights/bloom/reflection captures, and a static reduced-motion form. Wood cards and packs use newly authored wood-themed watercolor adornments based on nature-v3's asymmetric edge composition, retaining standard 63:88 card faces and clear rank/label areas.

- Castle book exterior and gold leaf (Eric, 2026-11-09): Page 119 begins outside the same leather-and-gilt book as page 5, with its perimeter fog and opening magic. Open the physical cover before approaching Believe on the inside page and entering its i-dot. Style Believe as mottled, worn gold leaf with metallic highlights; keep its depth stable throughout the flight rather than flickering against the paper.

- Page 119 portal arrival and bridge paint sparkles (Eric, 2026-11-09): begin the castle sequence by flying through the actual dot above the gold “i” in page 23's Believe lettering. Show the live castle inside the dot and continue directly through the existing castle approach to Hero, with no pop-up-book destination or intermediate camera stop. Spawn Hero outside the bridge sketch reveal radius. Bridge paint-in uses page 5's sampled-pigment sparkle renderer, synchronized to the same proximity paint field, with a bounded particle budget and reduced-motion fallback.

- Crater mist (Eric, 2026-11-09): surround the castle crater with soft mist banks; beyond the rim, make the fog taller and substantially denser so distant trees are barely visible. Reuse bounded world-space volume shading, keep Hero's nearby clearing and lantern scattering, and retain low-quality/reduced-motion budgets without extra lights, shadows or reflection passes.

- Bridge dismissal and triple-jump timing (Eric, 2026-11-09): a fresh click/tap outside the bridge blueprint, hand and puzzle controls leaves the puzzle through the same path as Leave, retaining partial progress. Releasing a dragged/flicked card outside does not dismiss. Hero's forward triple-jump flip starts earlier on ascent and resolves later on descent; preserve jump physics and other jump animations.

- Sticker inspection (Eric, 2026-11-07): inspect an attached sticker and its effects from the kin’s jumbo. Reserve combat buff icons for active effects. Flurry’s Ice Armor uses the standard Armor indicator when present.

- Jumbo dismissal (Eric, 2026-11-07): clicking or tapping empty space outside the jumbo card closes the current view. Card galleries, controls, and the sticker placement area beyond the portrait edge stay interactive.

- Hero’s musical ability gallery (Eric, 2026-11-07): omit Nip and Takedown in `117.html`. Keep Grit, Pack Leader, Bite, Vice Grip, Fetch and Dig in the gallery.

- Opening sticker reward (2026-11-07, playtest): `117.html?reward=snowflake` gives Flurry, a loose die-cut holofoil sticker. Flurry drifts toward pointer movement or taps inside the central 30% of the opening viewport. Dim the board behind the opening reward and reveal eligible kin foundations through the shade during a drag or keyboard target selection. Keep parallax and holofoil, disable her lenticular shading, and animate falling, fluttering flakes across a field with three times the original radius. Anchor the snowfall to the sticker’s resting position while she tilts, drifts, or is moved. Flake intensity builds to a 2× boost near the center and tapers outward. The fade starts at about 88% of the radius and reaches zero opacity at its edge. `?reward=<reward-id>` opens a specific existing reward at Common, and `?reward` opens the usual preview. Drag Flurry onto a living placed kin foundation to open a portrait-only Sticker Memory view. Flurry floats beside the jumbo. Hover for a placement outline and an easing Flurry follower, then click or tap to try a spot and confirm it when Flurry says “Let me help!”. Keep at least 10% of her sampled print on the front and fold overflow onto the inspectable reverse. Confirmed edge-wrapped stickers unfold on the front in jumbo inspection. The applied sticker sits on a parallax plane between the background and the kin artwork. The kin artwork also covers the placement outline and the moving sticker wherever they cross the portrait. Keyboard placement uses arrows and Enter. The Flurry jumbo anchors its effects panel on the left. Hover or focus an effect for its detailed rules. Sticker callouts sit to the right of the portrait. Confirming consumes the sticker, activates all listed effects together and saves the portrait position for later inspection. Flurry cheers “Wheee!” with a snowy letter wave and the jumbo stays open until the player closes it. The attachment and position follow the party across continued encounters and scenario links. The portrait draft grants 1 Ice Armor when the enemy turn opens, with a ceiling of 1 from the sticker. Grit adds 1 Chill on a damaging Grit proc, carried into the next enemy turn. Pack Leader’s draft chills the inherited target on a free promotion. Adept Bite adds 1 ice damage per AP spent. Fetch costs 2 AP and saves an exposed card for a later rank-free receipt. Enchanted Fetch and Dig give the retrieved card, its orthogonal neighbors and its new backfill Ice affinity. Enchanted Vice Grip adds 1 Frostbite per held enemy turn. Frostbite’s payoff is open.

- Face framing on cards and tableaus (Eric, 2026-11-07, standing rule): When featured artwork includes a face, make it the focal point of the visible composition. Set the crop and placement so the entire face stays clearly visible near the center, with breathing room around it. Check the rendered framing at each size and aspect ratio where the artwork appears.

- Musical tableaus and Adept Dig (Eric, 2026-11-07): the page is /117.html. During a player turn, drag Hero’s Dig expression onto any card immediately behind its column’s exposed card. During the drag, highlight eligible cards and lift the exact hovered card above the stack with a full-card outline and Dig target label. The lifted card remains a drop target across its face. Dig spends 1 AP to lift that card into a reserve beside the player foundations with an animated flight. This spends AP without earning a receipt. The card can be played once during any player turn in that encounter, bypassing rank adjacency and resolving normal golf receipt effects on the current lead foundation. Provide second-row targets in the Adept inspector for keyboard use.

- Hero Grit ability (Eric, 2026-11-07): include Grit in Hero's foundation ability gallery. It applies the existing 2-AP, exhausted-on-use buff to Hero, available independently of Adept's expression AP bands; preserve the existing offered keru targeting flow.

- Finite Frenzy golf (Eric, 2026-11-07): use ten tableaus of four face-up, overlapping cards, with only each column's exposed card playable. No infinifill or refills; cleared columns stay empty. The final card of any column deals 4 base damage instead of 1. Each receipt independently rolls a random living enemy target through stored seeded randomness, without changing the normal selected target. Present foundation and facedown stock piles with the remaining queued stock count above the stock. Retain the timed game, ±1/A–K rules, two stock/foundation cards per stack and a bonus stock every five receipts. End when all columns are cleared, the timer/stock runs out, enemies are defeated or the player finishes. This supersedes earlier infinite one-row Frenzy rules.

- Grit buff keru (Eric, 2026-11-06): Grit is available through the Momentum-revealed keru cards. Applying it costs the chosen kin 2 AP, exhausts the offered Grit, and adds a non-stacking buff. When attacked, the living buffed kin gains 1 AP up to its ceiling, once per distinct attacking enemy per turn; full-cap attacks still consume that enemy's trigger. Guarded/evaded attacks count, passive retaliation and DoT do not. Fresh combat resets the offer; continued encounters retain the buff and exhaustion. Four explicit draft rarities preserve this requested baseline. See docs/design/grit.md.

- Buff and AP row alignment (Eric, 2026-11-06): left-justify buffs to their owning kin's foundation edge, including smaller support foundations. AP wrapping is driven by maximum capacity and available width; only reserve overflow-row space if the rendered maximum-AP pips actually need additional rows. Keep full-capacity pips visible even at zero current AP.

- Adept AP ceiling (Eric, 2026-11-06): applying Adept sets maximum AP to exactly 12 without granting current AP. Preserve the current bank, clamping only an existing bank above 12. Fresh musical Hero starts at 1/12 with Dig. Casting spends current AP and receipts restore +1 up to 12. This supersedes the earlier bank-filling behavior, 1/13 default and additive capacity rules below.

- Adept expression artwork (Eric, 2026-11-06): Hero's enhanced foundation and expression inspector use Hero's holocard at 0 AP or when the current ability has no authored artwork. Use existing ability paintings for available expressions (currently Dig, Bite and Vice Grip). Frenzy and Avatar Howl retain their expression names while falling back to Hero's holocard until art is authored. Do not change AP thresholds or gameplay.

- Adept name and default (Eric, 2026-11-06): Adept is the enhancement keru's name throughout code, UI, tests and documentation. Fresh musical combat starts Hero enhanced by Common Adept: 1 current AP, 13 maximum AP, and the Dig expression. No extra kin/team slot is created. Continue to preserve the existing enhancement owner/tier on subsequent encounters; do not reattach a second copy. The available offer is hidden while Adept is attached.

- Kin and Keru terminology (Eric, 2026-11-06): creatures are kin; summons/entities are keru. Enhancement keru affect a target kin instead of occupying a foundation. Preserve legacy identifiers where renaming would risk unrelated systems; use the new distinction in new player-facing content.

- Adept enhancement first pass (Eric, 2026-11-06): Adept appears among Momentum-revealed available keru and enhances an existing target kin instead of adding a team slot. +12 maximum AP is capacity only; each accepted receipt grants exactly +1 AP. Enhanced supports have their own tableau value and can receive cards. Hero expressions spend the whole current AP bank: 1 Dig, 2–4 Bite (1 base damage/AP), 5–6 Vice Grip, 7/8/9 Frenzy I/II/III, 10/11/12 Avatar Howl (15/25/40 base damage). Frenzy provides two sequential stock/foundation cards per stack, ten infinitely refilling columns, one base damage per receipt and a bonus stock every five receipts. Use a deliberately generous timed first pass. Hero's existing free Bite is superseded while enhanced. Current draft attachment is free/non-stacking, other kin expressions await authoring, and Avatar Howl is a direct-hit stub. Confirmed capacity-only/all-current-AP spending; detailed draft choices and rarity clocks: docs/design/adept.md.

- Linear Momentum earnings (Eric, 2026-11-06): each accepted legal tableau move earns exactly 1 Momentum, regardless of elapsed time, rapid/sustained sequences, actor or Hearthflame binding. Invalid moves earn nothing. Remove the Hearthflame receipt multiplier and do not round away existing fractional balances when adding the unit gain. Existing explicit costs and configurable decay remain unchanged.

- Scenario share links, first pass (Eric, 2026-11-06): musicaltableaus has a Copy scenario link control that captures a versioned compressed battle snapshot in the URL, including actor/enemy stats and statuses, foundations, abilities, counters, Momentum, exact tableau/stock and stored random seeds. Opening restores the correct scenario paused, with an explicit Resume scenario action. Validate and bound imported snapshots; preserve ordinary starts on invalid links. Omit historical presentation events. This first pass captures battle simulation, not music/camera preferences or transient gestures. See docs/design/scenario-links.md.

- Rear tableau tops (Eric, 2026-11-06): retain two fully visible musical tableau rows. Suggest hidden rows three and four using only thin, non-interactive card-top rims behind the visible stack, without rank/suit disclosure or increasing the playable row budget. Show rims only for cards actually behind the visible rows; keep deeper cards and future row-budget scaffolding intact.

- Fast Swipes foundation tracker (Eric, 2026-11-06): replace the SUPPORT corner tag with live passive progress as accepted prime plays / rarity interval (for example 2/5). Keep it in that same corner, including after Mochi becomes prime, and retain the existing buff-tray counter.

- Musical summon hand (Eric, 2026-11-06): hide non-summoned Keru cards by default. Clicking the Momentum gauge toggles available summon cards; Mochi is the only offer for now and uses her Keru portrait card to the left of Bite. Clicking it opens Mochi's centered jumbo and side ability gallery. Fast Swipes costs 3 Momentum at every draft rarity, never Hero AP. Dragging Fast Swipes from inspection dismisses the jumbo and reveals an empty support foundation; a valid drop spends once and adds Mochi–Fast Swipes to the support team. Invalid/cancelled/unaffordable drops change neither team nor currency. Hide the offer after summoning; preserve support progress across encounters. Provide an equivalent keyboard-accessible summon action in inspection. This supersedes the earlier 1-AP recruitment hand and inert Momentum gauge rules.

- Fast Swipes approved pose replacement (Eric, 2026-11-06): use the v7 single Mochi painting based on the supplied upright, raised-forepaw pose reference in all Fast Swipes hand, foundation and jumbo views. Fit the full portrait silhouette over the existing separate background. Do not add lenticular extras, phantom limbs or animated frame exchanges to this painting for now. This supersedes the v6 sequence as active artwork; keep previous versions archived.

- Fast Swipes restrained cat slap (Eric, 2026-11-06): replace the frenetic alternating/many-fists presentation with one short anatomical right-forepaw slap (viewer-left shoulder). Keep the opposite forepaw down and generally immobile. Use four registered ImageGen poses and only three lenticular exchanges across the full signed lateral range, with no repeated cycles, phantom-arm planes, magic arcs or continuous limb interpolation. Preserve discrete angle selection, shared lens coating/toggle and static accessibility fallback. This supersedes v5's alternating six-pose multi-plane cadence. See docs/art/fast-swipes-lenticular-v6.md.

- Fast Swipes discrete exposures (Eric, 2026-11-06): alternating left/right swipes use six distinct registered ImageGen poses with changing X endpoints, wrist silhouettes and foreshortening. Three phantom depth planes select their own distinct six-pose sequences at staggered angular gates, producing many rapid swipes. Poses and fixed X placements jump at lens exchanges; never slide an unchanged paw or tween it along a trajectory. Fixed viewing angle freezes the action; reversing tilt retraces it. Preserve the shared global lens controls, DOM fallback and static reduced-motion/low-FX pose. This supersedes v4's four-pose sequence and sinusoidal afterimage translation. Full authoring contract: art.md; implementation: docs/art/fast-swipes-lenticular-v5.md.

- Pack Leader (Eric, 2026-11-06): Hero begins musical combat with a Pack Leader ability card and circular default buff, ready for one free leader promotion to any eligible living party member. The default recharge is three player-turn openings, configurable as n in Gear. Use the free charge before the ordinary 3-Momentum promotion cost; paid promotions during cooldown do not reset it. Expose free promotion targets in the ability inspector and retain value-ornament drag/keyboard promotion. Hero retains ownership while supporting; preserve charge/cooldown across continued encounters. Rarity drafts and tuning details are in docs/design/pack-leader.md.

- Jumbo center-stage revision (Eric, 2026-11-06): give the centered jumbo substantially more screen area. Arrange all other cards exclusively in left/right side fans; never place gallery cards above or below the main card. Preserve the crossing swap and reversible slot exchange. This supersedes the complete circular ring.

- Fast Swipes lenticular action (Eric, 2026-11-06): both prime/support Mochi foundations display the ability title Fast Swipes. Move the nearest extra swipe paw halfway toward her face. Use registered ImageGen ready/left/recoil/right paintings and separate afterimage planes for rapid horizontal swipes selected by lateral angle, repeating across a sweep like the shared Bite lens animation. Keep gameplay/progress, global lens flag and static accessibility fallbacks. Future ImageGen lenticular requests follow the full standing contract in art.md.

- Radial jumbo gallery (Eric, 2026-11-06): center the Keru holocard in the viewport with its ability cards in a floating radial gallery. Tapping or keyboard-activating any gallery card exchanges it with the jumbo along two crossing do-si-do arcs; the outgoing card occupies that same gallery slot and can be swapped back. Use real card previews instead of ability-name navigation buttons. Reduced motion swaps immediately. Preserve each card's tilt, lens effects, current signature rules and paused-combat inspection.

- Musical foundation spacing/status clearance (Eric, 2026-11-06): tighten the tableau-to-prime-value gap and put party buff/debuff trays in normal measured layout below their foundations. Wrapping status rows must participate in board fitting and remain clear of the solver/End Turn footer, including short portrait and landscape screens. Align foundation groups at the top so different status counts do not push the prime away from the tableau.

- Shared holographic lenses (Eric, 2026-11-06): apply the lenticular coating to all shared holographic TradingCards. `HOLOGRAPHIC_LENTICULAR_ENABLED` in src/proto/holoOpticsSettings.ts is the master code flag; Gear → Holographic lens effects → Lenticular effect (all holocards) is the remembered runtime toggle with shared strength/pitch. Preserve previous Bite preferences on migration. Bite retains its dedicated four-frame WebGL shader; other cards use a composed-art SVG lens displacement and coating that preserves existing parallax. Plain cards are excluded, labels remain crisp, and reduced-motion/low-FX fallbacks remain. This supersedes the Bite-only lens scope.

- Bite uniform lens coating (Eric, 2026-11-06): the experimental lens shader must remain visibly present across the complete artwork at rest and between jaw transitions. Apply stronger lens refraction, color separation and ridge highlights to the composited character plus background, rather than only the changing jaw paintings. Preserve the existing mid-bite frame-exchange calibration and keep labels readable outside optical distortion.

- Bite lens shader trial (Eric, 2026-11-06; experimental): enable a Bite-only WebGL lens-array approximation over the four registered jaw paintings. Use lateral angle to exchange adjacent frames through fine lens slivers, with restrained refraction, color separation and lens highlights; never autoplay or distort labels. Gear → Bite lens experiment provides a remembered comparison toggle, strength and lens-pitch controls. Keep the existing DOM angle flip as the no-WebGL/loading/context-loss fallback; reduced-motion/low-FX retains the closed painting. This trial does not change other cards' finishes or gameplay. See docs/art/bite-lens-shader-v1.md.

- Scratch-style holoart and Bite angle flip (Eric, 2026-11-06): use Scratch's lighter diluted swatches and softer merged wet brushwork for new Hero key art, Bite and Fast Swipes. Hero's new holoart has only her organic short bobtail, with no fire tail or old flame motif overlay. Bite uses four registered jaw states: closed face-on, progressively open at steep lateral viewing angles, reversible with tilt and driven by the shared pointer/gyro spring, never autoplay. Preserve live labels, HP flinch and gameplay; reduced motion uses the closed painting. Technique and reference roles live in art.md.

- Musical foundation inspection (Eric, 2026-11-06): clicking a friendly foundation opens its Keru jumbo portrait with selectable previews of its respective abilities, recommending its current signature ability first. Preserve card-to-enemy casting drags and prime-value transfers. Keep catalogue inspection separate from casting, show live signature rules, pause combat during inspection while card tilt remains responsive, and fit desktop/mobile with internal scrolling.

- Musical party leader (Eric, 2026-11-06): start a fresh game with 3 Momentum. During the player's turn, drag the tableau value ornament onto an eligible living support to make it the party leader and move the former leader to support. Keyboard activation of the ornament transfers to the next support. Each successful transfer spends 3 Momentum, including a transfer back. Invalid drops and attempts with insufficient Momentum leave the leader and balance as they were. Carry the rank and tableau pile to the new leader and keep each actor's HP, AP, abilities and passive progress with its owner. The leader receives tableau plays, charges Bite and drives Fast Swipes. Bite remains Hero's ability and resets the shared tableau value when cast. Continued encounters carry the party leader and Momentum balance forward. Support cards keep the 80% sizing. Clicking the Momentum gauge does nothing for now.

- Musical prime value ornament (Eric, 2026-11-06): extract the prime foundation's live rank from the card face into a dedicated ornament above the prime, outside its internal card structure. Match the prime's full width and use a height at least equal to its HP bar. Retain A/J/Q/K rank labels and normal receipt/reset behavior.

- Musical support opt-in (Eric, 2026-11-06): fresh musicaltableaus encounters start with Hero alone at 1/1 AP, with Bite and Pack Leader. Mochi is no longer a default foundation. Keep Fast Swipes available in the hand to recruit her for 1 Hero AP, and preserve recruited support/tier/progress on continued encounters. This supersedes the earlier default Mochi support setup.

- Musical visible tableau rows (Eric, 2026-11-06): show only the two frontmost tableau rows at a time. Preserve deeper dealt cards and reveal them as front cards leave. Keep the rendering and fitting row budget configurable for three, four or five visible rows later; this is a presentation limit, not a smaller deal.

- Musical supports and passive buffs (Eric, 2026-11-06): support foundation faces are 80% of the prime foundation's width and height. Show installed Fast Swipes on its owner's normal circular buff row, including live proc progress and standard buff inspection, instead of a text label beneath AP.

- Musical hand mockup sizing (Eric, 2026-11-06): persistent portrait hand cards use 55% of a single landscape foundation's width, with a slight outward fan angle and vertical centers aligned to the foundation faces. Use the same dimensions for hand placement and dragging; this supersedes the earlier half-size hand rule.

- Recruited support foundations (Eric, 2026-11-06): recruiting Mochi with Fast Swipes costs 1 Hero AP and creates an adjacent landscape support foundation. Support foundations have no tableau value and cannot receive or redraw tableau cards. Fast Swipes, its independent progress, +1 maximum AP and Legendary refunds belong to Mochi, not Hero. It listens to accepted cards on Hero’s active foundation and attacks Hero’s current target; preserve 5/4/3/3 rarity intervals and carry support ownership through continued encounters. Mochi starts at 0/1 AP; Hero retains the unconditional musical starting 1/1 AP, +1 AP per player round and normal capped receipt gains. This supersedes the earlier Fast Swipes attachment on Hero.

- Foundation rank labels (Eric, 2026-11-06): foundation tableau values, including ability holocards, display A/J/Q/K for ranks 1/11/12/13 using the shared rankLabel helper. Keep underlying ranks numeric and damage, HP, AP and progress counters numeric.

- Ability rarity authoring methodology (Eric, 2026-11-06): every new ability, including Fast Swipes, must include explicit Common/Rare/Epic/Legendary first-pass drafts in the same implementation. Preserve the requested Common baseline; scale selected amounts/caps/cadences through Rare/Epic and prefer a bounded thematic Legendary perk, without treating this as a universal multiplier or an absolute ban on Legendary numeric growth (Phoenix is an exception). Use shared tier data for rules and copy, store/migrate/persist the actual gameplay tier, expose tuning through inspection/config and test all tiers and signature restrictions. Cosmetic foil selection is never gameplay rarity. Document costs, stacking, triggers, resets and draft status. Evidence, procedure and examples: [docs/design/ability-rarity-methodology.md](./docs/design/ability-rarity-methodology.md).

- Fast Swipes rarity drafts (Eric, 2026-11-06): Common/Rare/Epic/Legendary trigger every 5/4/3/3 legal foundation receipts. Keep attach cost 1 AP, +1 max AP and base chip 1 at all tiers. Legendary also restores 1 AP when its proc actually removes enemy HP, capped at capacity; absorbed/invalid hits do not refund. These values are first-pass drafts. Config gameplay tuning updates the offered/installed tier and copy, clamps existing progress below the new trigger without a free proc, and preserves tier through continued encounters. Missing tier means Common. This supersedes the earlier Common-only Fast Swipes scaffold.

- Mochi Fast Swipes / round AP (Eric, 2026-11-05): in musicaltableaus, Common Fast Swipes is a once-per-foundation 1 AP attachment on Hero, with new ART.md-guided Mochi swipe art. It adds +1 maximum AP without refunding the equip cost, then deals 1 base chip damage to the foundation’s current target every five accepted tableau receipts. Invalid plays, Bite casts and enemy plays do not advance its independent counter. Show foundation AP capacity/current pips in a dedicated row below HP, with passive progress beneath. Start the initial encounter with 1 AP and grant +1 AP at each new player round, capped at maximum (including continued encounters); preserve other combat pages.

- Ability gyro (Eric, 2026-11-05): ability holocards, including compact hand cards and foundation cards, use the shared calibrated gyro tilt/foil spring. Request iOS sensor permission from a card gesture; retain stationary hit targets and drag behavior, pointer fallback, secure-context checks and reduced-motion/hidden-page reset.

- Bite zero accrual and HP flinch (Eric, 2026-11-05): Bite starts/reset at 0 base damage and gains exactly +1 per accepted tableau card; an uncharged cast cannot damage an enemy. When Hero’s actual HP decreases, briefly replace only the foundation subject art with her existing measured front-facing combat flinch cell, then restore the current ability art after 750 ms on the game clock. Refresh on another HP loss; shield-only absorption, healing, initial render and encounter reset do not trigger it. Keep card labels, rarity effects and HP visible.

- Bite readability and rarity foil (Eric, 2026-11-05): remove Bite’s AP pips, enlarge ability name/tableau value/current damage and move a larger-font HP bar below the holocard. Use a thicker rarity-colored border: Common silver, Rare blue, Epic purple, Legendary gold. Shared holocards use independently authored layered CSS gradients/blend modes inspired by poke-holo.simey.me: subtle Rare diagonal sheen, Epic prism foil and Legendary combined prism/star/glare layers. Preserve text readability, pointer/touch motion and static reduced-motion/low-FX alternatives. Bite’s config foil selector is cosmetic, not a gameplay rarity upgrade.

- Free Bite (Eric, 2026-11-05): Bite costs no AP and has no cooldown. Each legal tableau card added to Hero’s Bite foundation adds +1 base damage to the next Bite. It begins at 1 base damage; casting consumes the accumulated bonus and resets the foundation to value 2, ready to use again immediately during the party turn, including at zero AP. Preserve ordinary alive/target/turn/status restrictions and other abilities’ AP rules. Replace the Bite cost marker with live base damage.

- Musical landscape foundation row (Eric, 2026-11-05): remove the separate Bite hand card. Bite stays prepared on Hero’s foundation at value 2 and resets there after attacks, retaining damage charging and AP rules. Foundations use landscape 88:63 proportions and Hero’s matching landscape Bite art. Persistent hand cards align on the foundation row, overriding modal top placement and staying clear of system controls. This supersedes the earlier Bite equip-from-hand/reset-to-empty flow and portrait foundation orientation.

- Musical Bite holocard foundation (Eric, 2026-11-05): remove the visible Motes of Darkness descriptor/notes from musicaltableaus (encounter selection remains in config). Hero’s foundation uses a 63:88 playing-card silhouette and the shared Bite holocard instead of an emoji portrait, retaining live HP/AP, empty/equipped value and drag/cast behavior. Use new matching portrait/landscape Hero snapping Bite art with painted action strokes, separate background and original generated alpha; retain versions and sources.

- Musical hand size (Eric, 2026-11-05): render persistent hand cards, including Bite, at half their former width and height. Use the reduced dimensions for hand placement and drag geometry.

- Musical settings location (Eric, 2026-11-05): keep all musicaltableaus audio controls, instrument backends, cello/demo tests, theme preview/regeneration and seed information in the existing gear/config menu under Music. Preserve their state/playback while the menu is closed; keep the combat toolbar clear of music controls. Also place the Musical tableaus scene name, current constellation name/star progress/completion count and audio hint inside this config menu; remove the dedicated scene-information header from the play view.

- Seeded combat phrasing (Eric, 2026-11-05): always seed legal-play feedback from the first tableau row, even with continuous music off. Use minor battle motifs, recurring cells, chord position, event order and intensity; card values choose seed-hashed phrase variations rather than fixed scale degrees. Repeated values can play different notes as the phrase develops, and different deals/variations change their interpretation. Apply the same approach to enemy answers, combo voicings and generated constellation phrases, preserving immediate cello attacks and interval safeguards. Background music remains off on entry; this supersedes only the earlier seed-off default. Reference MIDI demo notes remain unchanged.

- SpessaSynth audition (Eric, 2026-11-05): provide an optional lazy-loaded SpessaSynth SoundFont backend in musicaltableaus using a locally hosted GeneralUser GS bank, with Test cello and the exact “TO MAKE THE END OF BATTLE” SC-88 arrangement by kou1 as a manually started/stoppable reference demo. Preserve music/seed off defaults, master mute, immediate play feedback, existing backends and loading/failure fallback. Use localhost/HTTPS for AudioWorklet; show a useful fallback message on insecure tailnet HTTP. Suppress generated game notes during the reference demo and restore live instrument programs on stop/end/mute. Keep source/version/credit/license records. See `docs/spessasynth.md`.

- Musical music/seed opt-in (Eric, 2026-11-05): start each musicaltableaus visit with battle music and automatic seed generation off, overriding older saved-on music settings for now. Keep legal-play pizzicato feedback available. Battle theme explicitly enables seed generation and accompaniment; constellation completion music follows this opt-in. Preview requires a generated theme. Preserve the experimental composer and regeneration controls for manual use.

- Musical Bite foundation (Eric, 2026-11-05): begin Hero with an empty foundation and Bite in her always-visible ability hand at tableau value 2. Drag Bite onto Hero to adopt 2; legal tableau receipts change its value and add +1 base damage each, without automatic chip attacks. Drag the equipped Bite from Hero to a living enemy to attack for 1 AP, starting from 1 base damage, then return Bite to hand at value 2 with charge reset and empty the foundation. Preserve the chip system for other pages. Keep the HP bar full-width above top-aligned wrapping AP pips.

- Musical player play voice (Eric, 2026-11-05): replace the main legal-player-play harp/electronic pluck with lower-register pizzicato cello. Use locally hosted CC0 cello-section pizzicato recordings in Orchestra mode and a rounded plucked-string synth fallback when off/loading. Preserve immediate attack, harmony protection, combo support and other musical voices.

- Musical tableaus combat hand (Eric, 2026-11-05): Hero starts with 1 maximum AP on `/117.html`. Show AP pips without the numeric AP counter or status readout. Top-align pips directly beneath HP and wrap added capacity downward into second/third rows as needed. Foundation clicks no longer open the ability hand; prepared ability/Keru cards stay visible in fans on both sides of the foundation, retaining inspection and casting interactions. Preserve other combat pages.

- Seeded battle music (Eric, 2026-11-05): on musicaltableaus, snapshot the parsed first row of the current tableau (left-to-right ranks/suits and empty slots) for a deterministic four-bar battle theme. Hold it during play; Regenerate reads the current row and advances a repeatable variation; Preview auditions two bars. Fade restrained percussion with Hero health, enemy turn and momentum, returning to quiet at battle end. Apply a pentatonic pitch and overlap guard to every pitched voice, including card receipts and constellation cadences: exclude semitone/major-seventh and tritone intervals, space low-register voices and account for releases/room tails. Rest if no safe pitch exists; avoid incompatible pre-rendered beds and repeating pitch echoes. Preserve immediate feedback, orchestra/synth selection, mute and theme toggle. This intentionally allows retuning card feedback to protect the arrangement, superseding the earlier exact-pitch preference for this page. See `docs/musical-battle-composer.md`.

- Musical soundscape expression pass (Eric, 2026-11-05): improve the orchestral mode entirely within the project, with no user-installed music software or manual production. Preserve player pitches and immediate card feedback while adding phrase dynamics, breathing gaps, restrained rubato, natural envelope variation and a softer staggered cadence. Automatically prepare layered, evolving string swells from the existing CC0 recordings using offline Web Audio rendering, with a nearby-voicing live fallback. Use a restrained chamber room and little rhythmic echo for the sampled palette. Keep the existing toggle, Preview, loading/failure fallback and mute/bed controls. These are code-arranged sample performances, not a claim of a newly recorded live orchestra.

- Musical tableaus turn camera (Eric, 2026-11-05): on `/117.html`, swing the open battle camera behind the newly attacking team whenever a turn ends, then return behind the party on its next turn. Use the existing smooth orbital transition and reduced-motion behavior; manual camera choices hold until the next turn change. Preserve the other combat pages' camera behavior.

- Musical tableaus orchestral toggle (Eric, 2026-11-05): add a remembered Orchestra on/off choice and a Preview phrase on `/117.html`. Off retains the original synthesized palette. On preloads a small, locally hosted CC0 VSCO 2 Community Edition subset: harp plucks, lower-register flute constellation melodies, viola-section support/bed and glockenspiel star accents. Use native Web Audio sample playback compatible with the existing HTTP review URL, preserve immediate card feedback and the original constellation/game rules, and keep synth fallback during loading or failure with an explicit retry. Bound sample polyphony, release loops/queued notes cleanly when switching palettes, preserve mute and bed controls, and ship source/version/credit records. See `docs/musical-orchestra.md`.

- Page 05 invisible painter (Eric, 2026-11-05): while the viewing camera is unlocked, default proximity painting to the original authored tour trajectory so the viewer can observe the same color/light reveal from any vantage. A separate brush toggle chooses original tour path or viewing-camera proximity. The notebook samples its original resolved shot independently of manual camera control and camera rejoin blending; the painter keeps the same tour clock, pause, replay and completion behavior. This supersedes the initial unlocked-camera trigger default while retaining the option to paint with the viewing camera.

- Tour camera unlock (Eric, 2026-11-05): provide an explicit camera lock/unlock toggle beside Play during forest tours. Unlocking allows existing orbit, pan, zoom, touch and WASD controls without stopping or pausing the sequence, book opening, paint or lights. Page 05's selected painter source controls proximity reveals independently of the viewing camera. Keep the current posture on unlock; following again blends into the current authored shot without restarting the clock. Pause and Stop retain their separate functions.

- Page 05 proximity painting (Eric, 2026-11-05): all perimeter pop-ups and extension trees unfold together with the book, initially rendered only as pencil sketches. Camera proximity now drives the existing ink-spread paint-in technique, surface pigment sparkles and subsequent local lights; it never drives their physical rise. Painted areas retain their color after the camera leaves, and replay resets to sketch. Keep the authored 36-second tour, manual preload/play, surrounding night/fog, treeline light origins and delayed clearing migration, physical stowing and standing front trees at the ending. This supersedes page 05's proximity-triggered/staggered pop-up opening, including saved shot staging during this cinematic; editor layouts remain intact. See `docs/book-proximity-paint.md`.

- Page 23 Believe film (Eric, 2026-11-04): use the complete user-supplied `Downloads/index.html` film archived under `public/assets/sources/book/page23`, preserving all four worlds and its 120-second sequence. Wrap it with a normally opening book, shimmering “Believe.” in the upper-left page, and a camera flight through the dot above its i. After the film, turn toward the dot as a light, fly out through the page, rise above the book and slowly spiral down to a centered, magically closing book. Hold the closed ending without looping. Preserve manual/preloaded Play, the shared paper policy and existing pages/editor documents. The isolated Three r180 runtime uses pinned local dependencies; see `docs/book-believe.md`.

- Hero platformer idle rig (Eric, 2026-11-04): keep breath animation open-eyed and schedule synchronized two-eye blinks independently, never more often than once per four completed breath cycles, with light random variation. Add sitting, rolling, spinning, canine soldier saluting and platform-bounded butterfly chasing as awake idles; tapping Hero's painted body cycles these, excluding sleep. After a substantial idle (currently 60 seconds), lie down, then sleep after a short prone rest. Wake/rise before navigation, finish action recoveries without teleporting, and restrict butterfly chasing to sufficiently long text ledges. Use the new ImageGen idle-v2 art and separate butterfly; see `docs/art/hero-idle-v2.md`.

- Page 22 lyrics and touch navigation (Eric, 2026-11-04): replace the Lorem Ipsum terrain with the user-supplied Alegría lyrics, preserving their wording/order while splitting longer lines into connected ledges. Add two-finger pinch zoom anchored at the gesture midpoint and two-finger pan. Delay single-touch navigation until tap release and suppress movement orders throughout multi-touch/cancelled gestures. Selecting a new destination resumes Hero camera follow. This supersedes page 22's placeholder wording.

- Page 118 Sacred Forest Meadow scale and paint (Eric, 2026-11-08): use the supplied ruin screenshot as a visual scale and atmosphere reference. Widen and deepen the original footprint to 1.5× as the estimated reference, then add 15% for a 1.725× footprint scale. Triple the height of the 1.15× version for a 3.45× vertical scale relative to the original model. Anchor the model and navigation at the return arch and keep Hero and both arches at their existing size. Repaint the meadow using the Hero-reference watercolor material atlas, with world-space mist and softly glowing motes throughout the gorge. Low reduces their draw and fog budgets. Reduced motion holds the washes, mist and motes still. The live doorway view shares the same meadow art.

- Page 118 meadow wall destinations (Eric, 2026-11-08): clicking visible wall geometry in Sacred Forest Meadow infers a nearby walkable landing, including elevated stone tops. Plan routes over the actual solid model triangles with full-body clearance, walking detours and checked jump arcs. Automated routes may select the existing single, double or triple jump height needed for a safe landing. Keep overlapping lower floors and upper ledges distinct by Hero's current height, and keep the destination marker on the selected elevation. Reject unreachable targets with feedback instead of teleporting or inventing climbing. Control-click still teleports only directly onto a valid upward-facing dry surface. Preserve portal routes, camera clearance and manual action cancellation.

- Page 118 instruction overlays (Eric, 2026-11-08): omit on-screen input instructions in the grounds, meadow and Free camera settings. Keep controls functional, retain accessible control descriptions, and use brief route status or failure feedback without input reminders.

- Page 118 journey camera (Eric, 2026-11-08): accepted point-to-move routes start a cinematic camera director in the grounds and Sacred Forest Meadow. Anticipate route direction, use wider establishing/travel views and closer arrival/jump framing, and co-frame Hero with visible distant landmarks when space and line of sight permit. Hold compositions through turns, ease shot/lens changes, protect Hero's framing, and retain the existing final lens and scenery-clearance checks. Manual orbit, zoom, follow-camera lift or Free camera immediately takes over and remains in control until a new accepted destination. Stop scripted framing across teleport/reset/portal handoffs. Reduced motion uses stable view offsets and lens rather than automatic orbit or zoom. Add no input-instruction overlay.

- Page 118 click-to-travel controls (Eric, 2026-11-08): click or tap dry ground, a bridge deck or either portal arch to move Hero in follow mode. Destination routes choose the pace and show a marker. Hold Control while clicking or tapping a visible dry surface to teleport there with no route search. A teleport stops the current route and returns Free camera to follow mode. WASD and arrow keys pan in Free camera. Space still jumps and can be used along a route. C and X retain their actions and release the route. This supersedes manual directional movement and Shift-to-run for Hero on page 118.

- Page 118 Sacred Forest Meadow doorway (Eric, 2026-11-08): the right-hand keep arch as viewed from the drawbridge gate is a two-way portal. The courtyard arch begins nearly black with dark fog flowing out over the ground. Within 15 ft of Hero, the fog recedes as faceted reflective foil and prismatic glass reveal a live view of the meadow from a second camera. Walking or tapping through triggers the reference's membrane ripple, light pulse, stardust and glass tones. Reduced motion holds the fog still, keeps the revealed view and stationary foil, and crosses immediately. Hero uses the existing movement rig on the meadow's dry model surfaces. The return arch leads back to the courtyard. Sacred Forest Meadow by [XanderPriest281](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/sacred-forest-meadow-4a8b56d5b9324272b5f2d5262650e125) is licensed under [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). The runtime GLB converts legacy specular-glossiness materials to metallic-roughness using the original diffuse textures.

- Page 118 camera controls (Eric, 2026-11-08): add combat's free camera through Settings → Free camera. Drag or middle-drag orbits, right/Shift-drag pans, wheel/pinch zooms, WASD/arrows pan and Q/E move vertically. Touch freecam uses one-finger pan, two-finger pan/pinch, and three-finger twist/tilt. Follow mode retains tap-to-travel and one-finger orbit while adding pinch zoom. In Sacred Forest Meadow, Q and E also lower and raise the follow camera. Check lens clearance and visibility to Hero before using a pose. Near a wall, shorten the horizontal boom at the requested height when possible and retract along the boom under a ceiling. Keep the requested orbit independent of collision corrections, hold the retracted pose briefly before easing back, and check the final eased position. Register overhead screen orientation to the orbit heading. Multi-finger gestures never issue a travel tap. Switch freecam off or press Escape to follow Hero; Return to drawbridge also restores follow mode. Keep Hero's action keys separate from freecam input.

- Page 118 celestial sky and player fog (Eric, 2026-11-08): show stars, faint constellation connections, a galactic band and distant galaxy washes in the night sky. Keep the sky orientation fixed in world space. Add height-bounded volumetric fog whose density falls near Hero and builds farther away; center its clearing on the player rather than the camera. Fog stops at visible scenery, catches nearby lantern light and attenuates celestial light through the same volume. Use bounded ray samples with no extra depth or shadow pass. Low retains fewer static stars and simpler static fog; reduced motion freezes twinkle and drift while player proximity remains responsive. Preserve navigation, sprint, water reflections and the existing dark atmosphere.

- Page 118 sustained sprint (Eric, 2026-11-08): after manual running reaches sprint, retain 10.8 m/s along the original world direction when run/movement keys are released. Camera orbit alone does not steer or slow that sprint. Fresh directional input that changes direction, crouch/dive, collision or an accepted travel destination takes over; jumping preserves momentum. Free camera, focus loss and reset cancel the latch. Destination navigation retains controlled arrival braking. This supersedes the earlier release-to-brake behavior once manual sprint has begun.

- Page 118 watercolor water (Eric, 2026-11-08): adapt the Susurrus reference with low-resolution, cadence-capped planar reflections and animated flowing pigment washes. Filter reflection detail locally, leaving Hero and UI untouched. Replace the regular sparkle grid with broken brush ribbons, diluted shoreline margins and pigment pooling; use the actual land boundaries without seams across connected water channels. Coordinate the waterfall and landing foam with the water clock. Low omits reflection targets and uses static painted water; reduced motion freezes ambient wash motion while reflections may follow deliberate camera/player movement. Preserve dusk, lantern light, cached shadows and shoreline collision. Research and further material recommendations: docs/research/castle-watercolor-susurrus.md.

- Page 119 moving-water variant (Eric, 2026-11-09): copy page 118 into `/119.html`, retaining the world, art and journey camera. Revise passive reflections to resemble the subtle parallax revealed by slightly moving the camera: gently offset the reflection capture's virtual eye within the existing resolution/cadence budgets, without moving the player's camera or adding passes. Avoid swimming UV distortion; retain quiet watercolor pigment, broad sheen and shorelines. Low omits reflection captures; reduced motion freezes passive motion. Page 118 keeps its existing water behavior. Page 119's fly-in and resting arrival use a raised three-quarter view of Hero, the crossing and moat. A single camera-icon button in Settings copies precise camera telemetry including position, target, orientation, lens, viewport and Hero location.

- Page 119 immersed bridge golf (Eric, 2026-11-09): remove the initial drawbridge. Near its outer abutment, reveal an interaction that eases into an overhead blueprint view. Four seed cards start the near-bank row (2,1,2,1). Build a full four-by-four blueprint with collected wood-element cards branded as Wood Planks. Every new card must touch an existing card along an edge and differ by one from every occupied orthogonal neighbour; diagonals do not count. Allow choosing cards, legal-target feedback, undo and leaving/resuming. The complete blueprint is required before the whole sketch bridge becomes traversable; partial cards provide no footing and triple jumps, routing or teleporting cannot bypass completion. Hero's traversal paints the completed sketch into the world using the shared book proximity-paint and ink transition shader. Preserve page 118.

- Page 119 presentation correction (Eric, 2026-11-09): fly directly into the raised moat composition without visiting the old arrival first. Seed follow-camera damping at the flight endpoint to prevent handoff jitter, including replay. All bridge cards, packet faces, inventory faces and blueprint outlines use the standard 63:88 playing-card proportion with uniform scale; widen the bridge and its collision footprint to fit cards instead of stretching them. For this intermediary, place all three Wood Plank packets beside the starting lightposts, floating a few feet above ground, billboarding to the camera and slowly spinning. Use shared geometry and view-dependent metallic/holofoil sheen driven by the existing lantern positions and flicker, without new lights, shadows or reflection passes. Reduced motion freezes packet drift/spin. Exterior golf redemption remains planned, superseding the earlier temporary distant ground-packet placement.

- Planned wood-plank redemption (Eric, 2026-11-09; not implemented): exterior exploration finds wood-element card packs branded as Wood Planks; play games of golf to redeem their construction cards before taking them to the bridge. Iterate on redemption encounters and their rewards later. The page 119 intermediary collects three authored packs directly; this is temporary scaffolding, not the final redemption flow.

- Page 119 upright carousel packets and united paths (Eric, 2026-11-09): keep floating packet faces upright, with their bottom edges level to the ground, and spin them slowly like a carousel around world Y. Camera movement must not tilt them or drive their rotation. Retain gentle vertical float, view-/lantern-responsive foil and standard 63:88 proportions. Reduced motion freezes float and spin. This supersedes both full camera-quaternion billboarding and the temporary no-spin rule. At the crossroads left of the drawbridge and other junctions, paint the road network as shared outer and inner wash layers so connected paths unite without internal borders or end caps. Retain outer path margins, watercolor texture and navigation.

- Card collection flair (Eric, 2026-11-09): collecting a world card or pack starts a quick spinning bounce toward Hero, tracking her current position. Shrink uniformly and linearly to zero as it reaches her, preserving the playing-card aspect ratio. Grant its cards once, on arrival, and prevent repeated collection while in flight. Contact creates a brief shimmer on Hero's actual visible sprite and a sparkle burst using the Sacred Forest Meadow portal's cyan/pink star particles. Pool and cap effects, animate particle age on the GPU without buffer uploads or extra lights/reflection passes, and preserve the portal's existing appearance. Low uses fewer particles; reduced motion uses a short direct pickup and restrained steady Hero glow without spinning, bouncing or particle bursts.

- Page 119 imagined bridge and quest identity (Eric, 2026-11-09): replace the floating bottom Build the crossing prompt with an interactive sketch in the moat. Reveal increasing span and pencil detail as Hero approaches, including from the unfinished edge of a partially built bridge; fade the unbuilt sketch away as she departs. Leave placed cards and completed structure intact between visits. Each contiguous complete added row forms one walkable third of the bridge; incomplete rows grant no footing, and the entire blueprint is required to reach the castle, including for jumps and teleporting. Completed sections remain a sketch until the completed crossing is painted by Hero. Undo returns Hero to remaining safe footing if needed. Keep Build/Continue the crossing as a secondary control directly beneath the chapter/page identity. Show “Chapter 1: The Castle Grounds” and “Page 119: Building Bridges”; either entry opens an accessible, dismissible details dialog with setting/quest text and live collection/construction progress. This supersedes the earlier all-or-nothing bridge footing and bottom-screen build-button rules.

- Page 119 tactile bridge cards and crossing shot (Eric, 2026-11-09): support mouse/touch drag-and-drop and momentum-based physics flicks into legal bridge slots. Move the actual world card, preserve 63:88 proportions and uniform scale, show legal destinations, apply bounded velocity/friction and snap legal landings. Invalid drops, missed flicks, cancellation and focus loss return cards without spending them; retain click/keyboard placement and Undo. Clicking a reachable space across the width of the far bridge end navigates Hero there and starts a cinematic zoom-out/pan with a gentle yaw sweep around the supplied reference (camera [20.2725,26.4359,60.1415], target [-.7921,4.8916,45.9332], FOV 60, reference aspect 2.7921). Co-frame the bridge and Hero, adapt distance to the viewport, ease from the rendered shot, and preserve manual takeover and reduced motion. - Frost rescue and surge (Eric, 2026-10-30): Fight two authored Void mote guards and collect their stranded Frost motes before challenging dark Demi. - Rescue lighting and provisional balance (2026-10-30): Use Roam's real local-only renderer with no sky/ambient or carried Hero light. The rescue variant adds faint Frost and dark-Demi POI emitters; collecting Frost and defeating Demi remove those sources. The cub emits a small cold light, the nearby berry light weakens during the surge and the den remains a warm navigation anchor. No CSS darkness overlays or descriptive text as a lighting substitute. Initial tuning: Hero 32 HP, zero innate abilities/Burn/AP capacity; two 6-HP Void guards, 18-HP dark Demi, then two solo 6-HP surge motes and two pairs of 4-HP surge motes. Victories restore 3 HP to living party members. Cub Demi starts at 24 HP. Each common mote adds 1 max AP to its current holder (cap 6); two Frost pickups outward and two small Verdant rewards on return. Motes can transfer between encounters and preserve holder-specific recipes/recharge. Failure loses unbanked motes; reaching the den after the surge permanently secures Demi and banks the haul exactly once. Keep earlier trial saves separate. These numbers are playtest parameters, not a finished economy.

Default darkness strength is 25% (Eric, 2026-10-30); the previous darkness corresponds to the 15% control setting; control actual engine transmittance and sprite illumination, not decorative darkness overlays. Roam has no sky/ambient illumination or carried Hero light: only world cells (0,2) and (0,1) emit light. Off bypasses visual lighting, shadows and lighting atmosphere. - Presentation discipline (Eric, 2026-10-30, standing rule): Do not propose or scaffold large, chunky, “PowerPoint” UI, including in early proofs of concept. Avoid dashboard shells, oversized title/vital bands, big empty panels, nested boxes, duplicated summaries and explanatory chrome around a small playable area. Use the existing game stage, cards and interactions with compact, deliberate controls. A proof of concept must test its game loop without establishing a throwaway presentation system.
- Darkness and Aldan lighting (Eric, 2026-10-30, standing rule): Darkness is a gameplay and spatial-visibility system, implemented through Proto’s overhead camera and actual lighting engine. The Aldan Seed must emit world light; decreasing energy must progressively affect its reach, what the player can perceive, orientation, environmental danger and the ability to find a way home. The dark must feel looming and dangerous, with a credible possibility of getting lost. CSS tints, gradients, vignettes, decorative fog and text describing darkness are not acceptable substitutes. Do not reuse these “flavored darkness” methods to claim this requirement is implemented. Validate world geometry, occlusion, shadows, visibility and changing Seed light in the real scene when that work resumes.
- Earlier staged Demi rescue prototype (historical, superseded by current exploration rescue): Used an authored outward journey, Demi’s refuge, recruitment-triggered surge and two return encounters. This supersedes the earlier combat-only trial’s starting AP/Burn, free instant extraction, all-rewards-on-defeat and no-companion scaffolding. Hero starts with no abilities, no innate Burn, zero AP capacity and no AP gauge. Every common mote grants its holder +1 max AP, capped at 6; Splash does not double-dip on capacity. Motes and their effects transfer freely between encounters, with current AP clamped rather than copied. Fresh builds remain separate from banked camp motes.
- Rescue commitment and permanent safety (Eric, 2026-10-30): Early retreat is possible only outside encounters and before recruitment. Recruiting Demi explicitly commits the return: no rope or extraction shortcut. The current exploration slice requires at least four return ambushes before the party reaches the den. Failure loses all unbanked motes and leaves an uncompleted rescue available to retry. Demi becomes permanently safe only on arrival home and cannot be rescued repeatedly. Recipe knowledge persists independently of run resources. Housing and harvesting are deferred.
- Holder-specific Frost recipes (Eric, 2026-10-30): Two Frost motes on Hero activate Ice Climber: take the immediately covered second-row card if playable on Hero, leaving its covering card in place; recharge after ten subsequent Hero tableau receipts. Two Frost on Demi activate Glacial Armor: spend 2 AP to grant each living ally 3 armor, capped at 6 without lowering existing stronger armor. Transfers preserve recharge state and never copy AP. Guarantee two Frost motes on the outward path; the return remains solvable without forcing either recipe or a prescribed allocation.
- Rescue presentation (Eric, 2026-10-30, updated for manual exploration): Use Proto’s actual world renderer and authored visual composition as the presentation field guide. The current exploration slice keeps the player in control of walking and camera; do not force establishing shots or staged travel. Use real renderer lighting for the surge, plus actual encounter escalation. No lock-step tutorials, mandatory ability demonstrations, scripted input gates, forced target selection or waiting for a movie before actions become available. This is a technical/combat investigation; the bespoke world now tests manual exploration and a combat rescue loop.

- Combat-only mote trials (Eric, 2026-10-30): /aldan.html tests five combat scenes using the Proto 100-series. Remove gathering, hazards, resources, supplies, world exploration, travel costs and all Aldan Seed/light mechanics from this iteration. Complete a fight, select a power, then choose the next fight. Use a compact FTL-inspired node diagram to show cleared nodes, the current fight, connections and at least three eligible next encounters. Node hints describe combat difficulty and the reward affinity; choosing a node immediately starts combat without travel confirmation or any cost. Keep the shared tableau, combat rules and reward-card inspection. No summons in this pass.
- Fresh builds, persistent collection (Eric, 2026-10-30): Each new combat run starts at the same baseline, with a fresh build. Earned motes and major abilities are kept in a browser-local collection, including on Escape Rope or defeat; do not equip the collection automatically on later runs. HP, AP, foundations, equipped motes and buffs carry between fights within the run. Escape Rope remains available at any time, immediately ends the run with earned powers kept, and retains an earned but unchosen reward for selection afterward. Bank exactly once. Preserve the earlier variant's browser save and import its earned ability collection without its build or Seed systems.
- Motes toward Keru rescue (Eric, 2026-10-30, design direction): Collected motes are intended to contribute to rescuing a Keru that matches those motes. Explore combining sufficient matching motes across runs to enable that rescue. Motes therefore connect small combat gains within a run to persistent Keru collection, and reward affinities can help players pursue a particular rescue. Keep equipped run stacks separate from banked collection quantities; a fresh build must not erase rescue progress. Exact Keru-to-mote matches, quantities, mixed-mote recipes and whether combining consumes motes remain open for design and playtesting. This records the progression direction; the current combat slice has not implemented combining or rescue, and this does not add summons to the current pass.
- Micro power progression (Eric, 2026-10-30): Hero starts at 3 max AP and an innate 10% chance to inflict the shared Burn debuff on a positive direct enemy hit. Ember Mote adds 1 fire to full-AP chip hits; Splash Mote adds 1 max AP and a 10% chance per AP payment to save exactly 1 AP; Mote of Frost adds 1 ice armor at turn end. Further small motes can adjust burn chance, healing, evasion, AP generation and opening armor. Each mote can be equipped up to three times; describe stacking and limits precisely. AP conservation never bypasses affordability, applies once per payment and uses persisted deterministic randomness. Keep these micro rewards out of other Proto sandboxes' major-ability pools.
- Combat-only provisional balance (2026-10-30): 32 starting HP, 0 banked AP, 3 AP cap, no Momentum decay. First weakened Mote has 6 HP; later solo Motes have 7–10 HP; optional pair encounters have 4–6 HP each. Victories restore 4 HP with no separate rest action. Fights 1, 2 and 4 award a mote; 3 and 5 award a defining ability or an upgrade. Frost armor gains are capped at 6 without reducing stronger existing armor. Innate Burn uses the shared 2 damage at the start of each of the next two enemy turns. Cinder adds 10 percentage points to burn chance; Verdant heals 1 per six receipts; Gale adds 5% evasion; Spark gives a bonus AP every six receipts (five/four with more copies); Pebble adds opening armor. This is combat-tuning scaffolding, not a finished campaign economy. The real-lighting rule above applies only when spatial exploration is resumed; this combat iteration has no Seed or darkness simulation.

- 100-series reward controls (Eric, 2026-10-29): Put the reroll dice on the reward view in place of the debug reroll button. Inspecting a reward ability opens its card at jumbo size while retaining rarity tuning and selection.

- 100-series victory rewards (Eric, 2026-10-29): Present rewards centered over the battle without a modal frame, keeping Rewards, Debug, Next scene and Go to scene controls. Remove the combat-training heading, completion heading and replay action from this presentation. Offer at least three distinct cards, filling a short themed pool from other available rewards. Select a reward before proceeding.

- Mobile map lighting trial (Eric, 2026-10-29): phones and touch-first tablets use a bounded Canvas 2D ground-light path, sharing the normal day/night clock, local pools, obstacle masks and Global/Table lights preferences. Retain existing sprite environment lighting and shadows, with lower sprite oversampling and no extra masked light washes, volumetric haze, shafts, decorative air particles or cinema blend on this path.
