This is an initial mobile rendering trial, pending real-device visual/performance validation; desktop keeps its current renderer. Append `lightingRenderer=mobile` or `lightingRenderer=desktop` to the Proto URL for A/B testing. Gameplay light sampling and discovery remain unchanged.

- Reward ability targeting (Eric, 2026-10-29): Dragging a powered reward ability targets a legal foundation by overlap with the card's borders, with the same overlap determining the highlight and the drop.

- Ability card cost and element (Eric, 2026-10-29): Show AP costs as reusable card bubbles and elements with authored element art; avoid spelling out the upfront AP cost in the card effect text.

- Enemy misses (Eric, 2026-10-29): Show a happy reaction on the intended party target and a stylized Missed announcement when an enemy attack misses or is evaded; blocked zero-damage hits are not misses.

- 100-series reward decks (Eric, 2026-10-29): Hero starts without the default Keru summon cards. Earned active reward abilities appear in the owning actor's radial and are dragged to a target to cast; passive rewards retain their buff effects.

- Reward rarity tuning (Eric, 2026-10-29): The reward card jumbo offers a manual rarity selector for tuning; update the card and its effects to the chosen tier and grant that tier when obtained.

- Keru editor creation and tags (Eric, 2026-10-29): Support creating new editable Keru and assigning multiple element, attribute and custom tags. Save them in the browser draft catalog and include them in JSON export; they do not create gameplay definitions. Renamed entries use their current name in the editor URL while retaining their stable internal identity and old links.

- Keru editor names and links (Eric, 2026-10-29): A renamed Keru’s displayed identifier and editor URL follow its current name. Keep stable internal catalog IDs for existing art and drafts, and keep original ID links working.

- Proto editor Keru workspace (Eric, 2026-10-29): Keru is the default editor section, replacing the Actors label. Keep existing entries and add the proposed Dewi, Frodo, Brramble, Achillies, Harecules, Skadi, Keshet, Galahail and Fenrirr as editable concepts, plus Snow Golem; do not imply concepts have finished character art. Put identity and the 3D/art preview side by side on wide screens, with tabbed asset categories and responsive browsing. Preserve browser-draft saving, export, existing rigs and reactions, and other authoring sections.

- Ice rewards and Golem (Eric, 2026-10-29): Whiteout uses the supplied snowy scene. Offer randomized rarity on a 60/25/12/3 Common/Rare/Epic/Legendary curve, with at least one above Common. Add Glacial Lance, Shatterstrike and Do you want to...? to the Ice pool. Shatterstrike consumes Frost for bonus damage and stagger and leaves enemies more vulnerable to Frost. Snow Golem costs 4 AP, has 20 HP and its own foundation, knows Glacial Lance, gains Rime Retort at Rare+, and pays 1 AP per later turn or loses 5 HP. Legendary farewell Nuclear Winter deals 25 to each enemy and applies two Frost to survivors. Reward selection supports multiple cards by tap or drag to Hero, variable offer sizes, a narrower tabbed modal, and visible Hero foundation and buffs without scrollbars.

- Reward modal debug controls (Eric, 2026-10-29): Offer Hero healing by 5 HP, half maximum HP or to full, a reward reroll, and a reward-theme selector. The currently authored Ice pool has three cards; rerolls rearrange them until additional rewards are authored.

- Ice reward presentation and preview (Eric, 2026-10-29): Use the shared layered ability holocard for the three reward choices, with ice artwork and parallax layers but no Dem or Demi art. Append `&reward` to a combat sandbox URL to open the reward picker at scenario start.

- 100-series ice Keru rewards (Eric, 2026-10-29): After a win, present a gentle fan of three common rewards; choose one to carry into the next battle. Ice Armor gains armor equal to unused AP at turn end, capped at 5, and absorbs damage. Rime Retort gains a stack per received chip or stagger attack and at six freezes the attacker for the rest of its turn, stopping its sequence; reset the stacks. Whiteout gives chip hits a 20% chance of +1 ice damage and Frost for two enemy turns; Frost blocks empty ability pip slots and each enemy stack grants the owner 10% evasion. Preserve reward choices across 100-series encounters.

- Scenario 103 Three Motes (Eric, 2026-10-29): Copy scenario 101 with three 10 HP Motes of Darkness, each with an independent tableau foundation and chip attacks. On every sandbox scenario win, offer a picker of authored scenes; a 100-series destination selected after a 100-series win carries the party state. Replay uses the encounter checkpoint. This supersedes consecutive-only progression.

- 100-series progression (Eric, 2026-10-29): Winning an authored 100-series encounter offers the next consecutive authored encounter. Carry the party’s HP, AP, statuses, foundations, buffs, Keru and Momentum into a fresh enemy/tableau setup; retarget new enemies. Replay restores the encounter-entry checkpoint, while manually selecting another scenario starts it fresh. Do not skip unimplemented encounter numbers.

- Scenario 101 Two Motes (Eric, 2026-10-29): Copy scenario 100 with two 10 HP Motes of Darkness. Each has its own tableau foundation and independently plays legal cards for 1 chip damage per receipt.

- Scenario 100 Mote of Darkness (Eric, 2026-10-29): Copy scenario 1012 with Hero as the sole foundation and eight columns. Replace the dragon with a 10 HP Mote of Darkness, with no abilities; it plays legal tableau cards onto its foundation and deals 1 chip damage per receipt.

- Global combat chip presentation (Eric, 2026-10-29): Apply chip damage immediately to gameplay and the HP number; retain an amber damage segment on the HP bar during the turn, then visibly drain it at the turn handoff. Respect reduced motion.

- S1012 full AP chip (Eric, 2026-10-29): When Hero is at her current AP cap, her tableau receipt chip damage doubles, including the receipt that fills the cap. Ability damage is unaffected.

- S1012 dragon foundation (Eric, 2026-10-29): Show the Dusk Dragon’s tableau value. On its active enemy turn, after feeding Deep Breath, it plays legal adjacent cards onto its own foundation and deals 1 chip damage to Hero per receipt. Ability feeds do not deal chip damage.

- Art authoring standard (Eric, 2026-10-29): Hero’s berry-snuffling painting is the primary reference for hand-painted storybook watercolor, including softer washes and restrained facial detail. Follow [ART.md](./ART.md) for ImageGen reference, prompting and review guidance; existing assets remain unchanged until explicitly revised.

- Scenario 111 partycomp (Eric, 2026-10-29): Copy 1012 with five tableau columns and three playable player foundations, Dem on the left, Hero in the center, and Banks on the right. Each has its own tableau value, HP and AP. Dem uses the supplied demi-expressions-v1 expression sheet, including combat reactions.

- S1012 familiar layout (Eric, 2026-10-29): Put each familiar name in a pill at the top of its foundation, let its art fill the width, place its six scaffold pips below the foundation, and show its buff on Hero’s foundation.

- S1012 foundations (Eric, 2026-10-29): Center Hero’s name over her HP/AP section. Familiar foundations share the portrait and meter design with six empty scaffold pips. Tap a familiar for its current usable spells; hold for two seconds with a progress circle to open its jumbo. Remove the cast explanation from Keru cards in 1012. Preserve feeding and drag dismissal.

- S1012 Hero expressions (Eric, 2026-10-29): Hero defaults to a happy battle portrait; show her angry expression with anger marks only when move highlighting is active. Preserve combat reactions and defeat expression.

- S1012 familiar foundations (Eric, 2026-10-29): drag a Keru from the hand onto Hero’s foundation to familiarize it; direct hand-card drops no longer cast on actors. Explicit cast actions remain available. Each invoked Keru has a narrower foundation matching Hero’s foundation height, with no value, HP, AP or status readout, and its familiar buff appears below that foundation. Preserve upkeep, feeding, dismissal and farewell behavior.

- S1012 upkeep meter (Eric, 2026-10-29): display AP reserved for familiar upkeep on the left end of Hero’s AP pip row, followed by free AP and any shortfall. Keep existing payment and preview rules.

- S1012 Keru drag token (Eric, 2026-10-29): as a dragged Keru card reaches Hero’s horizontal plane, replace it with a compact expression token; its visible overlap selects Hero and release uses the same hitbox. Show the complete current Familiarize effect, cost, upkeep, farewell and any blocked reason beside the token. Preserve legal targeting, AP costs, discard and cooldown rules.

- Keru Cir and Banks card art (Eric, 2026-10-29): center Cir’s face above her interaction text at a scale comparable to the other Keru, cropping her wings as needed. Banks’s Keru card uses soft watercolor brushwork and clear facial details while retaining her sleek, athletic proportions. Character and background are separate art layers.

- S1012 hand placement and hover (Eric, 2026-10-29): position the ability hand near the bottom of the screen, allowing it to cover Auto-M and End Turn while keeping every rotated and hovered card above the bottom of the viewport. On mouse hover, enlarge the hovered card 25% and place it temporarily above the other cards; preserve touch and drag interactions.

- S1012 hand fans (Eric, 2026-10-29): ability hand cards are 15% larger. Overflow forms a separate rear fan; hovering or tapping a rear card raises it over the front fan before jumbo inspection. Preserve existing drag and tap behavior for front cards.

- S1012 Keru card presentation (Eric, 2026-10-29): hand and jumbo cards show current Cast and Familiarize effects, with the AP bubble at the upper-right edge and stored value at the lower-right. Jumbo key art may extend beyond the card horizontally; frame Cir as a close-up. Dragged Keru are translucent. Invoked Keru occupy compact familiar foundations; hand cards familiarize on Hero’s foundation. Preserve cast, upkeep, cooldown and dismissal rules.

- Scenario 1012 opening AP (Eric, 2026-10-28): Hero starts with 0 AP on a fresh encounter or restart. Existing earning, invocation costs and upkeep rules remain in effect.

- Keru deck descriptions (Eric, 2026-10-28): ability deck Keru cards show separate translucent Cast and Familiar descriptions as parallax overlay boxes using current tier effects. While dragging over a target, show only the relevant outcome. Familiarized Keru leave the hand and return when dismissed, subject to existing cooldown and discard rules. Keep the portrait visible, and retain reduced-motion behavior, jumbo inspection and existing use rules.

- S1012 direct Keru hand use and clear art (Eric, 2026-10-28): dropping a hand Keru on a legal actor uses its immediate ability and discards the card from the hand for the battle, rather than displaying it tapped. Existing exhausted rules enforce no reuse; Restart restores the deck. Dropping on an empty familiar square invokes/familiarizes it under the current cost/upkeep rules. Distinguish these targets and reject illegal drops without spending or discarding. Tap still inspects; hold or dragging movement lifts only the selected card, leaving the rest of the hand stationary without pointer-driven hand scrolling. Ability card compact/jumbo faces omit the lower description/rarity footer body and heavy bottom scrim so holocard art remains clear; inspection rules and explicit actions remain outside the art. Preserve buff-hold jumbo and existing feeding/dismissal mechanics.

- Global lights enabled and configurable (Eric, 2026-10-28): global day/night lighting defaults on in every scene, including combat sandboxes and touch devices. The main and combat config flyouts share the same persisted, live Global lights preference. Switching off bypasses global shading/haze; switching on restores it without a reload. Preserve independent local/Table lights and gameplay rules. This supersedes the sandbox and mobile forced-off overrides.

- S1012 Keru inspection gestures (Eric, 2026-10-28): a single tap/click on a Keru radial card opens jumbo inspection. Holding its hand card lifts it out of the radial to drag freely, without opening jumbo or invoking until a legal drop. Holding a Keru buff badge opens its jumbo, suppressing the release click and dismissal. Preserve tap-to-details and drag-off dismissal on invoked badges.
- Riv card artwork (Eric, 2026-10-28): use the authored water-otter v2 artwork for Riv's shared invoke card in the hand, held/dragged face and jumbo, including S1012, replacing the R placeholder. Runtime copy: `public/assets/runtime/keru/riv/riv-watercolor-v2.png`; approved source: `artifacts/water-otter-keru/water-otter-keru-v2.png`.

- Broken enemy ability vulnerability (Eric, 2026-10-28): when its final pip is removed, an enemy ability stays on the field as a tilted, visibly broken card. It cannot charge or fire, but adjacent player cards can still be played onto it, changing its value and granting Momentum. Stagger its owner once when it breaks; retain the card through the skipped enemy turn and the following player turn, then remove it when that stagger ends. A stagger skips the next enemy turn and lasts until the end of the following player turn.

- Battle expressions (Eric, 2026-10-28): frame Keru faces close up and clip each expression to its own sprite cell and portrait frame. Use an aggressive expression for the battle idle, retaining damage, attack, victory and defeat reactions.

- Enemy foundation alignment (Eric, 2026-10-28): center enemy foundations horizontally; a single enemy stays centered even when its ability card is displayed beside it.

- Combat foundation layout (Eric, 2026-10-28): place player foundations directly beneath the closest tableau row, with player buff and debuff trays beneath the foundations and enemy trays above theirs. Remove the separate spills container while keeping loose cards playable in the tableau.

- Deep Breath stagger immunity (Eric, 2026-10-28): its owner cannot build or suffer stagger while the ability retains any pip capacity, including while charging or readied. Removing its final pip discards the ability and immediately staggers the owner; thereafter normal stagger rules apply. The party can counterplay Deep Breath in all encounters. This supersedes the earlier vulnerable-caster backfire rule.

- Enemy ability progress limit (Eric, 2026-10-28): ability definitions may cap positive pip progress per enemy turn, counting both card and passive gains. Deep Breath is capped at one pip per enemy turn; player reductions and permanent pip erosion do not consume this allowance.

- Enemy ability pip erosion (Eric, 2026-10-28): playing onto an enemy ability removes a filled pip, or when none are filled permanently removes an empty pip from its capacity. Scale the ability damage by remaining capacity, rounded to the nearest point. At zero capacity discard the ability permanently and immediately stagger its owner. Ability tooltips trigger on their names, including enemy ability cards.

- Ability card tooltips (Eric, 2026-10-28): trigger ability inspection from hovering or tapping the ability name, leaving the card body clear for play.

- Tableau keyboard controls (Eric, 2026-10-28): hold left Control to highlight legal moves; right Control plays the best available solver move. In combat, highlight mode also draws an arrow for the solver-recommended tableau move.

- Combat controls (Eric, 2026-10-28): remove the Element indicator and the Withdraw button throughout combat; the Withdraw button is deprecated.

- Scenario 1004 truefourstar (Eric, 2026-10-28): iterate on 1002 with Hero as the sole tableau foundation with a random opening value, two familiar squares stacked on each side; empty squares appear only while dragging a familiar card, and Dem, Cir, Mochi, Riv and Banks are available in Hero’s radial as placement scaffolding with effects undefined, and eight four-card tableau columns. Retain the Dusk Dragon encounter and automatic row movement, with discarded row cards visibly animating away at the enemy-to-party handoff. Elemental matching is disabled for 1004; foundation receipts use rank adjacency.

- Scenario 1005 Keru Invoke (Eric, 2026-10-28): a copy of 1004 (Dusk Dragon, Hero as the sole foundation, eight four-card columns, automatic row movement) where Hero’s hand holds Dem, Cir, Mochi, Riv and Banks as invoke cards. Each Keru card stores a tableau value, seeded at random each start and shown as a value chip on the card. Dragging a Keru card onto Hero (or tapping it and choosing Invoke in its enlarged view) swaps that value with Hero’s foundation top value, so the Keru keeps Hero’s previous value. Invoking is not a card play: no chip hit, AP or Momentum. The first invoke each party turn is free; later ones cost 1 Hero AP, blocked with a reason when she has none, and the cards show the current cost. One Keru is on Hero at a time, shown as a badge in her buff row with its buff in the tooltip; a new invoke replaces the previous buff, and re-invoking the same Keru only swaps values. Placeholder buffs until Keru effects are designed: Dem +4 max HP (heals 4 on invoke; HP clamps when removed), Cir +1 chip damage, Mochi +1 chip stagger, Riv +1 AP cap, Banks takes 2 less damage per enemy hit. Rules: rules/keruInvoke.ts; 1004 is unchanged.

- In-battle Keru rarity (Eric, 2026-10-28; scenarios 1006–1010, 1012): Keru cards in Hero’s hand have a rarity tier for this battle only: Common (grey), Rare (blue), Epic (purple), Legendary (orange-gold). Rarity never carries between battles, and Restart resets it. Feeding costs 1 Hero AP per feed: 1 AP reaches Rare, 2 more Epic, 3 more Legendary (6 in all); the card shows its tier and progress pips. Feeding and invoking are separate gestures. Feed (Eric, 2026-10-29, replacing the AP feed coin): drag from Hero’s AP meter itself (its “AP n/cap” label or pip row, always on the board, hand open or not) and hold over a Keru in a familiar square or a Keru card in the open hand; AP pours in about 1 every 300ms while the pointer stays there, each pip committed as it flows (a quick drop pours 1). Pips fly from Hero’s meter into the Keru (static on Low effects) and the target shows a live preview (“+3 AP · Rare in 0” with its tier pips); a tier-up flourish plays the moment a threshold is crossed. Releasing ends the pour; sliding off stops it and keeps what was poured. It stops by itself at 0 AP or Legendary and says why. Tap a Keru square or card for its jumbo, which offers Feed to next tier (N AP, when Hero can pay all of it) and Feed 1 AP; long-press also opens the jumbo. This is the only way to pour: there is no feed coin and no Feed 1 AP hand-card drag. Invoke by dragging the Keru card onto Hero as in 1005. A Keru keeps its tier when swapped off Hero, and feeding the Keru on Hero upgrades her buff at once. Invokes cost a flat 1 AP (no free first invoke). Placeholder tier buffs (Common/Rare/Epic; Legendary keeps Epic’s number and adds its own effect): Dem +4/+8/+12 max HP, healing the gain, Legendary adds a once-per-battle Ward that fully absorbs the next enemy hit; Banks −2/−4/−8 per enemy hit, Legendary first halves the hit and reflects 25% of the prevented damage; Cir +1/+2/+3 chip damage, Legendary adds 1 fire per chip hit; Mochi +1/+2/+3 chip stagger, Legendary lets Hero’s stagger ignore Duskscale Armor and Deep Breath (an exception to the Deep Breath stagger immunity, so the dragon can be broken mid-charge into its backfire); Riv +1 AP every 4/3/2 chip hits, Legendary also +1 AP cap. Presentation: Common plain frame; Rare blue frame and soft shimmer; Epic purple frame and pointer-driven holofoil over the art; Legendary orange-gold frame, animated holofoil and shimmer; a tier-up plays a short evolve flash on the card; Hero’s Keru badge takes the tier colour. Reduced motion and Low effects keep static colours without animation; text stays at least 16px. Each scenario shows its notes panel. Rules: rules/keruInvoke.ts, rules/keruInvokeData.ts; presentation: components/KeruRarity.tsx.
- Scenario 1006 Rarity Showcase (Eric, 2026-10-28): the rarity check room. A harmless endless Training Dummy; Hero starts with 6 AP and refills to her cap as each party turn opens, so every Keru can reach every tier. 1006 also has Keru immediate casts (see Keru immediate casts).
- Scenario 1007 Opening Pull (Eric, 2026-10-28): the Dusk Dragon with 3 opening Hero AP: one medium pull (invoke and feed 2 to Rare) or light pulls (invoke one now, swap later).
- Scenario 1008 Brace for Deep Breath (Eric, 2026-10-28): Deep Breath opens fully charged and lands on the third enemy turn unless pips are removed; Hero starts with 2 AP. Intended answers: Legendary Dem’s Ward, or Legendary Banks (55 → 20); an Epic needs pip removal alongside.
- Scenario 1009 Break the Dragon (Eric, 2026-10-28): the dragon opens charging Deep Breath at 0 pips; Hero starts with 4 AP. Intended path: Legendary Mochi and two Nips to break it mid-charge for the backfire and stun.
- Scenario 1010 AP Engine (Eric, 2026-10-28): the Vault Warden (placeholder art: the Khad Root portrait), 30 HP, strike 2 growing by 1 each enemy turn it acts, Tithe draining 2 Hero AP after each of its turns. Sealed Core: no chip damage until Hero pours 12 AP into the seal within one party turn (drag from Hero’s AP meter and hold over the Warden to pour about 1 AP every 300ms, or Pour 1 AP on its seal meter); a short pour is lost when the turn ends. A broken seal stuns it for 2 enemy turns with double chip damage, then it reseals 4 higher. Scenario rule: Hero’s harness holds three invoked Keru, a fourth replacing the oldest, and her value swaps with the Keru just invoked. Engine kit by tier (Common→Legendary): Riv Generator +1 AP every 4/3/2/1 chip hits; Banks Capacitor +2/+4/+6/+8 AP cap; Cir Multiplier ×1/×1/×2/×2 receipt AP, Legendary refunding 1 AP per 4 poured; Dem and Mochi keep their rarity buffs. Hero starts with 3 AP. Tuning is provisional and not yet simulated.
- Scenario 1012 Keru Upkeep (Eric, 2026-10-28): the Dusk Dragon with Deep Breath; Hero starts with 4 AP. A bigger AP pool balanced by upkeep instead of a load cap: any number of Keru (all five) can be on Hero, invokes cost a flat 1 AP and feeding works as in 1006. At the end of each party turn (so that turn’s AP can pay) each Keru on Hero costs upkeep equal to its tier number (Common 1, Rare 2, Epic 3, Legendary 4), paid oldest first, each while Hero can still afford it; any Keru she can’t pay is dismissed (starved). Hero’s card shows Upkeep N beside her AP readout, each badge shows its own upkeep, and a badge pulses red when this turn’s AP won’t pay it. Dismiss any Keru at any time during the party turn, free, by dragging its badge off Hero (tap fallback: tap the badge and choose Dismiss); it returns to the hand with its tier and value and costs 1 AP to invoke again. Dismissal cooldown (provisional, KERU_DISMISS_COOLDOWN = 2): any dismissed Keru, intentional or starved, can’t be invoked for the rest of the turn it left and the next 2 party turns, and is invokable again as the 3rd party turn opens (starved at the end of turn N: blocked N+1 and N+2, back on N+3). Feeding it meanwhile is allowed. Its hand card is greyed with a cooldown chip (⏳ and the party turns left, at least 16px); tapping or dropping it on Hero is refused with "Back in N turns". Restart clears cooldowns. Farewell, the reverse of a warcry, fires on every dismissal, intentional or starved, scaled by tier number n: Dem heals 3n and leaves a Ward that absorbs the next enemy hit, Banks halves incoming damage, both lasting 2 enemy turns and shown in Hero’s buff row with turns left; Cir bolts the enemy for 4n fire; Mochi adds 2n stagger (a Legendary through Duskscale Armor and Deep Breath, so it can break the dragon mid-charge for the backfire); Riv refunds n + 1 AP at once. A short farewell flourish plays over Hero’s badge row and on the enemy for Cir and Mochi; Low effects and reduced motion keep it static. While on Hero, Dem, Cir and Mochi keep their 1006 rarity buffs; Banks is a Capacitor (AP cap +3/+6/+9/+12) and Riv a Generator (+1 AP every 3/2/1 chip hits; Legendary also +1 AP per stagger break). Intended archetypes: a Machine (Banks and Riv fed until they sustain the upkeep, then Cir and Mochi kept on for steady damage) and a Burst (overspend for one turn, then starve or dismiss right before Deep Breath to chain farewells). No pair synergies in 1012. 1012 also has Keru immediate casts (see Keru immediate casts); its jumbo ladder lists each tier’s cast, and a cast is a third way to spend a Keru beside keeping it and cashing its farewell. Tuning is provisional and not yet simulated. Rules: rules/keruInvoke.ts (upkeepPlan, payKeruUpkeep, dismissKeru); presentation: components/KeruRarity.tsx.
- Keru immediate casts (Eric, 2026-10-29; scenarios 1006 and 1012 for now): each Keru has one cast aimed straight at a target, scaled by tier number n (Common 1 … Legendary 4), costing 0 AP. Riv Surge: target ally gains 2n AP at once (up to their cap). Dem Mend: heal target ally 6n. Cir Flare: 6n fire damage to target enemy. Mochi Pounce: 3n stagger to target enemy (Legendary ignores Duskscale Armor and Deep Breath, like Mochi’s other Legendary effects). Banks Hex: target enemy is Weakened for n enemy turns (its hits deal half damage, rounded up), shown in its status row with turns left. Casting is a true exhaust: the Keru card becomes tapped (turned 90° in the hand, desaturated, labelled Exhausted) for the rest of the battle and can’t be invoked, fed or cast again; Restart resets it. A Keru on Hero (in a square) that casts leaves her at once without its farewell and without a cooldown, and its buff goes. Gesture, distinct from invoke: each Keru card and square carries a small tier-coloured cast gem in its bottom corner; drag the gem to a target to cast (dragging the card body still invokes). Valid targets glow while dragging (allies for Surge and Mend, enemies for the rest), invalid ones dim, and the hovered target names the amount (“+12 HP”). Tap fallback: tap the gem, or the jumbo’s Cast <name> action, then tap a glowing target (Cancel or Escape backs out). The gem tooltip and the jumbo show the amount at the current tier. A short trail runs from the gem to the target as the cast lands (static on Low effects), then the existing hit or heal floater plays. Rules: rules/keruInvoke.ts (castKeru, castReason); data: rules/keruInvokeData.ts (KERU_CASTS); presentation: components/KeruPourCast.tsx.

- Scenario 228 Clearing Floor (Eric, 2026-10-28): a 224 variant filling the circular clearing between the tree props with large, unstacked card tiles and small gaps. Keep tile corners inside the clearing, both actors on their foundation-value start tiles, both 14-card reach rings, occupied-tile refill behavior, and synchronized camera and field input.

- Scenarios 222–227 tableau relief cutout (Eric, 2026-10-28): foreground relief opens over the full projected tableau footprint, including the space between cards, rather than only around individual values. Follow camera movement and the actual ground layout; leave scenery behind the tableau and other combat scenarios unaffected.

- Tiled-floor relief visibility (Eric, 2026-10-28): keep tableau values readable through foreground relief props in the combat camera, following the existing peek-through direction. Preserve the cards’ world positions, reach dimming, occupied tiles and camera input.

- Scenarios 225–227 floor studies (Eric, 2026-10-28): variants of 224 with respectively a close 9×9 grid, a staggered 9×9 floor, and an expansive 11×11 floor with smaller cards. Each actor begins on a tile showing its foundation value, surrounded by tiles; occupied tiles remain until the actor leaves. Both teams retain exactly 14 available cards in their rings, including after movement and redeals. Keep the left field and camera scene synchronized.

- Scenario 224 Tiled Floor (Eric, 2026-10-28): a 223 variant with 40 large, unstacked floor tiles in five rows of eight, separated by consistent tiny gaps. Each team reaches the 14 nearest tile centers within its own ring; taking a tile leaves that card in place beneath the acting team and recenters its ring; neither team can play an occupied tile. Refill the previous tile when its actor leaves, and keep 14 other tiles reachable. Preserve the live field, rank rules, camera card input, and 223 behavior.

- Scenario 223 Stepping Stones (Eric, 2026-10-28): a copy of 222 where Hero's reach is the 14 cards nearest to where she stands on the arena floor, not the end-row rule. Playing a card moves Hero onto that card's spot, recentring her reach; Withdraw falls her back to the player end. Dark Tyrant keeps 222's reach. The arena shows a gold reach ring around Hero and, while a reachable card is hovered, a pale ring where she would stand after playing it. The flat field marks the card she stands on and dims cards outside her reach. Simulation and rationale: docs/research/spatial-tableau-ideas.md.
- Scenario 222 camera tableau input (Eric, 2026-10-28): left clicks select the camera-space tableau cards through the same legal interactions as the original field, with both presentations synchronized. Reserve left click for card input; right drag pans, middle drag orbits, and wheel zooms in the sandbox camera.

- Scenario 222 fourth layer (Eric, 2026-10-28): add a fourth, outermost visual column on each side, five cards each. These outermost cards are inaccessible to both teams, including at their end rows. As cards move inward through the existing refill behavior, apply reach according to their current layer. Preserve the other three layers' access, dimming and foundation placement.

- Combat sandbox camera gestures (Eric, 2026-10-28): keep manual camera movement available on opening and match Proto's gestures: left/right mouse drag pans, middle drag orbits, wheel zooms; one finger pans, two pan and pinch, and three twist/orbit and tilt. Preserve battle camera presets and the other battle camera contexts.

- Scenario 222 three layers and five rows (Eric, 2026-10-28): present three visual columns on each side, five cards per visual column (30 cards total in ten three-card stacks). The inner layer is exposed to both teams throughout. At the player-end row, the player can also take either backing layer, including the new third/outermost column; Dark Tyrant has the corresponding access at the enemy-end row. Other backing cards remain row-inaccessible and dim for the active team. Refills preserve three layers; taking a backing card leaves the inner card untouched, while taking the inner card advances the remaining backing layers inward. Keep foundations above/below and their width equal to the clear central gap. Supersedes the six-row/two-layer 222 layout below.
- Scenario 222 standard ranks (Eric, 2026-10-28): “single value per card” means standard A through K, including 10/J/Q/K, rather than numeric 1–9. Restore standard rank labels (Ace displays A), ordinary A/K wrapping and the full rank range in fresh deals, stock exhaustion and redeals. Keep cards suitless and without alternate/split values. Supersedes every numeric-only/single-digit restriction for 222 below.

- Scenario 222 center gap (Eric, 2026-10-28): move the left and right tableau pairs outward so the clear gap between their innermost card edges equals the width of the centered foundations. Use the same responsive width for that gap and both foundations, accounting for grid gutters. Keep Dark Tyrant above and Hero below the tableau, with all existing layering, reach and dimming intact.

- Scenario 222 foundation placement (Eric, 2026-10-28): center Dark Tyrant above the entire six-row tableau field and Hero below it, rather than between the left/right card pairs. Keep the four visual columns, covered outer layering, end-specific reach, dimming and all battle rules intact. Supersedes the in-between foundation placement for 222 only.

- Scenario 222 six-row end access (Eric, 2026-10-28): reduce Vertical Columns to four visual columns of six cards (24 cards in twelve paired stacks). Both teams retain access to every inner card. Additionally, the player can take either card of the two bottom/player-end pairs, and Dark Tyrant can take either card of the two top/enemy-end pairs. Outer cards at all other rows remain inaccessible to that team. Removing an accessible outer card leaves its inner partner in place and refills behind it; taking an inner card promotes its paired outer card. Enforce this reach in click, keyboard/drag, direct rules, legal-move detection/solver fallback, enemy choices and forced rearm. Dim cards blocked by row for the team whose turn is active, independently of rank adjacency, and keep accessible end cards visually clear. Preserve single Hero, Dark Tyrant, numeric 1–9 values and suitless presentation. Supersedes the seven-row/inner-only 222 trial below.

- Scenario 222 Vertical Columns (Eric, 2026-10-28): add `222: Vertical Columns` to combat sandbox selection with one Hero foundation and the existing Dark Tyrant opponent (999 HP, tableau receipts/Indominable turn behavior). Use plain numeric 1–9 tableau values, no suits, affinities or split ranks. Present 28 cards as four visual columns of seven, two on either side of the centered enemy/player foundations. Internally use fourteen paired stacks: each inner card is the exposed playable top card, with its corresponding outer card behind it at a lower z-layer. Only inner/exposed cards are reachable by either team. Taking an inner card promotes its paired outer card and refills behind it, retaining two cards per pair. Fresh deals, stock exhaustion and Withdraw redeals keep this shape and numeric range. This is an isolated layout experiment; preserve other scenarios and do not add Seed/support foundations to 222.

- S1002 player counterplay on Deep Breath (Eric, 2026-10-27): while Deep Breath is charging, the player may play an exposed adjacent tableau card onto it even when no party foundation accepts that card. Count this receiver in legal-move detection and enable its click/drag target. Reuse the existing intent receipt: reduce pips by one (minimum zero), adopt the card value and grant one Momentum, with no AP or damage. Readied/inhaling abilities and enemy turns remain unavailable. This supersedes S1002's caster-only input restriction; preserve the original 1000 caster-only encounter.

- Scenario 1002 five-Keru foundation trial (Eric, 2026-10-27): start Hero, Dem, Cir, Mochi and Banks, with Hero leading inside the sole Aldan Seed receiver. Give the central foundation the standard 232px desktop width used by other sandbox foundations, flanked by two numbered support cards on each side. On narrow screens stack two supports on either side of the centered receiver. All four supports, including Banks (personal value 9), use the existing exposed ±1 adjacency, wrap and once-per-party-turn tap-in rules; switching leaders swaps the outgoing member into support without extra foundations, receipts or resource refills. This supersedes the four-Keru opening roster and equal-width Seed/support trial. Preserve current portrait reactions, names, value hierarchy and dual leader/Seed HP.

- Deep Breath matching clarity (Eric, 2026-10-27): caster-only opponents show their ability's receiving value rather than an unused personal foundation value. Deep Breath displays its currently adjacent matching ranks, including A/K wrap. It takes up to two legal exposed cards per enemy turn, changing its rank after each receipt, before team4star's automatic row discard. Keep its inspection text live with the charging/readied lifecycle.

- Dem and Cir portrait expressions (Eric, 2026-10-27): use the authored Dem v1 and frontal Cir v2 expression sheets in support and active-leader portraits instead of full-body ability art. Use shared happy idle, sad defeated, upset damage, stars attack and heart-eyes victory reactions; pause reaction timers with combat. Preserve authored paint and alpha, and keep abilities and health unchanged.

- Auto-moving tableau (Eric, 2026-10-27): display Scenario 1002 as `1002: team4star`. After both teams have had a turn on the exposed row, discard one remaining exposed card per main tableau column, promote the row beneath and refill at the back to maintain four cards per column. Advance once when the enemy hands back to the party, after enemy actions; never at the party-to-enemy handoff or after combat ends. Discards grant no receipt effects, AP, damage or Momentum. A pending Withdraw redeal replaces this movement rather than discarding an extra fresh row. Other scenarios retain their tableau behavior.

- teamfourstar (Eric, 2026-10-27): rename the Scenario 1002 display name to `teamfourstar`, retaining its stable internal ID and links. Replace the endless Training Khad Root with the existing Dusk Dragon encounter opponent (currently authored in 1000): 100 HP, no basic attack or Momentum, Duskscale Armor and caster-built, readied Deep Breath. Keep the seven-column four-Keru Seed setup and its presentation rules.

- Scenario 1002 plain tableau values (Eric, 2026-10-27): remove elemental artwork, glyphs and affinity labels from tableau cards in this scenario, including covered and exposed values. Keep ranks and card interactions. This supersedes the elemental value backsplash presentation trial for 1002; underlying receipt rules remain unchanged. Other scenarios retain elemental presentation.

- Leader-only abilities and direct support swaps (Eric, 2026-10-27): in Seed expeditions only the current leader may use active Keru abilities. The central foundation opens that leader's prepared hand; non-leader cards never open an ability hand. Tapping a living, available support swaps it with the leader only when the existing exposed-card adjacency and once-per-party-turn rules permit it. Keep pause, commitment, cooldown, exhaustion and health restrictions. A swap closes stale ability UI and costs no AP or cards. The leader spends the Seed's existing AP bank without transferring or resetting anyone's AP, HP, shield or personal value. Enforce ownership in casting rules as well as the UI; preserve legacy encounters without leadership. Prepared summon/invocation cards remain abilities of their authored commanding Keru; leading Cir can use her own prepared kit but cannot simultaneously be invoked/familiarized. This supersedes support clicks opening Hero's collected hand.

- Scenario 1002 names and value hierarchy (Eric, 2026-10-27): make the Keru name the topmost element of both support and leader cards, above the portrait and value. Dim non-leader personal tableau values while keeping the active leader/Seed receiver value bright. This supersedes placing names beside the bottom HP readout; keep the current health, portrait and input behavior.

- Scenario 1002 seventh tableau (Eric, 2026-10-27): deal seven tableau columns, each four cards deep, using the existing elemental deal and receipt rules. The seventh column is fully playable and participates in the same layout, targeting and leadership adjacency checks as the others. Other scenario column counts remain unchanged.

- Leader portrait foundation (Eric, 2026-10-27): rebuild the Aldan Seed's active foundation with the same portrait/name/value presentation and card size as its Keru supports. Show the current leader's portrait and name in place of the Seed sprout artwork and update them immediately when leadership changes. Retain the sole Seed receiver, its live tableau value/AP and separate protected leader HP and Seed HP bars. The Seed health label continues to identify its expedition role.

- Keru combat max HP (Eric, 2026-10-27): default Dem to 40 max HP, Hero to 32, Mochi to 22 and Cir to 25. Fresh actors start at their own maximum. Scenario 1002 uses these individual values instead of its former blanket 40-HP party override; the Aldan Seed remains at 40 HP. Preserve explicit HP overrides in other authored training scenarios.

- Scenario 1002 portrait emphasis (Eric, 2026-10-27): omit this scenario's insight/notes panel. Place each support Keru's name directly above its HP bar near the bottom of the card, using the freed upper area for a larger portrait. Keep personal tableau values clear, maintain equal support/Seed heights and preserve card access and health behavior. Other scenarios retain their insight panels.

- Scenario 1002 party presentation (Eric, 2026-10-27): omit the visible Supporting Keru label and standalone Tap in buttons on support cards. Display each support's live personal tableau value by default; this does not create additional receipt foundations. Center the Aldan Seed primary foundation and give it the same card height as supports while retaining both protected leader HP and Seed HP. Remove the bottom element-charge pip strip and Withdraw button in this scenario; preserve underlying abilities, charge rules and inspection-based leadership actions.

- Leader protection and dual HP (Eric, 2026-10-27): a Keru in the active leader position with the Aldan Seed cannot take direct damage; the Seed receives that hit using its own remaining temporary shield and HP. Preserve the leader's personal HP and temporary shield rather than healing, merging or replacing either health pool. Protection follows leadership immediately; the outgoing Keru is a vulnerable support again. Party-wide attacks damage the Seed once, skip the sheltered leader, and still hit other living supports independently. Apply receiver routing to direct attacks, reflections, enemy strikes and party-wide ability damage, with events/previews identifying the actual recipient. Show both the current leader's labeled, protected HP bar and the Aldan Seed HP bar on the active foundation; update the leader bar when leadership changes. Seed destruction remains expedition loss. Legacy battles without a Seed retain individual damage targeting.

- Keru personal values and leader tap-in (Eric, 2026-10-27): each Keru has a personal tableau value. Provisional defaults are Hero 2, Mochi 5, Banks 9, Cir 7, Dem A and Riv J. In Scenario 1002, a collected, available Keru may tap in as leader when its personal value is ±1 of any exposed tableau card, including A/K wrap. This swaps places with the current leader: the Aldan Seed remains the sole friendly tableau receiver and expedition HP owner, displaying the incoming leader's value. It does not add another foundation. The outgoing leader retains its last played value, and actual Seed receipts update the current leader's personal value. One tap-in total per party turn, resetting at the next party-turn opening; no AP cost, card consumption, receipt effects, free damage, healing or resource refill from the swap itself. Keep Seed HP/AP/shield, target and party resources intact. Committed/recovering/exhausted Cir cannot tap in, and leading Cir cannot simultaneously be invoked or familiarized. Other Keru commitment rules remain bespoke. Card inspection offers Tap in when legal; support tiles permit returning a former leader. Show personal values, active leader and ready/spent allowance.
- Keru value persistence boundary (Eric, 2026-10-27): outside combatsbx, personal values and leader belong to expedition state and carry between encounters. Use the JSON-safe `KeruExpedition` record, `keruExpeditionSnapshot`, and the `BattleSetup.keruExpedition` encounter input; `ProtoState.keruExpedition` and the world combat view's save callback carry this record without restarting a mounted encounter. Seed encounters that opt into this record restore the saved leader/value. Sandbox scenario creation/restart deliberately uses fresh defaults and does not write to expedition state. Turn allowance and in-encounter card piles are not persisted as personal-value save data. Existing legacy world encounters without an expedition record remain unmigrated; this contract does not introduce a disk-save system or Seed HP persistence.

- Scenario 1002 opening party (Eric, 2026-10-27): start with Hero, Dem, Cir and Mochi. Hero leads inside the Aldan Seed foundation; Dem, Cir and Mochi begin as supports. Keep the Seed as the sole tableau receiver and expedition HP owner. Supersedes the Hero-only opening party for this scenario.

- Aldan Seed artwork (Eric, 2026-10-27): use the transparent authored golden two-leaf sprout following ART.md in place of the temporary foundation diamond emblem. This replaces the earlier temporary-art note only; Seed gameplay rules remain in effect.

- Aldan Seed expedition cornerstone (Eric, 2026-10-27): the expedition foundation is the Aldan Seed, not Hero, Mochi or another Keru. It owns its own tableau value and HP independently of party composition. Destruction of the Seed ends the expedition even if Keru survive; individual Keru losses do not themselves imply game over while the Seed survives. Many enemy attacks should pressure Seed HP; specialized attacks may threaten Keru separately. This is the standing design direction, analogous to a vessel whose crew can be lost without immediate loss of the expedition. Keru costs, cooldowns, activation and support capabilities remain bespoke.
- Aldan Seed first implementation (Eric, 2026-10-27): Scenario 1002 begins with a distinct `aldan-seed` foundation and non-foundational Hero support. Only the Seed accepts party tableau receipts; it retains rank/cards independently of the supporting Keru and banks the existing receipt AP/Momentum. Provisional tuning reuses 40 HP, rank 2, AP cap 6 and a 1-damage receipt pulse, without establishing a permanent balance rule. Ordinary enemy strikes target the Seed instead of the highest-AP Keru. Seed destruction takes precedence over victory and cannot be undone by endless-wave settlement. The Seed opens the collected Keru hand (Hero support can also open it); Cir's attachment consumes Seed AP and uses a separate familiar square beside the Seed, retaining her current bespoke charge/shield rules. Immediate Burn/Hearthflame and Dem/Riv placeholders remain as previously wired. Seed artwork is an explicitly temporary emblem. Older scenarios and the exploration/world-map/save pipeline remain legacy scaffolding pending migration; cross-encounter Seed HP persistence, leader selection, substitutions and specialized enemy targeting are not yet implemented. Supersedes Hero-as-foundation/sole-receiver rules for 1002 below.

- Keru card art framing (Eric, 2026-10-27): zoom Cir's existing ability/holocard subject so her face is central and the full wingspan is cropped. Keep authored source art, painted windstorm, card motion and reduced-motion presentation. Scenario 1002's Dem placeholder uses the existing approved Dem storybook holo subject and separate painted background. This is an art-only pass; leader/support/backup roster proposals remain discussion, and Cir's invocation/cooldown/activation rules are bespoke mockups rather than universal Keru expressions.

- Elemental value backsplash trial (Eric, 2026-10-27): Scenario 1002 paints each element into the dark backing behind the live tableau value rather than around the card perimeter. Use separate authored compact and large plaques for covered header values and exposed centered values. Keep cream ranks as live text, the center dark and uncluttered, and preserve affinity labels, receipt rules and card input. Retain previous corner artwork as alternate sources.


- Scenario 1002 target-driven Keru cards (Eric, 2026-10-27): Hero's Keru cards offer immediate target use or familiar placement, never direct foundation creation/promotion. Name the prepared cards Cir, Dem and Riv; Dem/Riv remain visible, liftable/inspectable placeholders with no casts until their bespoke effects are defined. Cir on an enemy casts the existing Burn (3 Fire damage, seeded 25% Burning chance for 3 enemy turns); Cir on an ally foundation casts Hearthflame (+3 AP subject to the recipient's normal cap). Both immediate uses cost 0 AP and set a shared 3-party-turn-opening Cir cooldown. A separate empty familiar square appears beside each eligible living ally while aiming a Keru and disappears on cancel/cast. Cir on this square costs the existing 1 Hero AP and attaches dormant with zero pips/activation; preserve HP, Wings and remaining shield, with no healing or shield refill. Receipt charging remains +1, or +2 total Fire/Air, awakening at 3. Card copy/cost previews explain the hovered target's outcome. Remove Emerge from Hero's hand and reject its cast; retain standalone Cir ability scaffolding without a direct summon path. Supersedes prior ally-foundation attachment, Hero Emerge and Dem/Riv direct summoning in 1002 only; legacy scenarios retain their summons. Rarity remains deferred.

- Full collected Keru hand in Scenario 1002 (Eric, 2026-10-27): no pre-battle roster selection, two-slot selection limit or separate roster panel. Once Hero is clickable, her ability deck exposes all currently prepared collected Keru: Cir, Dem and Riv. Choose their use directly through the existing ability targeting/familiarization/foundation paths; preserve AP costs, activation, cooldown, exhaustion and duplicate prevention. Hero remains the only opening foundation. Supersedes the selected-roster setup and outset selection requirements below. Rarity remains documented exploration only; do not implement rarity yet.

- Scenario 1002 Keru progression first pass (Eric, 2026-10-27): implement a provisional two-slot battle selection from a temporary collected roster of Cir, Dem and Riv, defaulting to Cir/Riv. The roster UI permits party-turn selection changes; active familiars/foundations and charging Meteor occupy their selected slot until released/withdrawn (Meteor cannot be recalled). Dem/Riv retain existing foundation summon roles and normal AP costs; withdrawal stores their exact actor state and returning does not heal, refill or reset their foundation. A host carrying Cir must release her before withdrawal. This is prototype collection scaffolding, not an authored collection-unlock rule.
- Cir invocation/activation first pass (Eric, 2026-10-27): dropping Cir on a living enemy costs 0 AP for provisional 3 Fire damage and locks all her expressions for three subsequent party-turn openings. Recovery advances even while unselected and cannot be reset by selection changes. Dropping her on a living ally costs 1 Hero AP and attaches her dormant, with no affinity, shield or familiar damage benefit until activation. Actual host tableau receipts grant 1 pip, or 2 total for Fire/Air, capped at the provisional 3-pip activation threshold. Activation persists for the battle, granting Fire/Air affinity, the remaining 5-point fiery temporary shield, and +1 Fire damage to the host's target on Fire receipts (including an awakening Fire receipt). Active Cir can Emerge for 0 AP via Hero's hand, leaving her host for her own existing Fire/Air foundation and abilities, preserving HP/Wings and remaining shield without an AP refill. Release/reassignment preserves activation/pips and never refills shield or heals. Supersedes immediate familiar benefits, Fire-only host affinity, undefined familiar charging and inaccessible foundation progression above/below. Skill trees, rarity/AP bidding, empowered exhausting invocation and Dem Frost Armor remain exploratory, not implemented in this pass.

- Keru collection and battle selection (Eric, 2026-10-27): the game is a creature collector. Players have access to their full collected Keru roster, choose a few Keru at battle outset, and may change tactics by changing their selected Keru mid-fight. Do not assume a permanently restricted equipped roster. Initial selection count, replacement timing/costs and the treatment of outgoing Keru remain undefined; preserve meaningful expression commitments when defining these rules.

- Familiar receipt charging direction (Eric, 2026-10-27): to prevent a familiar from becoming useless on a board with few matching elements, successful tableau receipts by its host grant 1 pip, or 2 total pips when the received card matches the familiar's elemental affinities. Matching is a bonus rather than a prerequisite for charge. Activation thresholds, capacity, spending and applicability to individual bespoke Keru remain to be tuned; this does not define ability-generated receipt triggers or implement gameplay changes.

- Keru rarity exploration (Eric, 2026-10-27): retain for future ability implementation and balance consideration a Keru rarity system, potentially allowing the player to feed a chosen amount of AP into a summon before arrival to increase the chance of a higher-rarity expression of that same Keru. This is an exploratory design direction, not an implemented or finalized mechanic; rarity effects, probabilities, AP commitments, duration/persistence and interaction with familiarization, invocation and foundation emergence remain undecided. Evaluate alternatives against the preference for a smaller roster of bespoke, mostly fixed Keru kits and light pre-battle configuration rather than extensive skill-tree/build management.

- Restrained corners for every element (Eric, 2026-10-27): extend Nature v4 sizing to Fire, Water, Air, Earth and Chaos. All six select v4 transparent overlays with two modest upper-right/lower-left accents of comparable visual weight, distinct non-mirrored shapes and open centers; remove the dominant lower-left mass and extra side accent. Preserve rank layout, accessible affinities, covered-card orientation and interactions. Supersedes the v3 selection and Nature-only sizing limitation.


- Nature corner restraint (Eric, 2026-10-27): use Nature v4 with two small, similarly sized upper-right and lower-left leaf sprigs, using the smaller prior accent as the size baseline. Paint distinct arrangements rather than mirrored copies; remove the dominant corner mass and extra side accent. Other elements remain v3.


- Soft elemental adornments (Eric, 2026-10-27): Scenario 1002 uses the v3 asymmetric corner overlays for Nature, Fire, Water, Air, Earth and Chaos, with wider brush strokes, reduced detail and softer outer edges. Keep ranks, accessible affinity names, buried-card orientation and input behavior intact. Full-frame variants remain source studies; see docs/art/card-adornments-v3.md.


- Familiar resource presentation (Eric, 2026-10-27): familiarized Cir cannot store AP; display a separate pip counter along the bottom of her tile. Pip earning/spending rules remain undefined. The initial six empty visual slots are temporary mockup scaffolding, not a gameplay capacity ruling.

- Familiar tile top alignment (Eric, 2026-10-27): top-align attached Keru squares with their host foundation, superseding vertical centering. Preserve host dimensions and half-height square sizing.

- Keru host sizing and Cir card copy (Eric, 2026-10-27): adding a Keru square must preserve the host foundation dimensions. Cir’s card description reads `Invoke Cir` and its footer reads `Fire / Air`; preserve attachment mechanics.

- Cir Keru tile expression (Eric, 2026-10-27): use Cir’s frontal expression artwork for her attached Keru tile, with neutral as the default, instead of her full-body key art. Preserve the shared holocard artwork and attachment interactions.

- Covered tableau perimeter orientation (Eric, 2026-10-27): flip the painted perimeter overlay both vertically and horizontally on cards covered by another card so the heaviest clusters appear in the upper-right corner of the visible top strip. Uncovered top-row cards retain their original art orientation; keep ranks and interactions unchanged.

- Elemental perimeter art (Eric, 2026-10-27): replace the Nature, Fire, Water, Air and Earth code-drawn adornments with transparent authored watercolor paintings following ART.md. Each painting must be asymmetric, with unequal disconnected clusters and substantial open gaps rather than a uniform frame or matching corners. Preserve readable ranks, overlapping previews, accessible suit names and existing card interactions. Supersedes the Nature-first perimeter study's restriction of other elements to future work.

- Codex automation default (Eric, 2026-10-27): use the `codex --full-auto` equivalent for agentic work in this folder: `approval_policy = "on-request"` and `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"`, persisted in `.codex/config.toml`. Complete authorized work autonomously; runtime sandbox boundaries and required escalation approvals still apply. Explicit launch settings or managed policies may override project defaults.

- Expression emoji orientation (Eric, 2026-10-27): facial expression emojis face the camera directly, with both eyes equally visible and the face centered rather than in profile or three-quarter view. Preserve Cir's approved first expression sheet as an alternate art set when authoring the frontal version.

## Standing browser validation limits (Eric, 2026-10-27)

- For each user-requested task, browser validation is limited to one initial run and one corrective rerun, with a maximum of five minutes total elapsed browser-validation work, whichever limit is reached first. Include browser setup, harness/environment debugging, screenshot review and retries in that time. Do not reset the budget by switching scripts, browsers, viewports, subtasks or conversation turns.
- Keep the initial check focused on the changed behavior. A corrective rerun checks only the affected screen or interaction; do not repeat an entire viewport matrix or already-passing checks without a concrete reason and within the same budget.
- Stop browser validation sooner if the harness or environment becomes the problem. Do not turn feature verification into an open-ended tooling repair task.
- At a limit or an early stop, stop the browser test and its task-owned helpers, preserve completed changes, and report what passed, what remains unverified, and the blocker. Wait for explicit user authorization before continuing browser validation or repairing its tooling. Do not claim full verification, broaden testing, or substitute another expensive validation loop to bypass the stop.
- These limits take precedence over older requirements for exhaustive viewport checks, repeated visual polish, zoomed screenshots, or adding new browser checks when completing those requirements would exceed the budget. Report omitted checks plainly. Routine completion instructions do not authorize exceeding these limits.
- These are standing agent workflow limits, not guaranteed billing cutoffs. Do not promise an exact dollar or subscription-percentage cap. Where supported, a user-requested token-budgeted Goal is a separate budget mechanism; do not create one without the user's request.

## Gameplay and presentation rulings

- Keru attachment default (Eric, 2026-10-27): Scenario 1002 uses existing actor foundations as the default Keru hosts. Cir costs 1 Hero AP to attach to a living ally; she cannot immediately create her own foundation. Show the familiar as a square tile immediately left of its connected foundation, vertically centered, with the same border treatment and half the foundation height, instead of a buff icon. Preserve familiar effects, inspection/release and persistent identity/resources. Independent foundation progression remains undefined; retain existing standalone Cir abilities as scaffolding. Supersedes the immediate Cir summon path in the earlier first pass; legacy scenario summons remain unchanged.

- Scenario 1002 Hero hand (Eric, 2026-10-27): Hero has only Cir. Remove Hold and all UX sample cards from her hand; Summon: Dem is not available in 1002. Preserve Cir’s own abilities and the team Withdraw control. Supersedes earlier 1002 hand scaffolding.

- Summon target alignment (Eric, 2026-10-27): the adjacent new-foundation drop target aligns vertically with existing actor foundations, including their bottom edge; status/buff rows above an actor must not lift the empty target above the foundation row. Preserve shared summon targeting and responsive layout.

- Cir windstorm card art (Eric, 2026-10-27): add separate rear/front painted windstorm planes around Cir’s shared first-frame holocard art, following the card’s parallax at different depths. Preserve existing character art, inspection/targeting, reduced-motion stills and all ability mechanics. See ART.md for overlay assets.

- Cir companion first pass (Eric, 2026-10-27): in Scenario 1002 replace the 0 AP TBD draft with a functional Cir card costing 1 Hero AP. Drop on an ally for a familiar or the adjacent new-foundation slot for a summon. Cir is one persistent battle entity: preserve HP, AP, Wings, foundation and remaining familiar shield when released; no duplicate, healing or shield refill from reassignment. Familiar grants a separately tracked 5 temporary HP fiery extension, Fire affinity and +1 Fire damage to the host’s selected enemy on Fire tableau receipts. Damage consumes temporary HP first; normal healing never restores it. Drag the familiar icon off its host to release, with an explicit inspection action for keyboard/touch. Hero begins with no affinity. Summoned Cir is Fire/Air, provisional 12 HP, 10 AP cap, starts with 1 Swift Wings stack; first card must be Fire/Air at any rank, then normal ±1 matching. Cir has her own once-per-party-turn matching-element bridge, separate from the team bridge. Each receipt grants normal AP/Momentum and Fire chip damage. Swift Wings replaces Rising Flight: self-cast costs the resulting stack count (1/2/3/4 AP), max 4; 15% evasion per stack, base Air damage × stacks (minimum ×1). Air receipts preserve Wings; at 3+ stacks Fire receipts also preserve; all others remove one stack per receipt, checked before loss. No passive stack gain. Burn: 1 AP, 3 Fire damage, seeded 25% chance of receipt-triggered Burning (1 extra damage whenever afflicted creature receives a Fire tableau card; refresh without stacking, provisional 3 enemy turns). Keep legacy timed Burn distinct. Flare: 1 AP, 1 Fire damage, then Firelit until party-turn end (+10 percentage points crit chance, ×2 subsequent direct-hit stagger, nonstacking; provisional crit ×2). Windstorm replaces Firestorm: spends all Cir AP (minimum 1), deals (2 × AP spent + all Air tableau cards including buried cards) × Wings multiplier as Air damage to every enemy, snapshot on cast; excludes stock/foundations. At 4 Wings, 0 AP Meteor Ascend consumes Wings and replaces Cir with an untargetable ability foundation accepting no cards; +1 pip each subsequent party-turn opening, ready at 3. Ready Meteor is draggable from Cir’s hand onto an enemy for 22 base Fire AOE, exhausting Cir for this battle. No added PvP interruption system in this pass. Preserve legacy Cir tutorial expressions outside 1002. Supersedes Cir’s draft-only rule and Hero’s default Fire affinity.

- Cir entity first frame (Eric, 2026-10-27): use the first Gather frame (index 0) of the authored dive sheet for Cir’s shared entity/holocard art, including the Scenario 1002 Cir card. This replaces the selected Unfurl portrait and preserves shared layered effects. This is an artwork selection only; ability mechanics remain independently authored.

- Ability/holocard component parity (Eric, 2026-10-27): ability faces inherit TradingCard’s spring tilt, pointer/touch/device motion, foil/glare shimmer and copy depth, using the shared LayeredCardArt renderer for separate scenery and subjects. Enable these properties on hand drafts and jumbo inspection as well as authored abilities; keep hit surfaces, tap-to-jumbo, hold targeting and paid actions independent from the moving face. Preserve static reduced-motion views and stable noninteractive drag/cast clones. Supersedes ability-only reductions to the shared foil and motion presentation.

- Ability hold targeting (Eric, 2026-10-27): press-and-hold lifts an ability card from the hand and suppresses the radial/backdrop so the board stays visible while aiming. Keep pointer capture on the stable overlay for mouse/touch, tap-to-jumbo, release on legal targets for authored abilities, and Escape/cancel/invalid-drop return to the hand. Draft cards, including Cir in 1002, may be lifted to preview potential board actors/cards but have no cast or resource effect until defined. Unaffordable abilities remain liftable for aiming but cannot cast. This supersedes draft cards being inspection-only.

- Ability tap inspection (Eric, 2026-10-27): tapping/clicking an ability in the hand opens its jumbo holocard, including unavailable abilities and Scenario 1002 draft/mock cards. Enter/Space does the same. Available abilities offer an explicit Choose target action in jumbo; hold-drag casting stays available. Inspection alone never spends resources or casts. Closing jumbo returns to the same hand and focus; Escape closes jumbo before the hand. Retain pause and visible board values.

- Cir still/card correction (Eric, 2026-10-27): the requested dive was a still pose, not a standing animation requirement. Use the selected Unfurl frame as Cir's shared holocard art. In Scenario 1002 replace the Ember Step UX sample with a distinct Cir draft card costing 0 Hero AP and description exactly `TBD`. Its effect is undefined; do not bind it to an existing Cir ability, spend AP or resolve a cast. Keep the four remaining UX samples and Summon: Dem. The earlier animation study remains historical scaffolding.

- Cir dive animation study (Eric, 2026-10-27): prototype the accepted storybook Cir as a wing-folding meteor dive in `?cirsbx`, available from both main and Proto entry points and linked from Cir’s gallery. Keep this art review separate from combat timing and ability effects. Include pause/replay, speed, manual pose/progress review, transparent-background review and reduced-motion stills. See ART.md for assets and medium.

- Ability fan refinement (Eric, 2026-10-27): never render scrollbars in the ability overlay, while retaining touch/keyboard overflow access. Use a stronger fan angle, allow lower card tips to overlap, and keep the centermost cards highest in each row, with the front row above the rear. Preserve readable rules text and foundation centering. Supersedes the prior fully separated card-corner geometry.

- Ability hand UX prototype (Eric, 2026-10-27): center the ability fan above the clicked foundation, including a single card directly above its owner; clamp to viewport edges only when needed. Space full card faces so adjacent cards do not cover any text. When width cannot fit the hand, use a second rear/upper fan row, with horizontal panning at very narrow widths rather than shrinking text beyond readability. Preserve real card costs, drag/tap target selection, keyboard navigation, Escape and pause. S1002 temporarily shows Summon: Dem plus five explicitly labeled non-spending, non-casting faux ability cards for UX review; these are not authored gameplay abilities.

- Element suit identities and art (Eric, 2026-10-27): replace the Light tableau suit with Nature and Dark with Chaos, retaining six equally distributed seeded suits and the current receipt/element-bridge rules. This renames suits only, not other ability/actor elements. Use authored transparent watercolor suit sprites matching the Momentum icon medium, with distinct Earth, Air, Fire, Water, Nature and Chaos silhouettes on tableau cards and foundation element markers. Preserve accessible element names, rank readability and compact overlapping previews.

- Scenario 1002 hand and Clover (Eric, 2026-10-27): enable foundation-click ability radials and put Summon: Dem (6 Hero AP, adjacent new-foundation slot, Ace start) in Hero’s default hand. Hero remains the only starting foundation. Rename the stock-flip buff to “4 Leaf Clover”, retaining its stable internal status ID and mechanics, but do not grant it by default in 1002. Other scenarios retain their existing starting charges.

- Scenario 1002 solo setup (Eric, 2026-10-27): begin with Hero as the only friendly foundation and six tableau columns. Keep four-card depth, elemental suits, the once-per-party-turn element bridge, fresh deals and the Training Khad Root encounter. Supersedes the initial Hero/Mochi five-column setup below; historical two-foundation simulation figures are not measurements of this solo variant.

- Scenario 1002 Elemental Suits (Eric, 2026-10-26; from docs/research/suited-cards-montecarlo.md): every tableau card has one of six elements (earth, air, fire, water, dark, light; opposed pairs earth–air, fire–water, dark–light, display only), fixed by the card’s seeded stock identity. Hero is fire and Mochi earth. Normal ±1 golf with A/K wrap is unchanged; once per party turn, team-wide, a card of a Kin’s element may play on that Kin at any rank, and the foundation takes its rank while keeping the Kin’s element. It is a normal receipt (hit, +1 AP, +1 Momentum). A card that is also ±1 legal is a normal play and keeps the charge, which resets as each party turn opens; Withdraw still ends the turn. Hero and Mochi on 5 columns × 4 deep with the usual refill, against the endless Training Khad Root (008’s chip/stagger strikes), with a fresh deal each start. Cards show an element glyph on its colour, Kin foundations show their element, and one pip beside End Turn shows the element play ready or spent. Rules live in rules/elementSuits.ts.

- Split tableau cards in 1000 (Eric, 2026-10-26): each tableau card is diagonally divided, upper-left primary rank and lower-right alternate rank. Generate distinct, nonadjacent rank pairs, including excluding A/K; both values count for legality. A card is one receipt, never two sequential plays. Resolve automatically when exactly one legal rank/target outcome exists; preserve existing target-foundation choice and ask for a value when both values produce different legal results on the selected foundation. Store only the chosen rank on receipt. Riv’s wildcard starter follows the same choice rule. Enemy Deep Breath considers both values and selects seeded legal outcomes. Keep three overlapping rows and six compact columns, but restore exposed-card-only play instead of two-deep access, with normal uncovering/backfill. Covered cards show compact previews of both values. Keep pause visibility, mouse/touch/keyboard choices, drag behavior, AP/Momentum accounting and enemy pacing. Scenario 1001 retains its single-value comparisons. This supersedes the two-deep/inward-backfill rules for 1000.

- Riv and foundation-creating Keru (Eric, 2026-10-26): Riv is a female Keru placeholder with no art. Summon: Riv costs 1 Hero AP and creates her own friendly foundation, initially empty/wild: it accepts any first reachable tableau card, then uses that card’s rank and normal matching. Provisional 12 HP and 6 AP cap; unique actor, no duplicate summon while present or downed. Add her to the Hero hand in 1000/1001 and the ability playground. Global foundation-creator targeting: arming or dragging a foundation-creating Keru reveals an adjacent new-foundation slot. Drop or confirm there to summon. Existing foundations are not valid targets for the summon path; their separate effects remain undefined and do not spend AP. Apply this rule to Summon: Dem as well (6 AP, Ace start). Keep slot cleanup on cancel, impact and restart, and preserve keyboard/tap target confirmation, pause, card-flight presentation and normal receipt rules.

- Combat tableau visibility during holds (Eric, 2026-10-26): keep tableau, held-card and ability-foundation values visible during pause, tooltip inspection, ability menus and tactical/drag holds. Preserve simulation freezes and input guards. This supersedes prior combat pause-value concealment requirements.

- Scenario 1000 layered two-deep presentation (Eric, 2026-10-26): display its three rows as six standard vertically overlapping tableau columns, with enemy foundations above and friendly foundations below. Retain two-deep reach (party depths 1/2, enemy depths 0/1) and taker-side inward frontier backfill. Covered frontier card headers remain clickable and draggable when legal. Do not use separated spatial lanes or foundation underlap for 1000. Scenario 1001 retains its own layouts. This supersedes only the presentation of the preceding inward-frontier rule.

- Scenario 1000 inward frontier (Eric, 2026-10-26): use three rows of six cards. Hero/Dem reach party rear and shared middle frontier; Dusk Dragon/Deep Breath reach dragon rear and frontier. Taking a frontier card pulls the taking team’s rear card in the same column into the frontier, then draws a stock card into that rear. The opposing rear remains intact. Taking a rear card refills that cell directly. Preserve compact spatial layout and foundation layering, all encounter abilities and guards, and existing paced enemy actions/card flights. Scenario 1001 retains its independent-cell comparison rules. This supersedes the preceding standard-tableau restoration for 1000.

- Scenario 1000 standard tableau restoration (Eric, 2026-10-26): use the standard six-column, four-row overlapping tableau, with only each column’s exposed card available to either team and normal uncovering/backfill. Enemy foundations are above the rack and friendly foundations below it; no private lanes or underlap in 1000. Preserve the Dusk Dragon encounter, Summon: Dem, Every Dog Has Her Day, enemy pacing, camera controls and Space end turn. Scenario 1001 retains the spatial layout experiments. This supersedes the spatial tableau and foundation underlap rules for 1000.

- Combat sandbox Space shortcut (Eric, 2026-10-26): Space ends the party turn through the End Turn control, replacing its former pause binding. Ignore held-key repeats and text editing, preserve normal availability/pause/inspection guards, and retain onscreen pause controls.

- Scenario 1001 Layout Laboratory (Eric, 2026-10-26): six top tabs compare Two-deep reach, Reach by position, Fanned stacks, Contested frontier, Reserve hand and Rotating rows. Switching tabs restarts the same authored three-lane example deal with seed 1001, 3 Hero AP, 3 Momentum and the same stock sequence. Two-deep gives the dragon its rear only; Contested shares frontier access. Position gives the party frontier reach within one column of Hero, with bounded left/right shifts costing 1 Momentum per column and matching arena position. Fanned pairs expose both lower-lane cards; the frontier/back card costs 1 AP before earning its normal receipt. Reserve restricts party tableau reach to its rear and gives Hero three rank-matching stock cards, consumed when played and refreshed (discarding leftovers) each party turn. Rotating cycles [dragon rear, frontier, party rear] to [frontier, party rear, dragon rear] when the party hands off, with independent cell backfill between rotations. Keep baseline 1000 unchanged, labels explicit, tab keyboard access, pause/inspection, enemy pacing, and restart cleanup. Presentation rework (Eric, 2026-10-26): the authored opening (Hero on 2, dragon on 9) must make each tab's rule visibly change the playable set: Two-deep lights a party-rear 3 and frontier A and 3; Position lights only the A in Hero's window and leaves a frontier 3 two columns away worth shifting for; Reserve lights the rear 3 and the reserve 3; Contested adds a frontier 10 for the dragon. In the lab, cards Hero can play now carry a gold rim and lift, cards out of her reach under the tab are dimmed, and cells the dragon can also take carry a plum top edge. Lane labels sit against the rack's left edge and state who reaches each lane (Rotating adds each lane's next role). Fanned keeps the back (frontier) card's rank and 1 AP tag in the exposed strip. Reserve cards sit beside the rack at full card size without moving Hero. In 1001 the Insight panel replaces Intent / How to solve it / Why it exists with only the selected layout's pros and cons, updating as the layout tab changes. These variants and starting resources are temporary comparison scaffolding, not validated balance settings.

- Spatial tableau layering (Eric, 2026-10-25): keep friendly/enemy foundations and their buffs above tableau cards. Compact the stationary layout by allowing approximately one third of each adjacent card to underlap foundations: dragon rear below the dragon, frontier below the party, and party rear below the party. Do not cover the shared frontier from both ends. Keep values within the visible portion, preserve compact aligned columns, and let exposed card areas remain clickable/draggable while foundations own their overlapping hit areas. Inspection callouts and drag/flight overlays retain their existing overlay levels.

- Stationary spatial tableau prototype (Eric, 2026-10-25): scenario 1000 uses three explicit face-up lanes of six compact, horizontally adjacent columns, aligned across lanes. Place the Dusk Dragon foundation between the dragon rear and shared frontier, and the friendly foundation row between the shared frontier and party rear. Hero and summoned Dem can receive cards from frontier/rear; Deep Breath can receive from dragon rear/frontier. Each receipt refills only its own cell from the stock stream, without shifting any other lane. Keep enemy card-by-card pacing and flights, normal AP/Momentum/basic hits, buffs, summon, pause/inspection and reduced motion. No movement is added. Camera orbit/pan/zoom remains independent of gameplay; expose Copy camera data immediately right of Camera controls. Party Withdraw remains a delayed end-turn fallback refreshing frontier and party rear while preserving dragon rear. Older comparison scenarios retain their original tableau. Spatial solver tooling uses a legal reachable-cell step. This prototype supersedes 1000's four-deep overlapping tableau; do not apply prior Monte Carlo percentages to it as measured results.

- Temporary Summon: Dem (Eric, 2026-10-25): rework Hero’s temporary Hold ability as Summon: Dem (stable internal ability ID `hold`). Keep its 6 Hero AP cost, self-cast and once-per-fight limit. It adds Dem immediately after Hero in the friendly foundation row, where the temporary Hold slot was scaffolded, starting at Ace with an Ace foundation card. Dem is a normal friendly tableau receiver with AP, targeting and basic chip hits, and appears with their existing painted portrait and directional arena rig. Use provisional 12 HP and 6 AP cap; no proposed Dem spell kit is implemented. A downed Dem still prevents resummoning. Restart removes the summon. Preserve legacy scenario-authored hold-slot experiments (023/024); Summon: Dem does not open a Hold slot. Supersedes the earlier 1000 Hold ability requirement.

- Every Dog Has Her Day (Eric, 2026-10-25): Hero begins each combatsbx fight with this unique buff at three charges. Clicking it on a party turn spends one charge and flips a stock card onto Hero’s foundation, even when legal plays remain. It grants no receipt, AP, Momentum or attack, does not end the turn, and has no recharge during combat. Spending its last charge removes the buff, with the shared removal cue. Restart begins a fresh fight with three charges. Remove the PITY FLIP button beside Withdraw; this supersedes dead-opening/once-per-turn pity gating and scenario pityDraw flags. Preserve explicit pause, enemy-turn, defeated and disarmed guards.

- Ability foundation inspection (Eric, 2026-10-25): hovering, focusing or tapping any part of an ability foundation opens its ability tooltip, using the same authored details or contextual rules text as the matching buff. Preserve foundation drop/play behavior and inspection pause/input handling.
