- Status change cues, first-pass tuning (Eric, 2026-10-25): shared combat status icons bloom once with a glow and plus mark when added; removed statuses linger as noninteractive crossed-out minus-mark echoes, shrinking/fading out. Buff cues are mint and debuff cues coral; +/- marks distinguish the event without relying on color. Start with 900ms cues, no cue for initial scenario statuses or stack/duration countdowns. Freeze cues during pause/inspection, reset on restart, and use static timed highlights/echoes for reduced motion. These presentation values are provisional for tuning.

- Temporary presentation override (Eric, 2026-10-25): disable the combat arena's sepia tint during tooltip inspection and pause. Preserve clock holds, animation pausing and gameplay input guards.

- Combat inspection callouts (Eric, 2026-10-25): buffs, debuffs and abilities show their name on a separate slightly larger, lightly bold title line. Descriptions can link terms using `[[stable-id|label]]`, with optional `status:` or `ability:` namespaces. Underlined terms open nested callouts above their parent on hover, keyboard focus or tap. Keep contextual inspection, pause ownership and touch behavior.

- Proto editor scaffolding (Eric, 2026-10-25): expose `?ProtoEditor` on main and Proto entry points, gathering the existing actor artwork/text draft editor and combat ability browser with searchable combat statuses. Status inspection details can be edited as custom tooltip text or restored to the dynamic rules tooltip; custom copy changes presentation, not mechanics. Status names, glyphs, kind, expiry clock and stagger protection save to shared project data (`src/data/protoStatusOverrides.json`); stable IDs preserve scenario references. Reload combat to load authored edits. Actor text retains its existing browser draft/export scope; ability edits retain their shared saves. This is development authoring scaffolding.

- Deep Breath backfire is the Dusk Dragon win condition (Eric, 2026-10-25; supersedes the choke): the dragon has 100 HP. Breaking its stagger while it is vulnerable (cast until the breath lands) makes Deep Breath backfire: its 55 damage hits the dragon instead of the party, unmultiplied; its stagger jumps to stage III (×4 damage) and it is stunned for two enemy turns, holding the stagger until the last stunned turn, which is also its recovery. The card and charge are lost. If it survives, it re-arms with Duskscale Armor at the end of that recovery and casts again on its next turn. A breath that lands still deals 55 to every Kin (Hero has 99 HP).

- Readying ability template and Dusk Dragon (Eric, 2026-10-25): 1000 is the Dusk Dragon (999 HP, no basic attack, no Momentum buff or Heavy Strike). It opens behind Duskscale Armor (hits build no stagger) and, on its first enemy turn, casts Deep Breath: the caster-built foundation spins out beside it, the armor comes off and it plays up to 2 random matching cards (no passive pip). Template for abilities that need a waiting turn after meeting their requirements: at full charge the foundation becomes a readied card in the caster team's hand that cannot be used the turn it arrives (like summoning sickness). Deep Breath readied: the dragon skips its next turn to inhale, then breathes 55 damage on every Kin and re-arms. Breaking the caster's stagger while it is vulnerable (cast until the breath lands) chokes the ability: the card and charge are lost; it recovers, re-arms, then casts again. Stagger breaks are a universally important counter; armor that drops during charge-up is the reinforcing pattern. The dragon carries a Deep Breath buff with phase-aware text; its intent badge follows the phase.

- Caster-built ability foundations (Eric, 2026-10-25; trialled in 1000): an enemy ability such as Deep Breath is a foundation only its caster adds cards to, shown beside the caster in the enemy row. The party cannot play onto it; it can only deny it by taking the exposed cards one rank from its value. The caster picks among matching cards at random (seeded from the effect stream), and 1000 drops the passive pip so denial alone can stop it. No board markers show which cards it will take: reading the risky cards is the player's job (the red intent-play outlines are removed everywhere). Player abilities that build foundations will mirror this: only the caster adds cards, beside the caster. 020–024 keep the original shared intent card at the end of the rack.

- On-screen scenario notes (Eric, 2026-10-25; supersedes the Insight buff and popup): each combatsbx scenario with notes shows its intent, how to solve it and why it exists in a panel beneath the header's camera and settings buttons, paged by tabs (Intent / How to solve it / Why it exists). Tapping the title or the text collapses it upward to a single title row; tapping again expands it, and the choice is remembered per browser. The Insight buff and its popup are removed. "Load battle camera" is a camera icon button and Config is a settings cog, both icon-only with accessible names.

- Stepped enemy turns everywhere (Eric, 2026-10-25; universal requirement): every enemy turn in the played combat sandbox plays out one visible action at a time so players can follow what happened, never jumping ahead to a changed board. After a short hold each foe strikes in its own step, each card an enemy plays onto an intent flies there in its own step, then the passive pip or firing, then the handoff that lands a delayed Withdraw redeal; idle enemy turns still hold briefly. 999 already plays card by card on its own clock. The playable sandbox builds scenarios with `createPlayableSandboxScenario` (`rules.pacedEnemyTurns`); rules tests and tools may still resolve turns instantly. Exception: 007 keeps its scripted handoff and rescue pacing. Apply this to every new combat enemy behavior.

- Stepped enemy turns (Eric, 2026-10-25): paced combatsbx enemy turns (1000) play out one action at a time on real seconds after a short hold: the foes' strikes, each card played onto the intent (each flying from the tableau), the passive pip and any firing, then the handoff that lands a delayed Withdraw redeal. Scenarios without enemyTurnSeconds keep the instant enemy turn.

- Combat sandbox Momentum decay off by default (Eric, 2026-10-25): every combatsbx scenario starts with Momentum decay disabled; the Config toggle still turns it on. Intent-card pips only fill as pips are gained and empty as they are removed; no predictive or next-pip striping.

- Centered combat foundations (Eric, 2026-10-25): the combatsbx party row is one centered row in party order (Kin, hold slots, the empty well) and stays centered as the foundation count changes: 1000 with Hero alone, 019–024 with Hero and the empty well, Hold appearing, Mochi joining, Banks in 007.

- Scenario 1000 Hold ability (Eric, 2026-10-25): 1000 has no empty foundation placeholder and starts without a hold slot. Tapping Hero’s foundation opens her ability radial (wired for 1000 only) with a Hold card (6 Hero AP, self-cast, once per fight) that opens her hold slot for the rest of the fight; the slot then behaves as in 023/024. Hold is also in the 009 playground hand.

- Scenario 1000 Target Dummy (Eric, 2026-10-25): a combatsbx test range built like 999: a 999-HP Target Dummy that never strikes, Hero alone (99 HP) on six columns with a fresh random deal on every start and restart. Every dead-run system is on: the Deep Breath intent card, pity flip, hold slot, delayed-redeal Withdraw and board-plan markers. Its enemy turn is paced (`enemyTurnSeconds`, 2.5 s of real time) so the pending Withdraw row stays marked on the old board before the redeal lands. It carries a long-form Insight.

- Dem standing and reaction sprites (Eric, 2026-10-25): Dem has their own cream-and-blue watercolor standing bear set for low, elevated and bird's-eye cameras, with four planted paws and no trailing leaping legs. Add directional hit (a planted ice-claw strike) and flinch animations with wind-up/contact/recovery or recoil/recovery, replay, pause and reduced-motion support in the actor editor. Reuse the shared directional quadruped renderer with Dem's own atlas registration. These presentation assets do not implement their proposed combat kit. See `docs/art/dem-standing-v1.md` and `docs/art/dem-combat-v1.md`.

- Delayed-redeal Withdraw (Eric, 2026-10-25; universal in combatsbx): a party Withdraw ends the turn on the current board. The enemy acts on that board, and the chosen redeal (a destination row, or the whole tableau) lands as the next party turn starts, before any pity check. An enemy Withdraw still shifts at once, and an interrupt stops only the enemy shift. Shared board-plan markers (`rules/battlePlans.ts`, `TableauPlanMarkers`) draw where the board is about to move and what a side intends to take: a mint dashed band on the pending Withdraw row (or every column for a full redeal), a red band over the tableau for an enemy Withdraw, and red outlines on the exposed cards an intent owner will play next, each with an inspectable label left of the rack.

- Dead-run scenarios (Eric, 2026-10-25): combatsbx 019–024 put Hero alone on a six-column, four-row table against one Bellows Khad Root, with the same seed and tuning so each permutation compares like for like: 019 control (no fixes), 020 Deep Breath intent card, 021 Deep Breath keeping its rank, 022 intent + pity flip, 023 intent + hold slot, 024 all three. The intent card sits at the end of the rack as a ±1 golf target with 4 AP-style pips; each enemy turn its owner plays up to 2 matching exposed cards onto it (+1 pip each) and adds 1 passive pip, and at 4 pips it fires for 4 damage on every Kin and returns as a new card. A party card on it removes a pip and earns 1 Momentum but no AP or hit; the golf version takes the played rank, the fixed version keeps its own. A skipping owner (staggered, dormant, frozen, stunned or held) leaves the intent alone. The pity flip turns one free stock card onto the foundation, once, only when a party turn opens with nothing legal (intent and held cards count); it is not a receipt. The hold slot parks one card the Kin could play now, persists across turns and plays later onto a foundation or the intent; parking is not a receipt. Each scenario carries a long-form Insight. Rules: `rules/battleDeadRun.ts`; data: `battleTutorialScenarios.ts`; check: `tools/combat-dead-run-playwright.cjs`.

- Combat Withdraw shortcut (Eric, 2026-10-24): place a Withdraw button immediately right of End Turn in combatsbx. It directly enters the same committed row selection as the Momentum Withdraw card, with the same availability guards, visible values, party destination preview, row movement and end-turn behavior.

- Combat sandbox Kin abilities unwired (Eric, 2026-10-24): remove Hero’s and Mochi’s foundation-to-ability-radial bindings in combatsbx, including switching to those radials from the Momentum menu. Keep ability definitions and implementation available for future rewiring and ordinary encounters; retain tableau receipts/basic attacks, AP meters, targeting drags and the team Momentum Withdraw. This supersedes the earlier foundation-tap ability access in the mounted combat sandbox.

- Tableau drag source visibility (Eric, 2026-10-24): hide the source card once a tableau drag starts, keeping its slot and tableau state intact. Do not backfill, promote or consume the card until a valid drop commits the receipt. Restore the source on invalid drop, cancellation, Escape, lost capture or blur; retain mouse/touch input and flick behavior.

- Query-aware browser tab titles (Eric, 2026-10-24): all app HTML pages include their query parameters in readable browser tab titles. Resolve combat scenario names from the scenario catalog; label known sandboxes and retain arbitrary flags, values and repeated parameters. Preserve the original title when there is no query, and refresh after History API URL changes and back/forward navigation.

- Exponential chained stagger costs (Eric, 2026-10-24): each successive stagger stage costs twice the previous stage: base threshold × 1, × 2, × 4 (normally 6, 12, 24 points to reach stages 1–3). Keep three stages, existing vulnerability and reset-on-break behavior, Indominable protection and turn recovery. Gauge displays and previews use the current stage threshold; the terminal-stage gauge stays capped at the final threshold.

- Team turn-end banners (Eric, 2026-10-24): componentize the fighting-game “A New Challenger…” banner and reuse it for large player/enemy team turn-end announcements in combatsbx. Fade turn-end banners in, hold briefly, then fade out before advancing the queue. Freeze the fade with pause and use a static timed banner for reduced motion. Queue both announcements for instantaneous enemy responses; preserve pause, reduced motion, restart cleanup and the authored Banks entrance. These requested announcements are an exception to the no-unsolicited-guidance rule; they do not block or alter gameplay.

- Indominable stagger protection (Eric, 2026-10-24): while any Indominable stacks remain, incoming hits cannot increase the enemy’s stagger gauge or trigger a new stagger. Preserve existing stagger progress; HP damage still removes stacks normally. The hit removing the last stack remains protected; subsequent hits at zero stacks gain stagger normally.

- Enemy tableau card flights (Eric, 2026-10-24): cards received by enemies from the tableau use the same card-flight animation as player receipts, landing on the enemy foundation value. Include normal enemy plays and forced rearming, retain immediate rules resolution, and preserve pause, reduced motion and restart cleanup. Stock draws without a tableau source do not invent a flight.

- Covered combat tableau values (Eric, 2026-10-24): increase the vertical peek between combatsbx rows and enlarge covered-card value text so every complete value, including 10, fits above the next card. Inset value headers independently of card-size padding and allow smaller overall cards to retain the seven-column/four-row layout and viewport fit.

- Scenario 999 intent presentation (Eric, 2026-10-24): enemy targeting uses a shared dashed stem that splits toward both living player foundations, passing beneath tableau cards, with an All allies route badge instead of a tableau destination. Show Indominable as an inspectable buff above the enemy at all stack counts, including zero. Its end-of-turn damage hits every living party member (superseding the previous highest-AP target).

- Enemy intent routes (Eric, 2026-10-24): combatsbx enemy intents use the player foundations’ dashed-arrow and compact route-badge system instead of a separate framed intent pill. Enemy targeting lines run straight beneath tableau cards, while player routes retain their outer lanes. Preserve readable intent details and hover/focus/touch inspection, hidden intentions, dormant/no-target states and responsive geometry.

- Withdraw row selection (Eric, 2026-10-24): choosing the Momentum Withdraw card commits the player’s turn; it cannot be cancelled back to normal play. Hover/focus a tableau row to highlight it and show living party members’ painted emojis beside it. Confirming removes rows in front of the destination, keeps it exposed, refills behind it and ends the turn. The exposed row is also a valid destination. Hold gameplay during selection while preserving exposed and otherwise revealed card values; selection does not apply global-pause value concealment. Explicit manual pause still conceals values. This supersedes the full-tableau redeal for the Momentum ability; legacy untargeted Withdraw paths retain their existing behavior.

- Combat Momentum overlay and larger tableau (Eric, 2026-10-24): float the combatsbx Momentum gauge in the viewport’s lower-right corner above the FPS count, keeping that count unobscured. Use the released middle-field space for larger tableau cards fitted to all seven columns and four rows; the sandbox tableau is no longer capped by the foundation face size. Preserve gauge interactions, pause and responsive layouts.

- Kin AP and ability radials (Eric, 2026-10-24; clean cutover in combatsbx): every tableau receipt banks +1 AP on the receiving Kin (cap 6 per Kin as data, `AP_CAP`; recommended by Monte Carlo on the 7×4 table) and +1 shared Momentum. Ability cards belong to one Kin and cost that Kin's AP (Bash 1 Hero, Pounce 3 Mochi, Sticky Paws 2 Mochi; Cir is paid by the Kin she binds to, Meteor by Hero). Momentum keeps its decay and pays only for Withdraw (0) and interrupting an enemy Withdraw (3); the Momentum radial holds only Withdraw and is exclusive. AP persists across turns and Withdraw, does not decay, and is lost when the Kin is downed; enemies target the Kin holding the most AP. Tapping a player foundation opens that Kin's ability radial (same hold/slowdown as the Momentum dial) instead of the jumbo holocard (the map holocard still opens jumbo); several Kin radials can be open together, tapping an open foundation closes its radial, and dragging a foundation still retargets. AP shows as a labelled pip meter on each foundation, without hover/touch inspection tooltips (Eric, 2026-10-24).
- Combat tutorial scenarios (Eric, 2026-10-24): combatsbx 008 Chip Tutorial (endless backfill, "Rounds completed" counter, rounds shown on defeat), 009 Ability Playground (every card, full AP, decay off) and focused scenarios 010–018 (Tempo, Frost & Shatter, Storm, Steam, Bleed then Burn, Silence & Banking, Haste + Vice Grip, Ember Rage, Focus Fire). Every one gives a party member the Insight buff, whose paged popup explains intent, how to solve it and why it exists. All enemies are Khad Roots; there are no slimes or real-time timers. Rules: `rules/battleTutorials.ts`, `battleStatuses.ts`, `battleStatusData.ts`, `battleTutorialScenarios.ts`.
- Tutorial statuses as tableau mechanics (Eric, 2026-10-24): durations count party/enemy turns or receipts. Enemies bank 2 Momentum per enemy turn and spend 4 on a double-damage Heavy Strike. Haste doubles the Momentum a unit gains (on a Kin, its next 4 receipts also earn double AP); Slow halves it (rounded down for enemies). Chill costs 1 enemy Momentum gain per stack; 3 Chill, or Chill on Soaked, Freezes (skips its turn); Takedown on Frozen Shatters. Soak + fire = Steam (Blind, 50% miss); shock on Soaked conducts; fire on Bleeding Cauterizes. Bleed ticks per receipt, Burn per enemy turn. Sap blocks two receipts in a row on one foundation; Silence blocks a Kin's abilities but not its AP. Enemy dispositions are reusable foe data: Dormant, Regrowth (heals to full at the end of its turn unless below a threshold), Heavy Bark (physical hits of 3+ halved), Dry Bark (fire doubled), Stifling Sap (the party plays one ability per turn; Withdraw exempt).
- Ember Rage (Eric, 2026-10-24; name is one constant, `EMBER_RAGE`): Hero, 0 AP, once per turn. Until the turn ends she uses no other ability, her AP cap is 12 and each chip hit adds floor(AP/3) fire (counted after that receipt's AP; fire adds no stagger); AP above 6 burns off at the end of the turn. Focus Fire (Hero, 2 AP): pick a tableau card; every card of that rank burns (buried or exposed), the columns refill, each burned card deals 1 fire to every foe, and burned cards are not receipts.
- Future idea, not current gameplay (Eric, 2026-10-24): let a Kin raise its own AP cap, and let pooled AP act as a separate resource; for example Hero's AP above 5 adds fire damage to each basic attack that round, so pooling long combo chains linearly strengthens her hits. Ember Rage is a first, one-turn version of this.

- Scenario 999 Dark Tyrant (Eric, 2026-10-24): combatsbx enemy has 999 HP and plays legal tableau cards on its turn. Generate a fully randomized seven-column, four-row tableau and stock on every start, restart and Withdraw redeal; do not guarantee a legal opening or use an authored rank sequence. Every successful play grants one persistent stack of “Indominable”. At the end of its turn, if it has more than 25 stacks, the buff deals 1 damage for each stack above 25 (damage = stacks − 25; 26 stacks deals 1) to every living party member. This passive also resolves on skipped/rearm turns. Each actual HP lost removes one stack, clamped at zero; blocked damage and overkill do not remove extra stacks. This supersedes the original inert buff. Present stacks through the existing inspectable status UI.

- No-move party expressions (Eric, 2026-10-24): living player foundation emojis look upset while the player's combat turn has no legal tableau receipts. This takes priority over happy attack reactions; downed actors stay sad. Restore normal reactions when legal plays return, control passes to the enemy or combat ends. Use existing painted expressions and preserve portrait geometry and pause behavior.

- Mochi vine variant (Eric, 2026-10-24): keep her original holocard art and offer a separate same-pose vine-wrapped painting for review. Vines fit comfortably around legs and tail and form one painted character layer, replacing the separate Mochi motif parallax experiment. Original remains the normal game portrait; Hero's flame layers are unchanged. See ART.md.

- Mochi holocard artwork (Eric, 2026-10-24): replace the flat portrait presentation with the approved transparent softened-face Mochi cutout over a separate painted forest through the shared layered holocard renderer. Preserve the latest dark facial markings and slightly lightened mouth, opaque cream fur, card interactions and reduced-motion behavior. Prior flat art remains historical; see ART.md.

- No unsolicited guidance UI (Eric, 2026-10-24): do not inject hint banners, instructional overlays, narrative status strips or similar extra elements into the game unless explicitly requested. This includes the Scenario 007 `aside.night-rescue-status` strip with copy such as “Break the bark. Wound the heart.” Do not invent persistent coaching or story-phase summaries to explain authored encounters. Present approved mechanics through the established game components and inspection tooltips; this rule supersedes any older requirement for unsolicited guidance elements.

- Voluntary End Turn (Eric, 2026-10-24): players may end their combat turn with legal tableau moves remaining, including Scenario 003. Keep pause, enemy control, battle completion and Scenario 007's automatic entrance/rescue input guards. Early handoffs during Scenario 007's opening/finish resolve the dormant enemy turn and return control without changing its authored cards or story phase. Supersedes prior mandatory move-exhaustion and Scenario 003 Withdraw-only handoff restrictions.

- Scenario 007 seven-column layout (Eric, 2026-10-24): populate all seven tableau columns with four rows in the opening, snare/rescue and finishing deals. Use a fixed irregular distribution that crosses between columns rather than progressing left to right; scenario restarts repeat the same deal. Preserve the deterministic legal opening, 15 earned Momentum (and the AP each Kin banks for the desperation turn), buried outer 8, Banks's 9/10/stolen-11 sequence and final 12 break. Keep wraparound opening cards buried to prevent legal choices from stranding progress; rescue filler cards cannot interrupt Banks's authored route.

- Scenario 007 bark copy (Eric, 2026-10-24): name the enemy's opening protection buff Duskwood Bark, with tooltip “Immune to damage and status effects unless staggered.” This is a buff/tooltip copy update; existing scenario mechanics remain unchanged.

- Scenario 007 callouts (Eric, 2026-10-24): omit instructional callout overlays and their dismissal/input gates from The Gallows Bloom. Keep the boss countdown buff/status and Banks's automatic rescue pacing, cinematic entrance and ability presentation. This supersedes the callout requirement below.

- Mobile global lighting (Eric, 2026-10-24): disable global day/night lighting and haze on touch-first mobile devices (coarse primary pointer with no hover), including landscape phones and tablets. Disable the Global lights control there; preserve the saved desktop preference and independent local lights.

- Gentle combat camera action (Eric, 2026-10-24): tie the arena camera to actual actor attack cues, including chip attacks, with a small eased push-in, directional drift and brief approach motion blur. Keep contact readable, blend repeated attacks within bounded limits, return to the current authored/manual framing, freeze with combat pause and disable these effects for reduced motion. Presentation only; preserve targeting and gameplay timing.

- Paused combat presentation and sandbox panes (Eric, 2026-10-24): global pause applies a sepia filter to the battle camera and combat tableau field, including inspection pauses. Restore normal color on resume. In combatsbx, the loaded camera occupies the wider right pane and the tableau the smaller left pane; retain responsive stacked views on narrow screens.

- Combat inspection (Eric, 2026-10-24): hovering or tapping inspectable combat elements, including buffs, debuffs, intent summaries and abilities, invokes global pause and conceals tableau values. Use the shared parchment tooltip anchored near the inspected element, fitted to the viewport; retain hover-leave, outside-tap and Escape dismissal. Keep inspection UI usable while time is held. Dismissal releases the inspection's hold while preserving manual pause and other holds; preserve ability targeting, dragging, casting and explicit gameplay actions. Keyboard focus also supports inspection, with Escape dismissal. This supersedes the one-second Cir buff inspection gesture for reading its explanation; its holocard and paid recall remain available.

- Dem identity and tank direction (Eric, 2026-10-24): globally rename the ice polar-bear Keru to Dem, inspired by Demeter; Dem uses they/them pronouns. Their proposed kit pivots to tanking: Ice Wall is a self-buff that replaces automatic foundation-receipt attacks with +1 ice-wall barrier HP per card added to Dem's foundation; Momentum generation continues normally. Aurora Bearealis is Light/Ice, revealing the encounter tableau (including backfills) for two turns and exposing affected enemies for a 25% attack critical-hit chance. Timing, barriers, crit resolution, targeting and costs remain design proposals pending implementation. Dem's own actor/foundation is a future prerequisite; do not silently redirect these abilities to Hero or Mochi. See `docs/design/keru-elements/dem-tank.md`.

- Scenario 007 The Gallows Bloom (Eric, 2026-10-24): deterministic story encounter in combatsbx. Player strips Ironbark and meaningfully wounds the Gallows Khad, then Snaring Khad Roots block tableau receipts and Withdraw while abilities remain usable. A full desperation turn lets each Kin spend the AP it banked in the opening on ineffective ability attempts against Sealed Heartwood (Momentum is no longer spent there). Announce the fatal team-wide Gallows Bloom countdown through both boss buff and callouts (two handoffs, then one). The next End Turn triggers a rendered hand/paw slap and original fighting-game challenger entrance. Banks arrives left of Hero through the shared approved spinning holocard flight and shimmer as an inspectable NPC ally foundation, never a manual receiver. Precipice of Night makes Dark abilities cost equal self-stagger instead of Momentum. Script Misty Step, outer-column Midnight Reach, legal front-row sequence, Sticky Paws priming/attack/theft/play, Veil of Night causing the actual Bloom to miss, Momentum-funded Borrowed Burden stagger swap, and a final legal receipt to stagger the boss and release roots. Player finishes by legal sequencing. Freeze scenario Momentum decay before the finish so reading and spending cannot spoil the authored rescue; retain pause, reduced motion, restart and prior scenarios. Encounter ranks, HP and ability costs are scenario tuning, not general balance.


- Distinct Keru abilities (Eric, 2026-10-24): each Keru has multiple named abilities with stable identities, costs, effects and explicit targeting rules. Target selection does not determine which ability the Keru becomes. Unlock/progression requirements are future design; expose authored abilities for review/playtesting now. This supersedes the target-dependent expression model. Keep existing Cir effect resolution, costs, recall/exhaustion and Scenario 006 safeguards while presenting Hearthflame, Ember Claws and Meteor as separate sandbox cards. Other Keru effects remain authored proposals until implemented; do not silently imply they are functional.

- Combat sandbox reaction removal (Eric, 2026-10-23): omit the damage-reaction emoji callout in `/proto.html?combatsbx`. Retain reactions in ordinary encounters and the sandbox's foundation portraits and target badges. Supersedes the compact combat flinch presentation for this sandbox only.

- Creature-based Keru redesign (Eric, 2026-10-23): elemental Keru use distinct animal anatomies with supernatural motifs integral to their respective elements. Remove the uniform round spirit/oversized-eye/hyper-cute treatment; vary temperament from composed and formidable to dangerous. Dem is a glacial polar bear, Bri a woodland stag, Sol a radiant crane, Vey a shadow panther, Flux a volatile hyena and Axi a stone-shell tortoise. Cir is also rebuilt as a phoenix retaining her bright meteor core, diagonal shooting-star gesture and streaming ember train. This supersedes preserving Cir's previous orb creature artwork. Retain the layered watercolor workflow and current mechanics; other Keru abilities remain drafts.

- Compact combat flinch reactions (Eric, 2026-10-23): show only the painted reaction emoji in a compact 56px footprint above the receiving foundation, without speaker, dialogue text or bubble chrome. Retain accessible reaction text, pause, expiry and damage triggers.

- Layered card mockups (Eric, 2026-10-23): scaffold holocard/ability previews using game components with independently reusable watercolor background and character elements. Render text, costs, badges and foil in code. Do not generate static flattened card/product mockup artwork. Dem remains a presentation/mechanics draft until adopted.

- Major element Keru drafts (Eric, 2026-10-23): author Nature, Light, Dark, Chaos and Order options in the layered `/proto.html?kerusbx` gallery, alongside the Dem ice draft. Bri, Sol, Vey, Flux and Axi explore RPG-inspired growth, wards/cleanse, drain/curse, visible variance and cadence respectively. These are art/mechanics proposals, not additions to battle execution or approved balance. See `docs/design/keru-elements/README.md` for target-dependent expressions, single-use invocations and synergies.

- Scenario 006 Ember Trial Sentinel (Eric, 2026-10-23): require Cir's three uses in order. Phase I Counterflame blocks direct damage and stagger, targets Hero, and advances after three 1-fire Hearthflame counters (36 to 33 HP). Phase II Khad Armor admits only the fire portion of Mochi's Ember Claws attacks, including enhanced Pounce; clamp at 7 HP. Phase III Indomitable Thorns clears stagger, cannot be staggered, and ignores individual damage hits below 5; recall Cir and invoke 8-fire Meteor to finish. Block early Meteor invocation to preserve the required sequence. Keep paid bindings, recall and Momentum generation/decay; this replaces Scenario 006's previous free-form familiar/Meteor trial. Three counters and the phase-I targeting override are scenario tuning, not ordinary encounter rules.

- Hearthflame rework (Eric, 2026-10-23): while Cir is bound to Hero, attackers take 1 fire damage per attack against Hero, including zero-damage attacks, and Hero's builder receipts earn 1.25× their base Momentum (1.25 instead of 1). Other Kin retain normal earnings; recall removes both effects. Keep the 3 Momentum binding cost, fractional reserves, whole spendable readouts, fire mitigation/vulnerability and no retaliation chains. Supersedes Hearthflame's previous 2-damage, actual-damage-only retaliation.

- Flame numeral smoke contrast (Eric, 2026-10-23): strengthen Flame lab digits with a dense near-black 4.5px outer stroke and a hot readable face above flame streaks. Add an opaque charcoal backsplash with an irregular billowing smoke perimeter behind the value, sized to the actual single/multi-digit geometry, so the surrounding spiral cannot obscure it. Keep the smaller numeral size, free outer sparks, unlit zero, Config style choice, bounded rendering, pause/bullet time and reduced-motion stills.

- Keru potential-target badge (Eric, 2026-10-23): replace the Keru ability card's bottom potential-target name with the target's existing watercolor emoji at the upper-right tip, half overlapping the corner. Enemies without emoji artwork use their actual portrait in a rounded frame. Update on drag overlap and keyboard target focus, preserve accessible target names, changing effect/cost, input ownership and cast behavior, and retain the badge through cast flight.

- Smaller inked flame numerals (Eric, 2026-10-23): reduce Flame lab numeral geometry by 20% and add a near-black stroke around the actual glyph silhouette, including its holes. Keep flame tongues, sparks and ash free of the numeral stroke. Apply to the combat and raised ability dials and lab preview, retaining the Config style choice, whole values, multi-digit fitting, motion controls and fallback.

- Cir recall (Eric, 2026-10-23): remove familiar-duration boilerplate from card and tooltip copy. Hold Cir’s applied foundation buff for one second (mouse/touch; Enter opens directly) to inspect her holocard. Pause combat while inspecting; offer Send back to deck to remove the current familiar effect and restore the unique Cir card. Recall refunds no Momentum; every subsequent binding or invocation pays its full target-specific cost. Meteor still exhausts Cir for the battle and cannot be recalled. Preserve cancellation, focus, reduced motion, restart and ordinary encounters. Supersedes the initial no-relocation/recasting restriction.

- Momentum numeral style switch (Eric, 2026-10-23): Config offers Flame lab and Stroked Momentum numerals, with Flame lab as the fresh-load default. Apply the selected style to both the combat dial and raised ability-menu dial; retain the choice across scenario changes/restarts in the mounted sandbox. Switching changes presentation only and releases the unused numeral renderer. Preserve the existing spiral, whole spendable values, accessibility, pause and reduced motion.

- Ability holocard depth (Eric, 2026-10-23): ability faces share pointer/touch/focus spring tilt, inspection-only holofoil and parallax title/cost/effect labels through the existing TradingCard framework. Parent ability controls retain press/hold/drag/cast ownership with no nested gameplay controls. Cir uses the existing painted night background behind her separate approved cutout; inspection slightly enlarges her sprite and lets it protrude beyond the frame. Clip the sky and foil to the card while allowing bounded subject extrusion. Preserve target-changing copy/costs, card drop geometry, contact bloom, cancel, pause and static reduced motion. Supersedes the plain-finish/no-motion restriction for ability faces; no idle shimmer or new artwork is required.

- Flaming Momentum numerals (Eric, 2026-10-23): adapt the supplied Omni Flames lab's displaced shell, surface sampling, curl flow and flame-to-ash lifecycle to numeral geometry representing the current whole spendable Momentum value. The value itself burns above the existing combat spiral; show a comparison in the Momentum design lab. Keep numeral holes/readability, multi-digit fitting, an unlit zero, immediate numeric updates, pause/bullet time, reduced-motion stills, local dependencies, bounded particle/render budgets, offscreen/hidden culling, disposal and a plain-number fallback. The supplied torus scaffold is a reference, not a new gameplay mechanic.

- Combat Auto-M placement and shortcut (Eric, 2026-10-23): place Auto-M immediately left of End Turn as a shared group beneath the player foundations. Either Ctrl key invokes the same single-move solver action in combat sandbox; held-key repeats do not. Respect text editing, pause, active solver/animations, enemy turns and battle completion.

- Linear Momentum baseline (Eric, 2026-10-23): each valid builder receipt earns a fixed +1 shared Momentum, independent of sequence length or play speed, so earned Momentum grows linearly with cards played. Exponential growth, growth bonuses and slowed/prevented decay belong to explicit Keru abilities or modifiers rather than default behavior; specific new effects remain to be designed. Preserve the uncapped reserve, spending, sequence handoff, existing 0.5/s combat decay, pause/bullet-time clock and optional decay-freeze modifiers. Supersedes the exponential and rapid/sustained default earnings below; cost previews and whole-point visible readouts remain in effect.

- Fluid Momentum flame growth (Eric, 2026-10-23): animate the displayed flame front continuously toward reserve changes along its center-out spiral, including rapid successive earnings and overflow, rather than snapping the flame to each receipt. Keep numeric reserves and mechanics immediate; spending/decay contract smoothly, pause and stagger bullet time govern presentation, reduced motion renders directly, and rendering remains bounded and visibility-aware.

- Unique Keru expressions (Eric, 2026-10-23): there is one Cir, the fire Keru. Keru express different abilities through the Kin they bind to; do not use Mind/Heart/Paw slots. Effects last until combat ends unless explicitly specified otherwise; future Keru may have temporary effects, cooldowns or battle exhaustion. Direct invocation exhausts Cir for the current battle. A bound Cir remains attached until recalled through her foundation buff or combat ends; recall permits another paid use. Hero gets Hearthflame (3 Momentum, attackers take 2 fire damage after actual attack damage); Mochi gets Ember Claws (4 Momentum, +1 fire damage on each damaging attack); an enemy target gets Meteor (6 Momentum, 8 fire damage, exhausts Cir). These numbers are temporary tuning. Fire respects Ironbark and stagger vulnerability, adds no extra stagger or Momentum, and retaliation never chains from Thorns/other retaliation. A single shared Cir holocard uses approved existing artwork and changes effect/cost with drag overlap or keyboard target focus. Persist a familiar badge and show bound/exhausted availability; reset Cir on fresh combat. Scenario 006 supplies an attacking 36-HP Sentinel: one 2-damage attack at team handoff, targeting the normal highest-AP living Kin; stagger skips its next response and recovers. Older scenarios and ordinary encounters retain their existing behavior.

- Combat sandbox decay and full flame front (Eric, 2026-10-23): double combat Momentum decay from 0.25 to 0.5 points per gameplay second, retaining pause, stagger bullet time, freeze modifiers and the Config toggle. The Momentum art lab stays at 0.375/s. Distribute combat flame-tail particles and sparks over the full radial height of the current leading edge, including its outward billow, rather than one narrow point. Track the live ribbon profile and retain bounded particle counts and reduced motion.

- Combat sandbox Momentum prominence (Eric, 2026-10-23): hide the Momentum heading, enlarge the dial and reserve type by 25% (180px desktop/140px mobile before viewport fitting), and strengthen the leading flame tail and sparks. Retain accessible Momentum names, raised ability dial, bounded particles, pause/reduced motion and gameplay rules. Supersedes the prior 144px/112px sandbox dial sizing; the design lab retains its own effect tuning.

- Ember Tongues combat gauge (Eric, 2026-10-23): promote the live Ember Tongues spiral to the combat sandbox Momentum dial and select it by default in the design lab. Replace the old central raster/ring presentation with the bounded twelve-point flame and hotter core-out overflow. Preserve fractional earnings, spending, decay and ability mechanics internally, but show only whole spendable points in reserves, projections and visible Momentum readouts. Use a stronger 6px dark text stroke. Render the raised radial dial as a real component rather than cloning canvas DOM; preserve pause, stagger bullet time, reduced motion, mobile fitting and a static icon fallback when WebGL is unavailable. Other small currency icons remain shared artwork. Supersedes the experimental-only restriction for this selected combat sandbox gauge.

- Exposed Momentum flame edge (Eric, 2026-10-23): containment is local to the newer spiral coil actually wrapping over each section, not completion of that section's own circumference. Keep the entire unwrapped outside edge alive, with large irregular outward billows and pointed licks strongest near the leading front; older exposed arcs still flare. Reserve drawing room for this eruption while retaining a tight wrapped core, strong silhouette ink and bounded shader work. Apply the local wrapping rule to all live studies; Ember Tongues is the billow tuning reference. Supersedes whole-turn completion as the containment trigger.

- Momentum design lab decay (Eric, 2026-10-23): enable Decay by default in the living flame sandbox and drain 0.375 Momentum per second (half the previous 0.75). Keep the playtest toggle available.

- Dense Momentum core and path-led eruption (Eric, 2026-10-23): preserve the luminous Solar Filaments texture while revealing it along one connected center-out spiral, never by a uniformly expanding perimeter. Pack the inner turns tightly with a filled burning heart; completed turns settle into dense coils while the exposed front has broad irregular tongues and a curling turbulent plume. Use progressively wider radial pitch toward the outside in every live study, retaining skinny inner ribbons, 12-point waves, hotter overflow and existing motion/performance controls. Experimental sandbox art only.

- Dense patch card height (Eric, 2026-10-22): anchor each bush's card stacks higher across its foliage so they cover more bush than bare ground, in both wide and narrow layouts. Preserve prop placement, card coverage, rewards and play order.

- Ability card footer copy (Eric, 2026-10-22): omit bottom availability/reason text (including “Not enough Momentum”) and per-card drag instructions. Retain costs, actor/self labels, effects, unavailable styling and accessible interaction/reason labels.

- Traversal suspension (Eric, 2026-10-22): temporarily disable between-grid traversal tableaus, including den exit and outgoing/incoming crossing deals. Walk routes automatically; retain destination gathering, finite berry rewards/choices, rest and encounters. Keep the traversal planner, deals, camera and tests available behind `TRAVERSAL_TABLEAUS_ENABLED` for future use. Skip the den card-play lesson while suspended. Supersedes the required traversal deals below during this playtest.

- Dense patch scenery continuity (Eric, 2026-10-22): its reward-free incoming traversal and gathering puzzle look at the same authored three bushes (two large, one small), with the same placement, sizes and two/two/one fruit. Reverse the approach dolly into the gathering composition; keep traversal rewards separate and remove fruit only through actual gathering.

- Ability card breathing room (Eric, 2026-10-22): give the desktop Momentum hand separate full-face slots for Withdraw and actor abilities, a gentle arc, readable text and enough vertical room for complete effects. Actor focus must preserve face exposure; keep cost previews clear of the hand hint. Preserve inspection, targeting and spending rules.

- Tableau viewport envelope (Eric, 2026-10-22): every generated/authored tableau must fit all transformed card corners, key prop silhouettes, crowns, fruit and contact shadows inside its measured play viewport at desktop and phone portrait/landscape sizes. Use a shared padded envelope for cards and props; use available upper-field space before extending toward the bottom. Reflow bush/card groups vertically on narrow portrait fields to retain readable values. Never rely on scene overflow/clipping to conceal a misfit. Validate full deals and partially cleared states with actual browser bounds checks and screenshots.

- Travel camera and dense approach iteration (Eric, 2026-10-22): the den's painted cave shares the departure pullback. After the final outgoing traversal card and its boundary handoff, swing toward the onward view, then reverse the dolly through incoming traversal to approach the destination puzzle. Pause freezes the turn; reduced motion changes views directly. Before the -1,1 dense patch's resource puzzle, require its own isolated six-card reward-free traversal deal; keep the finite five-berry gathering puzzle and last-berry choice after it. Divide the existing incoming stamina budget between these stages, preserving finite rewards and actual-walking stage gates. Supersedes the previous single interwoven approach/resource sequence for this journey.

- Travel chrome reduction (Eric, 2026-10-22): remove Play to actor, Reset trail and Pause/Resume journey buttons from every travel tableau, including map overlays. Remove the segment title/direction/card-progress summary and stamina/streak/cards-ahead readout globally. Preserve actor holocards, direct card play/dragging, solver controls and global pause.

- Travel tableau departure camera (Eric, 2026-10-22): each card-earned step away from a tile pulls the tableau scenery camera back and slightly upward, letting the bushes and ground recede across the departure sequence. Follow actual earned walking, preserve the scene until its crossing boundary, and keep card geometry, costs, depletion, pause and reduced motion intact. Gathering scenes retain their close composition.

- Solver control naming (Eric, 2026-10-22): label the single-move solver button “Auto-M” (formerly “Best Move”); preserve its existing behavior.

- Sparse berry continuity (Eric, 2026-10-22): the final uncollectable berry on the first bush belongs inside the vine-covered right branch. Share its placement across tableau, map views and downstream scenes; preserve the reachable first fruit and existing Khad blocking rules.

- Solar filament reference and completed-turn containment (Eric, 2026-10-23): add a fourth procedural Momentum study, Solar Filaments, inspired by the supplied fiery vortex reference’s luminous coiled core, translucent sheets and curling filaments; no source pixels or watermark are incorporated. Select it by default while retaining earlier designs. Completed concentric turns reduce width, distortion and tongue reach; unfinished leading regions remain loose and turbulent. Apply containment to the existing ribbon studies too, keyed to completed circumferences and local wave fill, retaining overflow and motion controls. This remains experimental sandbox art.

- Momentum-scaled ink and outward flame sizing (Eric, 2026-10-23): include the ethereal tail tip in a strong black contour around the aggregate particle plume (not individual bubble outlines). Increase stroke width with total Momentum, while retaining thinner inner-turn ink. Rebuild variation ranges so both minimum and maximum flame width grow from skinny inner turns to broader outer turns; scale tongue reach, tail particle radii and smoke billow ranges accordingly. Preserve free particle motion, colored overflow and top-layer silhouette rules. Supersedes unoutlined tail tips.

- Ethereal Momentum tail and smoke (Eric, 2026-10-23): replace dedicated tapered/curved tail geometry with 20 deterministic, respawning shader particles per visible wave. Their puffs drift buoyantly, shrink and fade like the supplied Canvas example; keep them unoutlined. Widen the hard opaque near-black contour of the main topmost flame. Add a separate nine-puff curling charcoal/rust smoke underlay behind the flame, with a Smoky billow comparison toggle. Preserve existing sparks, bounded three-wave overflow, reduced motion and transparent empty state. No new production combat changes.

- Flame silhouette and free tail (Eric, 2026-10-23): Momentum study tails should be the least structured element. Use independent wandering, curling wisps rather than one rigid taper. Remove transverse periodic pigment cutouts and discontinuities at turn joins so flame does not read as snake scales. Add hard cartoon-style dark contour strokes to the topmost flame expansion, preserving soft pigment inside and leaving sparks unoutlined. Retain bloom overlap, hotter overflow, reduced-motion stills and bounded rendering.

- Unconfined Momentum bloom/overflow studies (Eric, 2026-10-23): retain Ember Tongues width while adding seeded irregular spiral silhouettes, larger animated tongues, overlapping outer blooms and fixed-budget leading-tail embers. Add Bloom overlap and Rekindle shape controls. Set the study base range to 12, previewing overflow up to 60. Each additional 12 starts another core-out expansion over prior flame; render only the three latest waves for bounded work. Cooler inner red/orange warms toward outer yellow/white; overflow introduces hotter cyan/blue-white as a temperature-inspired artistic cue, not a physical combustion model. Keep animation pause/reduced motion, hidden-tab culling and production combat unchanged.

- Living flame Momentum studies (Eric, 2026-10-23): continue `proto.html?momentumsbx` with three web-rendered alternatives (Ember Tongues, Windcoil, Painted Vortex). The original icon’s cream/gold/orange/red pigment and flame licks/tails govern the direction; avoid uniform molten channels. Use an internal spiral fill field with live irregular ribbons, curved leading tails, negative-space breakup and layered rotating/scaling procedural noise informed by the three user-supplied JS/GLSL examples. No remote texture dependencies or capture helpers. Provide motion/energy controls, transparent backgrounds, focus comparison, reduced-motion stills, bounded 480px backing resolution and 30fps draw throttling; skip drawing offscreen/hidden and dispose GPU resources on unmount. Earlier procedural/ImageGen studies remain optional references; do not change combat. Supersedes continuing the pre-rendered Tidal Halo as the primary direction.

- Connected Momentum spiral (Eric, 2026-10-23): in the pigment study sandbox, each variant is one continuous spiral starting at the center, not disconnected concentric rings. Map Momentum linearly to cumulative path length and advance a narrow watercolor wet front along that path, leaving completed pigment behind and no disconnected paint ahead. Larger outer turns consequently consume more Momentum per turn. Cache path geometry and raster coverage; retain the five motifs, grain, comparison controls and combat isolation. Supersedes per-ring noisy fill.

- Momentum pigment study sandbox (Eric, 2026-10-23): `proto.html?momentumsbx` is a dedicated experimental art lab comparing five concentric Momentum motifs: Spiraling Ember, Ember Orbits, Tidal Halo, Solar Bloom and Ink Vortex. Fill inner bands before outer bands using the existing discovery-style multiscale noise threshold/soft bleed method, adapted to cached canvas pigment layers instead of live SVG filter stacks. Include shared value/range/size controls, builder/spend steps, animated fill, decay, value toggle, dark/light backgrounds, per-variant focus and the current icon reference. Keep combat and production Momentum artwork unchanged until a design is selected. Procedural art studies are code-native, not replacement approved raster assets. Validate desktop/mobile controls, full/empty/partial states, animations and viewport access.

- Momentum dial and sandbox config (Eric, 2026-10-23): enlarge the sandbox Momentum dial to 144px desktop/112px mobile before viewport fitting. Center the Spiraling Ember artwork inside the rings with the reserve number overlaid in outlined high-contrast type; retain raised radial presentation and cost previews. Move Momentum decay and Add/Remove Mochi controls into the keyboard-accessible Config flyout in the sandbox header. Preserve existing control effects, ordinary combat controls and viewport bounds.

- Ability holocard framework (Eric, 2026-10-22): render combat ability faces through the same TradingCard framework as actor holocards, using the shared 63:88 aspect ratio across fan, Withdraw utility card, drag ghost and cast clone. Preserve costs, actor/self labels and effect copy, without per-card instruction footers. Fan owns input; disable nested card interactions/device tilt, use plain finish (no idle shimmer), and retain contact-only foil, actor focus and targeting behavior.

- Banks photo identity correction (Eric, 2026-10-22): Banks is predominantly dark charcoal, with subtle muted gray tabby markings and short close fur; no pronounced cream chest, paws or broad pale bands. Standing and sitting match the mature proportions/silhouette of walking, flinch and down. Apply across all Banks artwork while preserving existing poses and gameplay; ART.md records current assets and provenance.

- Banks global art pass (Eric, 2026-10-22): use the supplied reaching-paws action pose for his holocard and Hero's approved watercolor artwork/ART.md for the medium across every active Banks asset. Preserve mature slate-tabby identity, existing gameplay, travel/combat poses, expressions and protective paired rescue. The v6 action portrait supersedes preserving the seated v5 holocard; runtime rigs v4, expressions v3 and nuzzle v3 share the new pass. See ART.md.

- Mochi face identity (Eric, 2026-10-22): her photo-specific asymmetrical charcoal/copper markings down the nose bridge, pink nose top with black underside, and dark mouth/chin are standing identity details. Preserve them in soft watercolor; see ART.md. Current holocard uses the reference's slightly lowered head angle.

- Mochi holocard muzzle (Eric, 2026-10-22): nose, mouth, whisker pads, chin and whiskers share the soft diffuse watercolor brushwork of Hero and the surrounding Mochi portrait. Avoid crisp outlined features and clean bright whisker arcs. Preserve approved proportions and identity; see ART.md.

- Opening callout timing (Eric, 2026-10-22): conceal the opening callout until the first camera action fully settles on the rendered world. Start text reveal after arrival and never show an empty bubble. Preserve later camera cues, pause and reduced motion.

- Exponential Momentum playtest and cost preview (Eric, 2026-10-22): supersede flat +1/large early rapid bonuses. Each builder starts from 0.25 × 1.4^(prior combo count), adding +25% under 1s, +15% under 2s, +5% up to 4s, and +10% for three plays within two seconds, capped at 6 total per receipt and rounded to thousandths. Reserve remains uncapped; combo resets on team handoff, and spending does not reset it. Preserve decay toggle and bullet-time clock. Hover, keyboard focus or tap on an ability flashes its projected cost and remaining reserve at the elevated Momentum dial; insufficient costs are explicit and inspection never spends. Show hundredths for small reserve values.

- Sticky Paws self-buff (Eric, 2026-10-22): supersedes direct stealing from friendly/enemy foundations. Spend 3 Momentum to cast only on Mochi, applying a visible nonstacking Sticky Paws buff. Her next valid attack consumes it and steals the enemy target’s latest actual foundation card into the loose tableau alongside normal attack resolution; an emptied foundation receives the established Disarmed/rearm turn penalty. Other actors and invalid attacks never consume it. Show Self · Mochi on the ability and highlight only Mochi with an explicit Play Sticky Paws here cue while targeting. The buff itself grants no damage, stagger or Momentum.

- Sandbox control footer (Eric, 2026-10-22): place Pause/Resume and Restart alongside Auto-Solve, Best Move and Highlight in the footer. Remove the sandbox Deal a row button. Keep End Turn in that footer centered horizontally beneath the player foundations, with compact wrapping of left-hand controls on phones and no viewport overflow. Preserve turn eligibility, enemy Withdraw responses, ordinary encounter controls and restart behavior.

- Opening quest spotlight (Eric, 2026-10-22): once the opening callout and camera pan settle, dim the full map scene except the projected Find a way out ground card. Fade the spotlight overlay in smoothly over 1.2 seconds, including on return from inspection. Show a repeating passive hand/tap preview with “Tap here”; follow the live card and camera, freeze with pause and use a static cue under reduced motion. Hide during jumbo inspection and pickup, restore on closing inspection, and remove after docking. Preserve actual pickup and keyboard interactions.

- Tableau value priority (Eric, 2026-10-22): Khad Roots must never obstruct tableau values. Paint both the value ink and its semitransparent backsplash above the root elements. Legacy obscureValue bindings must not override this rule; supersedes the earlier opt-in value coverage rule.

- Sparse tableau Khad bush (Eric, 2026-10-22): use a separate ImageGen copy of the painted sparse bush with crimson/charcoal thorned Khad Roots coiling around its right branch. Preserve the healthy source and map scenery. Tint the tableau ground and ground-cover effects beneath and just beyond that branch in feathered dark reds and grays; keep the left side healthy and the separate fruit/glow presentation intact.

- Quest receipt progress (Eric, 2026-10-22): quest cards provide a lower-right progress dock for their tableau objectives. Update only from accepted gameplay progress. On collecting the sparse patch’s berry-bearing 8, pop the painted manaberry from the played card, flourish and fly it into First Fruits’ dock; increment its berry count on arrival before completing the quest card. Share progress presentation across world, jumbo and dock, preserve pause/reset cleanup and use quiet reduced motion.

- Opening quest pan (Eric, 2026-10-22): do not automatically highlight the destination tile when the camera pans to Find a way out. Preserve the world quest card’s own presentation and later quest interactions.

- Opening quest world presence (Eric, 2026-10-22): Find a way out is already laid on the sparse patch from the opening scene, before the camera pans toward it. Do not hide or spawn it at camera arrival or callout completion. Retain the opening pickup interaction gate and Hero reveal after docking. Supersedes the earlier placement timing after the first callout/pan.

- Simple Steps (Eric, 2026-10-22): after Hero starts her opening travel, complete Find a way out and discover Simple Steps in the den tableau on the dirt directly in front of its cave mouth. Text: “Click or drag cards that are one higher or one lower in value to Hero to navigate her out of her den.” Use overhead den artwork showing only Hero’s inquisitive head peeking outward. Keep all den traversal cards face-down and unplayable until Simple Steps finishes docking; use the shared world-to-jumbo-to-dock spin, arrival shimmer and then the existing tableau reveal.

- First berry instruction reveal (Eric, 2026-10-22): keep sparse berry tableau cards face-down until the Pick the first berry / First Fruits quest pickup finishes docking. Then play the existing arrival reveal. World quest previews must reflect the same saved edits as jumbo and dock views, including live authoring updates.

- Quest card travel (Eric, 2026-10-22): the existing jumbo-to-dock animation is the only approved card spin globally. All card spin travel uses its centered two-turn rotation, smoothstep position/size interpolation, 80px sine lift and matching contact shadow; do not substitute the former corner-pivot keyframe spin. World-to-jumbo, jumbo-to-dock, return travel and table quest arrivals share this motion. After a card finishes arriving in jumbo, play one shimmer across its face; never shimmer during the incoming flight. Preserve input locking during travel, pause, cancellation/restart cleanup, sharp world rendering and quiet reduced-motion transitions.

- Opening quest ground pickup (Eric, 2026-10-22): after the first callout and pan to the sparse manaberry patch, lay Find a way out on that tile, covering its label, inheriting the ground plane with a gentle irregular tilt. Render the ground prop at full card resolution before world scaling so its face stays sharp. Keep Hero’s holocard hidden until pickup finishes. First click/tap or keyboard activation opens the shared jumbo quest view. Activating the jumbo card flies it from that view into the quest dock; closing the view leaves it uncollected on the ground. Only then reveal the holocard and show its drag preview and allow opening travel. Supersedes travel-first automatic opening quest arrival.

- Flick sensitivity (Eric, 2026-10-22): triple the shared tableau flick launch velocity and maximum launch speed, giving the same recent gesture three times the unobstructed coast distance. Preserve friction, slow-drag/hold rejection, trajectory receiver checks and existing input safeguards.

- Compact callout editing (Eric, 2026-10-22): place the draggable callout editor beside its world callout, preferring the right or left when space permits and keeping it viewport-bounded on smaller screens. Keep the world clear without backdrop dimming or blur; use a compact editor with the callout ID in its title and all existing authoring and live-preview controls.

- Shared permanent authoring saves (Eric, 2026-10-22): Save callout, Save sequence, scene emotion Save, and other editing-tool saves persist authored project data on the development server and load on every testing device. Browser storage is only for preferences or recoverable drafts, never the sole destination of an authoring save. Report failures and retain drafts; show success only after disk persistence. Keep Apply/Preview session-only. Shared entries supersede legacy browser overrides; reset also persists across devices. Existing browser-only edits can be promoted by opening and saving them again. Supersedes earlier browser-local callout/camera/emotion saves.

- Foundation emoji faces (Eric, 2026-10-22): player foundation portraits use the existing Hero/Mochi/Banks watercolor emoji faces instead of holocard artwork. Default happy; brief 1.5-second stars on landing attacks, heart-eyes on finishing blows, upset on damage (prioritized over simultaneous attacks/Thorns), and persistent sad while downed. Pause reaction expiry during combat inspection/pause, reset with battle state, and keep portrait geometry stationary. Enemy artwork remains its own entity portrait.

- Ability hand and Momentum visibility (Eric, 2026-10-22): elevate the Momentum dial above the ability-menu dimmer alongside the foundations. Place a Close button beneath the dial with clear separation. Widen the ability hand, using rotation plus vertical offsets for a natural arc and more horizontal face exposure. Keep Withdraw separate from the hand and preserve actor focus, paused reserve, legal target dragging and responsive viewport bounds.

- Foundation polish and receipt motion (Eric, 2026-10-22): keep all foundation content inside its border with readable, full-height meter labels and consistent portrait/value alignment. Ability-menu foundation hosts must not inherit sandbox shell padding or clipping. Sandbox tableau receipt flights land on the numeric foundation value tile; unified actor portraits remain stationary during their automatic receipt-triggered attacks. Preserve enemy hit feedback and ordinary encounter motion.

- Foundation-focused ability fan (Eric, 2026-10-22): keep foundations fully lit above ability-menu dimming and allow mouse, touch and keyboard selection. Selecting a player foundation centers its ability group and puts it above the still-visible cards belonging to other actors; selection does not cast or alter combat targeting. Withdraw remains a separate high-layer utility card beside the fan (below and beside it on phones), preserving its zero-cost tableau-targeted cast. Pause, drag hitboxes, unavailable states and keyboard cancellation remain intact.

- Withdraw ability card (Eric, 2026-10-22): replace the standalone Withdraw button with a zero-Momentum team maneuver card in the Momentum fan, available in every combat sandbox scenario. Drag it onto the tableau (or select its Tableau keyboard target) to redeal the entire tableau and end the team’s turn. Preserve existing Withdraw rules and animations; no attack or Momentum is earned.

- Foundation ownership and Sticky Paws (Eric, 2026-10-22): sandbox foundations share a strong bordered title, left portrait, HP/stagger/status meters and a right-hand current card value, with floating statuses above. Use character artwork without the holocard frame. Track actual foundation stacks. Mochi’s Sticky Paws costs 3 Momentum and pulls the latest actual card from any living friendly or enemy foundation onto the loose tableau; it does no damage, stagger or Momentum generation. Loose cards are top cards but require normal golf adjacency. Remove all automatic stagger spills. Taking the only card leaves an empty Disarmed foundation; its owner spends its next team turn only taking the lowest-value exposed tableau card, without an attack or Momentum, and remains unable to play cards or abilities that turn. These rules supersede earlier automatic spill guidance.

- Ability cast travel/impact (Eric, 2026-10-22): valid ability drops launch the card from its actor along the actual targeting trail to the chosen enemy. Apply the skill once at impact, then give the target a red variant of the existing holofoil bloom. Contact shimmer is card-only. Lightly rebound the card and destroy it with the shared enemy defeat particles and evaporation animation. Expose launch/trail/impact/destroy phases for future hooks. Lock gameplay through flight; pause, restart cleanup and reduced motion remain supported.

- Ability contact holofoil (Eric, 2026-10-22): use the existing received-card holofoil bloom (shared rainbow gradients, color-dodge and food-foil-bloom animation) at valid ability/target contact. Expand from the contact origin across card and target; no new ripple animation and no idle inspection shimmer. Preserve the shared reduced-motion quiet flash. Supersedes the custom contact wave.

- Momentum icon artwork (Eric, 2026-10-22): use the approved watercolor Spiraling Ember for shared Momentum identity and spending/cost displays in Proto/combat sandbox, and Comet Spark for incoming Momentum receipts. Retain text labels, accessible names, numeric reserve, dial rings and existing gameplay. Both approved studies and transparent runtime sprites are retained under `public/assets/{sources,runtime}/ui/momentum/`; see ART.md.

- Ability contact shimmer (Eric, 2026-10-22): ability cards do not shimmer during idle/inspection. On entering a valid drag overlap, emit a single outward shimmer wave from the detected card/target contact point across the card and enemy highlight. Do not restart it on every pointer move; retrigger on leaving/re-entering or switching targets. Reduced motion uses a static contact glow.

- Ability card drop bounds (Eric, 2026-10-22): detect targets using the dragged ability card’s full transformed visual bounds, not only the pointer. Any edge contact qualifies; use the same bounds for hover highlight and release, compensating for pointer movement since the last render. When several living enemy foundations overlap the card, choose the largest overlap deterministically. Invalid drops remain free.

- Ability-card fan interaction (Eric, approved 2026-10-22): overlay a fan of original ability cards above Momentum without changing tableau layout. Hover/tap inspects (highlight, shimmer, slight magnification and frontmost stacking); it never casts. Unavailable cards remain gray and inspectable with reasons. Hold 280ms to pull an available card free, hide fan/dimming, and drag onto highlighted living enemy foundations. Release on a valid target casts atomically and spends once; invalid release/cancel restores the fan without spending. Combat stays paused through inspection/targeting. Escape/backdrop/Back closes inspection; keyboard Enter arms target selection and accessible target buttons cast. Preserve actor targeting and all existing rule gates. This supersedes immediate radial-button casting.

- Momentum ability radial (Eric, 2026-10-22): hide skill buttons beneath foundations. Clicking/tapping the Momentum dial opens a keyboard-accessible modal radial with a dimmed world, shared reserve, actor/target names, skill costs, effects and unavailable reasons. Skills still unlock only in 005. Choosing an affordable skill uses its existing actor target and closes the radial; Escape, backdrop or Back returns without spending. Freeze combat timers and effects while choosing. Preserve Decay as a separate control and existing skill rules.

- Stagger halo weight (Eric, 2026-10-22): double the halo stroke from 1px to 2px and embolden its orbiting stars; preserve the halo footprint, portrait-edge position and bullet-time rotation speed.

- Halo and decay-tip refinement (Eric, 2026-10-22): center the tilted stagger halo vertically on the portrait’s top edge so half overlaps the portrait and half floats above it. Add a bright white/cyan sparkler at the live Momentum arc’s decaying endpoint, following the active overfill ring as necessary. Keep its five-ray budget bounded, pause animation with combat, use a static core in reduced motion, and hide it when decay is disabled, frozen, or reserve is empty.

- Stagger halo/debuff (Eric, 2026-10-22): stars orbit in a tilted perspective halo just above the affected enemy portrait, with a 26-second revolution (90% slower than the former 2.6-second spin). Pause freezes the orbit; reduced motion keeps a static tilted halo. Represent the existing stagger state as an explicit Staggered debuff icon in the entity status row, with stage and custom hover/tap details for stun, vulnerability and bullet time. Remove it on recovery/defeat. Do not add a second duration or change gameplay state.

- Stagger bullet time (Eric, 2026-10-22): while any living sandbox enemy is staggered, advance the shared gameplay clock at 10% speed (90% slower). Apply this once at tickBattle so Momentum decay, optional decay-freeze durations and rapid-sequence timing use the same clock, and future gameplay timers inherit it. Multiple staggered enemies do not stack slowdown. Defeat/end of stagger restores normal speed; untimed sandbox stagger remains untimed. Keep input, animations, callout reading and pause responsive. Add a fixed-budget twelve-mote CSS bullet-time overlay using transform/opacity; no particle simulation loop. Pause freezes motes; reduced motion uses six static motes. Ordinary encounters retain their authored clock.

- Design preference — customizable Momentum skills (Eric, 2026-10-21): deeply customizable ability modifiers are a desired direction. Keep the base rule that Momentum abilities do not generate Momentum, but support downstream engine interactions rather than treating it as a prohibition on all feedback. Example desired modifier: freeze Momentum decay for five seconds after Pounce. Preserve this preference for future skill/loadout work; the example is not a default Pounce effect.

- Scenario 005: Break and Finish (Eric, 2026-10-21): dormant 24-HP Ironbark Sentinel, six stagger, distinct branching deal across both foundations. Hero’s Bash costs 3 shared Momentum for 2 stagger and zero damage; Mochi’s Pounce costs 6 for 3 damage, doubled to 6 against staggered targets. Skills use existing actor targets, never generate Momentum, and leave foundation ranks/tableau unchanged except a Bash-triggered stagger spill. Compact skill buttons beneath actors show cost and custom tooltip details; no extra targeting mode. Keep Decay toggle. A typed optional modifier seam supports decay freezes without enabling any by default.

- Ironbark stagger presentation (Eric, 2026-10-21): remove the Ironbark buff icon while the enemy is staggered instead of showing a broken/exposed variant. Retain the reserved buff-row space so the foundation does not jump. Damage immunity remains inactive while staggered.

- Viewport-bounded sandbox (Eric, 2026-10-21): no sandbox scrollbars or content beyond the viewport. Keep header/solver at native size and fit the complete board into its remaining stage, reserving enemy stagger and spill space up front. Retain readable buried peeks, all three foundations, legal spill access and controls. Adapt the entire composition on short viewports instead of collapsing the tableau alone. Target arrows account for stage scaling; callout/FX anchors use rendered bounds. This supersedes scroll-overflow behavior.

- Sandbox buried-card readability (Eric, 2026-10-21): expose 32px per buried row on desktop and 28px on phones, with a correspondingly larger minimum rack height. Show complete buried rank indices; keep stagger-stable card widths and scroll overflow.

- Stagger layout stability (Eric, 2026-10-21): sandbox tableau card width depends on available width and foundation size, never shrinking vertical space after spills. Reserve a readable rack height and fixed buried peeks; stagger spill rows and expanding combat chrome add scrollable board content on short/mobile screens instead of collapsing cards. Keep spills playable and foundations, controls and solver reachable.

- Buff icons and stagger lesson (Eric, 2026-10-21): enemy buffs occupy a reserved floating row above their foundation with thorn/bark SVG artwork and bespoke hover/focus/mobile-tap tooltip details. Do not clip or nest the interactive icons inside the foundation button. Add 004: Ironbark Khad Root (10 HP, dormant, six-point stagger). Ironbark blocks all HP damage until already staggered; blocked attacks still add stagger and Momentum. The breaking hit deals zero; subsequent vulnerable hits deal damage. Show the bark guard as broken while staggered. Give 004 its own descending A→K→Q route across distinct columns, varied deterministic backfill/spills, separate from 001–003; preserve unique redeals and deep links.

- Combat scenario deep links (Eric, 2026-10-21): `proto.html?combatsbx&scenario=003` opens scenario 003 directly; accept catalog IDs 001/002/003 and fall back to 001 for missing or unknown IDs. Ordinary encounter query behavior remains independent.

- Attached combat reactions (Eric, 2026-10-21): damage callout tails must touch the affected actor’s foundation top edge. Use a compact bubble immediately above that foundation, following its live position and clamping horizontally on phones. Do not search free tableau space for combat reactions. Keep the existing emoji, brief lifetime and pause behavior; the transient reaction passes gameplay clicks through. Other dialogue retains free-space placement.

- Scenario victory timing (Eric, 2026-10-21): show the sandbox completion modal only after all pending enemy defeat burst animations finish, including their impact delay. Await actual animation completion, respecting combat/global pause. Reduced motion skips the wait because it omits these animations. Restart invalidates old completion callbacks; gameplay ends immediately on victory while its presentation finishes.

- Sandbox legal-card highlighting (Eric, 2026-10-21): add a Highlight button toggle beside Best Move, off by default. Only when enabled, give currently eligible exposed tableau/spill cards a white border. Preserve selection, keyboard focus, legal play and drag behavior. The preference remains during scenario replay/selection within the mounted sandbox; a fresh load defaults off.

- Combat team layout (Eric, 2026-10-21): center the shared Momentum dial horizontally in battle-field__status above its action row with compact reserve/pace/decay controls. Hero and Mochi foundations sit adjacent with a third empty, inert foundation slot. Empty slots are presentation only, never legal recipients, actors or drag targets. Keep phone layouts, targeting, damage callouts, overfill, and ordinary encounters working.

- Combat damage reactions (Eric, 2026-10-21): resolved HP damage to a player actor triggers a brief existing Callout with that actor’s watercolor upset emoji, anchored live to their foundation. Thorns gets a specific sting reaction; a downed actor uses sadness. Positive damage events only, once per event; repeated hits replace the speaker’s previous reaction. Bubbles avoid actor foundations, pause with combat, and clear on restart/scenario changes or battle completion so the result modal takes priority. Preserve actual damage and gameplay.

- Momentum visual overfill (Eric, 2026-10-21): above the existing three-point visual threshold, show excess charge in expanding concentric rings with warm glow and escaping sparks, plus an explicit Overfill surplus. Each ring represents another three points; beyond three outer rings the last ring advances through further laps and the label shows total filled thresholds. Keep the gauge footprint bounded on phones, reserve uncapped, and decay/spending reflected immediately. Pause freezes sparks; reduced motion uses static sparks. This changes presentation only.

- Approved Mochi holocard (Eric, 2026-10-21): use the 10% larger-face version based on the painting before the rejected eye-reduction experiment. Remove the other holocard variants; see ART.md.

- Battle chrome reduction (Eric, 2026-10-21): remove the battle-field__status-text section and its turn/instruction/outcome copy. Retain battle controls and the sandbox completion modal.

- Enemy intent summaries (Eric, 2026-10-21): show each living enemy’s intent as a separate icon control beneath its foundation, with custom parchment tooltip details on hover, keyboard focus or mobile tap. Attack summaries show damage, target portrait and name with actor color; Withdraw targets Tableau, Dormant has No target, and concealed intents reveal neither action nor actor. Explain dormant inactivity, passive Thorns reflection, full-tableau Withdraw/interruption, and actual attack damage/target selection. Keep all other globally suppressed tooltips suppressed.

- Combat scenario completion (Eric, 2026-10-21): finished sandbox battles show a keyboard/touch-accessible modal offering Replay scenario or Next scenario in selector order, using fresh scenario state. The final scenario explicitly offers Back to scenario 001. Show the scenario name and victory/defeat outcome; background controls are inert while choosing. Ordinary encounters retain their existing result flow.

- Rapid Momentum playtest (Eric, 2026-10-21): legal sandbox receipts earn base +1, plus +2 for gaps under 1s, +1 for 1–under 2s, +0.5 for 2–4s, otherwise no speed bonus. First receipt earns +1. Three receipts within a rolling two-second window add +0.5, maximum +3.5 per receipt. Actors share timing; illegal actions earn nothing. Active player-turn time drives pace even with Decay off or an empty reserve; pauses and enemy responses freeze it. Handoff resets pace history. Show actual gains and rapid/sustained feedback. Keep decay and interrupt cost; no abilities. Supersedes fixed +1 earnings.

- Combat training scenarios and full Withdraw (Eric, 2026-10-21): the sandbox has an upper-left selector defaulting to 001: Dormant Khad Root (10 HP, no active attacks), 002: Thorns Khad Root (4 HP, Thorns reflects exactly 1 damage onto each attacker per attack, including the killing blow), and 003: Bad Deal (10-HP Dormant Khad Root with no legal opening card plays). Scenario selection and restart restore fresh actors, resources and scenario state. In 003, END TURN cannot bypass the first Withdraw lesson.
