## Tableau layout workshop (Eric, 2026-10-17)

- Tooling → Tableaus authors reusable travel layouts with free card positions, rotation and drawing layers, explicit named coverage stacks (buried-to-exposed order), and fixed A–K or deterministic random ranks. Geometry and play blocking are independent; card positions and fitting stay stable as cards leave.
- Assign layouts to terrain types, with sparse berries as a distinct target. Assigned travel crossings use the authored card count while retaining normal stamina, AP, discovery, finite harvest and rank-reset rules. Explicit passages keep their own deals. Gathering, rest and combat retain their specialized generators. Unassigned terrain retains existing deals, including the approved sparse cluster.
- Include a berry-cluster starter, creation/duplication/deletion, pointer dragging and numeric placement, undo/redo, local draft recovery, validation, JSON import/export, and an interactive seeded foundation/coverage test. Random values are independent A–K samples and need not produce a solvable deal.
- Apply to playtest stores a separate browser-local override for new journeys/restarts; clear it to return to repository defaults. Save authored data validates and writes only the layout catalog and assignments to src/data/tableauLayouts.json through the trusted development endpoint. Never serialize session card removals, ranks, resources or progression. Custom free-positioned travel deals use the fitted tray even when the optional world-station trial is enabled.

## Proto authoring surface (Eric, 2026-10-17)

- `/tooling.html` is a Proto-only tile/tableau authoring workspace. Use ProtoVariant, its actual map, shared TileEditor, tile schema/validation and runtime generators. Do not expose the legacy Kin, ability, encounter or combat-log editors here; their existence elsewhere in the expansive codebase does not make them requested or shipping features.
- Start in the authored-map sandbox with tile editing open and gameplay paused. Keep map selection and a bounded authoring workspace simultaneously visible on desktop and phone. Select actual authored tiles by map or selector; edit identity/footprint, traversal overrides and passage rules, population and gathering tuning, arrival/discovery/flags, road prints and scenery lights, with separate shared terrain defaults.
- Provide travel-layout authoring in the same workspace, with selected tile/type context. Type assignments affect every matching Proto tile; passages, gathering, rest and fishing retain their existing specialized rules. Show generated gathering/rest samples through Proto's materializeTiles and shared card/template components. Explain the actual type-specific generator and scripted-day overrides instead of exposing unrelated engine ideas as supported properties.
- Tile Apply changes only the current Proto session and survives its scenario restart through the existing applied-document mechanism. Tile Save and Export use the existing authored-data boundary, excluding session deals and progression. Keep tile commands distinct from layout catalog commands. Closing discards unapplied tile drafts and permits playtesting the applied state. This supersedes the generic entity-tooling entry point.

## Den exit traversal trial (Eric, 2026-10-17)

- Trial a separate six-card tableau from the Den center to its north mouth before the destination crossing. Use the map-tableau presentation automatically for this stage, with fixed pins on the two flanks and four across the arch, a smooth close frontal camera swing, and a complete adjacent-rank progression from the incoming foundation. Solving earns the half-tile exit; the berry approach follows with its existing stamina and finite harvest. The Den permits entry/exit only through its north face. General eight-way tile traversal remains under consideration; retain orthogonal routing elsewhere. This is temporary gameplay/presentation scaffolding and supersedes the optional-map-trial default for this Den stage only.

- Present the Den exit trial in the separate tableau field with a fixed frontal replica of the painted cave. Fit the cave and six card pins together: four across the arch and one on each lower flank; keep their positions stable as cards leave. Use the shared tableau template, foundations and normal play/solver rules. Remove the exit-specific map camera shot and automatic map-tableau activation; the puzzle composition supplies its own solving view. Preserve the north-only exit, earned movement and subsequent berry crossing. This supersedes the map presentation and exit-camera portions of the previous trial; general eight-way traversal remains deferred.

- Tableau biome continuity and Den background (Eric, 2026-10-17): give the cave tableau the shared painted landscape floor/ground-cover and atmospheric backdrop behind its single frontal cave composition. On completing a travel deal, retain the final crossing biome rather than falling back to the origin/Den; keep that scene through arrival until the actor moves to another biome. Preserve depletion and finite rewards.

- Immediate sparse-berry dialogue (Eric, 2026-10-17): start Hero’s nearby-bush reflection on the final legal sparse-tableau play, without waiting for the earned walking buffer to settle. New travel restores movement-facing.

Keep same-cell no-ops, legal/discovery routing, scenario rails and stamina checks. Retain preview/waypoint scaffolding for reinstatement. Blocked journeys stay halted until a new valid destination drop starts a checked route directly; do not reopen OK!/X automatically. This is a temporary playtest flow change.

## Ordered traversal and point-of-interest iteration (Eric, 2026-10-17)

- Leaving the sparse patch adds a six-card, solvable, reward-free exit deal in the planned direction. For that journey, present each exit and destination traversal deal separately in route order; unlock the next deal only when actual earned walking reaches its boundary. Keep the tile biome visible until Hero crosses its threshold, including depletion. Split the existing crossing STA estimate between the two halves instead of adding cost; retain rank, AP, streak and reserves across stage handoffs. Use bounded acceleration and finite braking into earned endpoints rather than an asymptotic motion tail.
- Automatically open the authored point-of-interest tableau at arrival. Traversal earns movement; that gathering/event deal earns its own resources/event progression without movement. Preserve the finite sparse harvest. Carry Hero’s travel rank forward, and register a fresh scripted gathering ladder to that incoming rank once, retaining its reward positions and subsequent saved deal. Origin/departure, route-direction and stage-isolation scaffolding initially apply to journeys leaving the sparse patch; explicit passages and other existing journey generators retain their current behavior.

- Travel solver scopes (Eric, 2026-10-17): replace travel Auto-Solve with Auto-S Tile and Auto-S All. Tile captures the current tile’s outgoing threshold and stops earning cards there, allowing earned walking to finish without playing the next tile. All continues through the ordered travel stages to the destination; point-of-interest deals remain separate. Preserve Best Move, Stop, legal plays, resources, pause and blocking rules.

- Cave threshold presentation handoff (Eric, 2026-10-17): the final cave exit card immediately switches both cards and tableau scenery to the next deal, independently of Hero’s animated walking progress. Keep actual movement card-earned and preserve Auto-S Tile stopping at that threshold. This supersedes movement-gated cave scenery only; the sparse-patch exit retains its existing boundary behavior.

- Unified actor holocard (Eric, 2026-10-17): keep Hero’s single live holocard in the map’s upper-left corner. It replaces the travel/gathering actor foundation face and compact actor popup, shows the active foundation rank and resource receipts, receives tableau card flights, opens full inspection on activation, and initiates the normal checked travel gesture when dragged. Keep tableau controls and the distinct rest stock receiver available.

- Actor card value identity (Eric, 2026-10-17): print the live foundation value prominently inside the actor holocard’s lower identity band beside the actor’s name. Replace the detached upper-right rank bubble; retain artwork, card proportions, live rank, inspection, receiving feedback and movement gestures.

Remove the selected definition from src/engine/actors.ts, preserve unrelated source and shared artwork, and keep current gameplay catalogs protected. Through the trusted local development server, persist removed IDs in src/data/deletedActorCatalogIds.json so roster- and art-generated entries stay absent across browsers, reloads, exports and builds. Legacy actors additionally lose their source definition. For generated entries retain scripted gameplay definitions and shared art, and state that scope in the editor and confirmation. Failed writes keep the entry visible; support deleting the final catalog entry. Hosted builds explain that repository writes require the local development server.

- Proto actor editor roster (Eric, 2026-10-25): scope ?actorEditor to exactly Hero, Banks, Mochi, Cir and Dem, in that order. This supersedes discovery from legacy actors, enemies, other Keru, runtime artwork and deletion records. Keep those five available regardless of old catalog deletion records; artwork discovery supplies assets only. Remove the catalog-delete controls from this focused editor. Preserve text editing, JSON export, per-actor art review and shared 3D previews.

- Nature tableau affinity study (Eric, 2026-10-27): iterate Nature first as a dedicated organic perimeter adornment: irregular climbing vines, varied leaves and tendrils projecting beyond the face, with open gaps instead of a uniform rectangular border. Retain existing painted suit assets and readable ranks/overlapping previews. Other elemental perimeter treatments remain future work.

- Adorned tableau refinement (Eric, 2026-10-27): let organic growth occupy corners as well as edges while preserving rank readability. Hide the separate affinity glyph on adorned cards (currently Nature), retaining accessible affinity text and the original art assets. A centered affinity glyph replacing a hidden rank is a possible future use, not enabled by this change.

- Fire tableau affinity study (Eric, 2026-10-27): extend the organic perimeter treatment to Fire with irregular curling flame tongues, broken ember trails, varied sizes, corner coverage and a few outward sparks. Keep ranks and overlap previews readable, use static artwork, and omit the separate Fire glyph on adorned cards while retaining accessible affinity text and the original asset.

- Fire flame direction refinement (Eric, 2026-10-27): flames should predominantly point inward toward the card face, including the side and top clusters; retain asymmetric spacing, open gaps and rank clearance.

- Fire billowing refinement (Eric, 2026-10-27): replace vinelike connecting trails and repeated candle/leaf-shaped flames with broad, irregular billowing fire fronts, deep inward curls and layered amber/gold heat inspired by the shared Momentum spiral. Preserve inward direction, open gaps and rank clarity.

- Water/Air tableau affinity studies (Eric, 2026-10-27): Water uses irregular rolling blue wave fronts, inward foam curls and sparse spray; Air uses open, sweeping pale mint ribbons and fine spiral gusts. Keep asymmetric perimeter and corner coverage, inward flow and rank clearance. Both replace their separate tableau glyph with accessible affinity text while retaining original assets.

- Fire/Water brush texture (Eric, 2026-10-27): break up clean vector contours with static irregular brush edges, pigment grain and layered wash texture. Remove crisp outline strokes on fire and waves; render seafoam and hot highlights as broken dry-brush accents. Keep existing inward billows, readable ranks and separate affinity assets.

- Fire/Water texture restraint (Eric, 2026-10-27): keep brush texture subtle. Reduce edge displacement and pigment contrast, omit the noisy wash overlay, and use longer foam/highlight strokes with smaller breaks so the broad painted shapes remain cohesive.

- Earth tableau affinity study (Eric, 2026-10-27): use broken ochre stone and packed-soil strata around the perimeter, with varied faceted rocks, fractured corners, open gaps and restrained brush texture. Keep rank clearance and interaction surfaces; replace the separate Earth glyph with accessible affinity text and retain the original asset.

- Combat progression iteration (Eric, 2026-10-29): The 100-series sandbox uses a branching encounter map with 2–5 authored destinations and visible reward-theme motifs. A won encounter offers three themed rewards with rarity scaling; choose one and carry it with the party into the next 100-series encounter. Debug controls can inspect other themes and scenes. Fire, nature, earth, water, air, shock, light, dark, chaos and order rewards join ice; their initial values and shared effect families are provisional pending playtesting. Frost encounters use restrained enemy versions of ice powers. Scenario 102 is Frost Sentry, 104 Rime Pair and 105 Frost Warden.

- Elemental reward identity (Eric, 2026-10-29): Elemental abilities should create distinct strategic and tableau decisions, rather than reuse generic effects with different motifs. Fire emphasizes escalation, ice control, nature growth, earth preparation, water adaptation, air reach, shock charge timing, light coordination, dark sacrifice, chaos managed uncertainty, and order deliberate patterns. The newly authored generic rewards remain provisional scaffolding until redesigned and implemented; see docs/design/keru-elements/reward-redesign-proposal.md for working proposals, not validated balance.

- Fire and Nature reward redesign direction (Eric, 2026-10-29): Rename Ember Cadence to Incite; remove Hearthguard. Heart of the Phoenix revives downed Hero, damages and stuns all enemies, with rarity-dependent damage, healing and recharge; Common permanently breaks after use. Move Cinder Road's rank-gap mechanic to Nature with a new motif. Regrowth heals per successful moves and may scale exponentially with consecutive streaks. BLOOM plants a 10 HP allied seed foundation that cannot play golf, gains BLOOMING each turn and becomes a Wildcard after a rarity-dependent number of turns; show played Wildcard interpretations using the horizontal split double-value system. Thorns damages attackers for a limited duration. These are redesign requirements, not yet implemented or numerically balanced; see docs/design/keru-elements/reward-redesign-proposal.md.

- Earth and Nature tableau direction (Eric, 2026-10-29): Explore Fossilize as damage-lost-to-HP becoming armor over subsequent turns, replacing samey Stone Shell. Resonance starts on a targeted tableau card and propagates through adjacent (including diagonal) ±1 ranks, with damage scaling by affected cards. Bedrock locks and converts a row to Rock; a roots/vines Nature ability locks and converts a column to Nature. These are redesign requirements or candidates, not implemented balance; resolve movement interactions and Rock versus the existing Earth element. See docs/design/keru-elements/reward-redesign-proposal.md.

- Tableau lock initial testing direction (Eric, 2026-10-29): A locked row stays on the tableau instead of being automatically shifted off, and its cards are locked to the applied element. During automatic movement, unlocked columns shift down while a locked column stays in place; its cards are likewise locked to the applied element. A conversion to an element the locked type is weak to is a possible exception, not yet confirmed. These rules are design direction for testing, not yet implemented; see docs/design/keru-elements/reward-redesign-proposal.md for remaining movement details.

- Dewi Whiteout reward presentation (Eric, 2026-10-29): the snowy owl Keru is named Dewi; Whiteout is its ability. Reward cards emphasize creature name above a styled ability name, use larger faces with rarity color and an elemental badge, and expose ability descriptions on hover or inspection. Dewi uses separate painted owl, winter scene, rear and front whiteout layers with card parallax. Preserve reward selection and drag-to-Hero behavior.

- First redesigned rewards (Eric, 2026-10-29): 100-series rewards now offer Incite, Regrowth and Fossilize as an initial playable pass. Incite builds Heat with receipts and increases chip damage at 2 Heat; Heat clears at turn end (Legendary retains 1). Regrowth heals every three receipts, with longer same-turn sequences raising the amount within rarity caps. Fossilize is active for the first two enemy turns of each encounter (three at Epic), turns accumulated HP lost after shields into armor at rarity-dependent ratios, and caps this armor at 8; lethal damage does not create armor. Values are provisional. Retired Fire, Nature and Earth placeholders are excluded from reward rolls while replacements are being built, so those themes currently offer one reward each.

- Dewi Glacial Lance art (Eric, 2026-10-29): Glacial Lance identifies Dewi as its Keru and uses the approved lance-first dive with separate painted rear motion and foreground snow/ice planes. Reuse the shared holocard parallax and respect reduced motion; show the current tier description and cost without changing the combat rules.

- Keru ability ownership (Eric, 2026-10-29): Keru abilities can be acquired as rewards and used independently; their owning Keru need not be summoned, familiarized, or present as a tableau foundation. Ability ownership identifies the source and art, not a prerequisite for use. Dewi owns Whiteout and Glacial Lance; the polar-bear Demi owns Ice Armor and Rime Retort, whose defensive and retaliation mechanics suit a tank. Preserve existing reward carry and combat behavior. This supersedes the earlier prohibition on Dem/Demi art in ice rewards.

- 100-series reward handoff (Eric, 2026-10-30): Use the existing Hero battlefield foundation as the reward drop target, hide unusable solver and turn controls, and position the foundation below the offerings. Dragged cards track the pointer smoothly; obtaining the required reward immediately opens next-scene selection.

- Completed combat presentation (Eric, 2026-10-30): Once the scenario result is presented, hide enemy foundations, tableau cards and combat targeting lines while retaining the party foundation for rewards. Reward previews use the same clear board.

- 100-series path selection (Eric, 2026-10-30): Show destination choices in a compact vertical list with no default selection. Clicking an available destination immediately starts that encounter with the selected reward.

- Opening sustain and early additions (Eric, 2026-10-30): The first reward offer after scenario 100 presents Fossilize, Heart of the Phoenix and Ice Armor. Heart of the Phoenix revives Hero, damages all enemies and stuns survivors; Common breaks permanently while higher tiers recharge after Hero receipts. Thorns retaliates against attackers for the first three enemy turns of each encounter, with damage based on rarity. These are initial tuning values for playtesting; other redesign abilities remain under development.

- Book den transition (Eric, 2026-11-01): In the isolated book demo, hovering the cave on spread 01 gently highlights it; clicking it folds the scenery and relief grass in a stagger, lifts the cave above the turning page, then sets it down and glides it towards the viewer while the den interior rises behind it. Respect reduced motion and preserve ordinary page and map navigation.

- Book cave on spread 01 (Eric, 2026-11-01): Center the den across the gutter as two registered image halves, each hinged and flattened on its own page. Keep the joined hover and cave-entry transition intact.

- Book den diorama and page layout (Eric, 2026-11-01): The den is built from independently unfolding rock layers and hinged side leaves extending beyond the book; do not use a flat painted backdrop or grass inside. Distribute ground clutter across the usable page outside each story rectangle, and support scene-authored story positions throughout a spread.

- Book foldout correction (Eric, 2026-11-01): Side panels fold upward and outward around their page-edge crease, with counter-hinged reliefs, rather than rotating in the horizontal page plane. Support one-based direct spread links such as /book.html?page=04 for the den; invalid page values open spread 01.

- Den camera and continuous enclosure (Eric, 2026-11-01): As the den unfolds, lower the view towards the front edge of the book. Use overlapping continuous rock arches and connected side walls around an open interior; eliminate separated cropped rock standees and exposed straight crop boundaries.

- Den enclosure and secondary leaves (Eric, 2026-11-01): Fill the rear of the cave, overlap wall joins with smaller rock reliefs, carry dirt/stone ground cover across the floor and both side leaves, and add secondary panels on the side foldouts that unfold towards the camera. Include foreground ceiling/wall layers to extend the enclosure towards the lower page.

- Den fold camera and diagnostics (Eric, 2026-11-01): Fold/unfold drives the den camera between an overhead flattened view and a close low unfolded view; manual camera input can interrupt it. Show a compact selectable live position, target, yaw, pitch, height, distance and FOV readout. Side reliefs must flatten completely using independent mounting and fold transforms.

- Rock popup silhouettes (Eric, 2026-11-01): Most natural popup elements have irregular material-appropriate panel boundaries. Cave ceilings need angular rock contours on both outer crests and inner openings, rather than smooth uniform arch cuts.

- Universal book fold envelope (Eric, 2026-11-01): Every popup and foldout must pack into a thin fully flattened state within its assigned page, with a .18 world-unit clearance at outer edges and gutter. Oversized scenery may compress during the final folding phase and restore its full geometry on opening. Use the same packing in live folds and page-texture bakes; verify geometry bounds rather than relying on a camera view. This is a digital presentation mechanism, not a physically manufacturable paper construction.

- Book prose readability (Eric, 2026-11-01): Story rectangles lift and billboard at grazing camera angles, settle towards print at overhead angles, and use the shared depth-aware peek-through mechanic with generous margins to clear overlapping props, including nearly coplanar folded scenery. Keep raised prose within the viewport; page-turn bakes retain the printed flat placement.

- Book text states (Eric, 2026-11-01): Text defaults to locked-to-page: flat printed placement, with no billboarding, popup lift or camera-driven movement. Retain peek-through for legibility. Keep camera-popup as an opt-in dynamic state on named text panels for future scene wiring.

- Book text clearance correction (Eric, 2026-11-01): Peek-through for prose follows the actual text surface in perspective, with a small local margin. Do not use a large screen-aligned actor ellipse for text: rotating the book must not erase remote props or ground. Keep printed text depth-tested against the scene.

- Book page 01 forest relief (Eric, 2026-11-01): Swap the upper-right pine and den; add twelve pines of varied sizes while retaining the half-scale popup trial. Use the forest lab's fixed single-image depth displacement for pines and Hero, with book camera yaw constrained to ±30 degrees. Do not import facing changes, alternate poses or temporal blur. Keep relief geometry compatible with shadows, peek-through, flat-page bakes and fold packing.

- Book performance diagnostics (Eric, 2026-11-01): Show a compact FPS, mean/peak frame time, draw/triangle count and scene CPU readout. Cache settled popup poses and static shadows, batch flat ground and grass rendering while preserving individual fold sources, and throttle diagnostic DOM updates. Aim for 60 FPS during camera movement; software-rendered headless checks do not certify hardware frame rate.

- Book camera movement (Eric, 2026-11-01): WASD pans the book camera along the ground plane relative to its view, moving the camera and orbit target together. Permit zooming four times closer than the former 11-unit minimum (new minimum 2.75); retain the existing maximum distance and yaw limit. Manual pan/zoom interrupts camera choreography.

- Book Hero artwork (Eric, 2026-11-01): Use the elevated front pose from characters/hero/rig/watercolor/v4/hero-directions-high-body-v4.png, cropped to its registered [44,43,194,228] atlas cell. Retain book popup sizing and folding. The old holocard portrait's depth estimate must not be applied to this different pose; a matching depth estimate remains unauthored.

- Book shot notebook and cover (Eric, 2026-11-01): Adapt Proto's shot/flight/hold/easing pattern to native world-space book camera snapshots (position, look-at target and FOV). Capture, name, time, preview, pause, stop and share shots through the compact Shots notebook; persist drafts browser-locally and support JSON import/export. Manual camera input releases shot playback. Page 00 is the physical hinged front cover and opens into page 01 without renumbering the existing spreads. Cover copy: Hero's Journey / The adventure of a very good girl. / retain the icon / A story waiting to unfold.

- Book mouse controls (Eric, 2026-11-01): Match Proto: middle mouse drag rotates, left mouse is reserved for object/page interaction, right mouse drag and WASD pan, wheel zooms. Suppress middle-click browser autoscroll; preserve existing touch gestures.

- Book scene authoring navigator (Eric, 2026-11-01): A compact left-edge panel organizes chapters, page-bound scenes and camera shots. Single click selects; right-click offers Go to, Rename and Copy as. Allow adding chapters/scenes, dragging shots before/after other shots or across chapters, and precise up/down reordering. Save to disk writes validated scene/shot documents atomically to src/data/bookScenes.json through the trusted local development server, with revision-conflict checks. Preserve recoverable browser drafts on failures; hosted builds consume the saved file. This is scene and camera authoring data; the page artwork/geometry remains authored in the book scene code.

- Book context camera update (Eric, 2026-11-01): The scene tree context menu includes Save/update current camera. Update the selected shot (or a scene's opening shot; for a chapter, its current-page scene), preserving existing name/timing/easing and other shots, then save the scene document to disk. Create an opening shot if needed; retain the updated browser draft if the disk save fails.

- Book page authoring toolkit (Eric, 2026-11-01): Provide a right-edge Page editor beside the scene navigator. Allow selecting, adding, moving, resizing and editing text and watercolor popup props directly on the book, with precise properties, locking, visibility, duplication/deletion, snapping and undo/redo. Text defaults to fixed printed placement; camera-popup is opt-in. Preserve fold packing and printed page-turn artwork for edited controls. Save validated page overrides atomically to src/data/bookPages.json, with revision checks and recoverable browser drafts; camera/chapter data remains in bookScenes.json. Per-page atmospheric height fog reuses the forest/live-paint lab shader approach, with color, density, height, softness and darkness controls. This extends the earlier code-only artwork baseline for the isolated book demo.

- Book atmosphere view consistency (Eric, 2026-11-01): Default the page editor's fog to Angle-consistent, using surface height and a fixed authored optical thickness rather than camera-ray distance/height. Preserve the fog bank's height/softness and independent darkness. Keep the previous integrated Depth-based mode as an explicit option, and save mode/thickness per page with backward-compatible document loading.

- Book page editor overhead and text selection (Eric, 2026-11-01): Enter page editing in a fully top-down camera posture, fitted to the available authoring surface. Apply that posture on entry rather than forcing it each frame, preserving camera overrides and a Top view reset. Existing printed text must support direct selection, bounding boxes, resizing and text editing just like inserted text controls; offer double-click text editing and keep the text field readily accessible in the inspector.

- Responsive book composition (Eric, 2026-11-01): Establish a safe-frame system for book.html that centers and fits subjects within unobstructed scene space, accounting for authoring panels, mobile controls and device safe areas. Provide canvas/viewing/book-center guides, a safe-frame outline and center readout, Frame book/Frame selection/Reframe actions, a configurable margin, and real desktop/tablet/phone aspect previews. Default ordinary views to fitting the whole book. Manual camera overrides release automatic fitting until Reframe. Responsive shot captures retain subject, angle and margin with raw-camera fallback; preserve old fixed shots and disk-save compatibility. Use mobile drawers so editing panels leave useful scene space; retain correct picking and handles in preview viewports.

- Compact book JSON export (Eric, 2026-11-01): Remove inline shot JSON readouts. Keep explicit copy actions for shot/scene documents and current framing/camera measurements; retain import through a paste-only disclosure rather than displaying generated JSON in the shot panel.

- Compact book editor controls and grouping (Eric, 2026-11-01): Rename the scene navigator Scene Editor and use icon-only action buttons with tooltips and accessible names. Place Capture shot and Play/Pause/Stop on the second row. Stack Page Editor, Fold and Framing as right-edge icon controls; hide Controls help and floating camera details by default. Put live camera information in a Page Editor Camera tab. Group page controls into Left page, Right page and Cross-page from their bounds, including objects spanning both leaves. Offer Delete shot in the shot context menu with an explicit confirm/cancel modal. Hide the home page Wake the seed button while retaining object interaction.

- Scene Editor import removal (Eric, 2026-11-01): Remove the Import scene JSON disclosure and import action from the Scene Editor; retain copy/export and disk save/reload.

- Scene Editor details split (Eric, 2026-11-01): Omit the repeated selected scene/chapter name from the lower inspector. Give the tree remaining vertical space and make the inspector vertically resizable through a draggable, keyboard-accessible divider. Preserve the chosen split locally and keep the tree/inspector independently scrollable.

- Book sketch/paint authoring (Eric, 2026-11-01): Interior pages initially present their existing raster artwork as estimated outline sketches, with an optional automatic ink-spread color reveal using the supplied 40-frame sprite rather than a uniform rising wipe. Author held sketch props and rotatable/resizable paint regions, with named runtime flags and explicit scene-tree paint breakpoints. Persist initial page settings/regions in bookPages.json and breakpoint timing/targets in bookScenes.json; preserve session paint progress across revisits, reduced motion, pause and page-fold/bake behavior. Keep prose legible and editor region helpers out of presentation/bakes. Add luminance-bucket SVG compilation/reveal as a separate reusable paint technique and preview, including direction, seeded jitter and style auditing.

- Book viewport and resize refinements (Eric, 2026-11-01): Remove nav#book-pages and its bottom page-name/arrow bar, reclaiming its vertical safe-frame reservation; retain page-edge and keyboard navigation. Corner resizing of text, props and paint regions anchors the opposite corner, including rotated controls. Constrain the requested size at authoring limits rather than moving that anchor; allow signed elevation for anchored prop resizing.

- Continuous authored book opening (Eric, 2026-11-01): Combine the front-cover opening and arrival at Home Tree Opening view into one slow camera sweep toward the saved upper-left composition, without an intermediate center stop or second flight. Pass the arriving authored camera shot into physical page navigation, consume its travel during that transition and preserve its hold/following shots. Direct cover clicks use the same saved opening view; retain reduced motion, responsive framing and manual camera overrides.

- Book combat arena scaffold (Eric, 2026-11-01): `book.html?combatsbx` mounts an isolated combat presentation inside a fully opened two-page book. A continuous tree perimeter encloses dirt and grass clutter, with the combat Hero on the left and a combat enemy on the right. Keep a stable sandbox index and readiness hook for future battle wiring; this initial scaffold does not alter campaign battles or story authoring saves.

- Book reference-frame refactor (Eric, 2026-11-02): Use a shared 1920×1080 logical authoring frame for book camera composition, independent of the browser viewport and GPU framebuffer resolution. New fixed-shot captures normalize camera distance into that frame and persist composition metadata; replay adapts distance to the unobstructed viewport while preserving target, angle and FOV. Existing shots remain readable and retain their stored data, with 16:9 as the reference fallback. Keep subject-framing, page/editor safe areas, manual camera overrides and continuous cover-opening handoff. Provide a 16:9 authoring preview/guide and independent Auto/Low/Medium/High render budgets (also selectable with `?quality=`). Auto caps rendering near a 1080p pixel budget rather than forcing a 1080p canvas. Combat sandbox defaults to the whole arena in landscape and a closer fighter-centered composition in portrait; expose Whole arena and Combat focus overrides. Portrait focus may crop the book perimeter deliberately, superseding the sandbox's earlier always-fit-whole-book policy. Character world sizes and spacing remain unchanged by viewport or quality changes.

- Blank opening spread for authoring (Eric, 2026-11-02): Clear both 01A and 01B of built-in props, Hero, ground clutter, prose, interaction markers and page effects. Page 01 is an empty base for Page Editor authoring; reset-to-default stays blank. Preserve the book, navigation, camera shots, reusable artwork and other spreads. A per-page content epoch prevents stale browser drafts from restoring the removed layout while retaining unrelated spread drafts and all newly authored content after the reset. This supersedes the earlier Home Tree scenery/ground-fill requirements for page 01 only.

- Flurry Rush abilities (Eric, 2026-11-02, combat design direction; not yet implemented): Some special ability cards for the player party introduce a "solve quickly" mechanic. The player purchases the card with AP and uses it on a target, then powers up that single ability by rapidly solving tableau cards against a real-time countdown. The number of legal tableau cards played to the ability's foundation enhances its efficacy and effects. An ability may contain multiple stages within one combo, each with its own respective foundation and individually powered by the cards solved to that foundation. The player must balance building the current stage's power against advancing through as many stages as possible before the countdown expires. Completed stages trigger their earned effects; incomplete stages do not trigger. Failing to finish the whole combo does not cancel already completed stages, but lingering on stage 1 of a five-stage combo forfeits the opportunity to trigger the remaining stages. AP prices, countdown durations, stage completion/advancement rules and effect scaling remain to be authored and tuned per ability.

- Book paint-region authoring (Eric, 2026-11-02): Page Editor action toolbars use compact icon buttons with explanatory hover tooltips and accessible names. `&Edit=true` opens the requested page directly in editing mode with the normal top-down authoring view. Paint regions explicitly choose paper wash, artwork reveal or both; provide Live Paint generator presets, palettes, reproducible seed selection/customization, tuning, reveal style, timing, flags and previews. Persist full recipes with the page document; legacy reveal-only regions remain valid. Actual wash pigment remains visible outside editing and in page-turn bakes, while selection guides stay editor-only.

- Procedural watercolor book paper (Eric, 2026-11-02): Adapt the static paper method from msurguy/watercolor-playground (MIT, pinned source and license under src/book/vendor). Bake deterministic Cold Press paper height/mottle/fibre fields, relief lighting and color into reusable textures for open sheets, combat-sandbox paper and turning leaves. Keep physical grain scale independent of viewport size, preserve blank page-01 content and existing authored art, and avoid per-frame regeneration/readback. Further wet-paper fluid simulation, pigment-aware mixing and grain-dependent deposition are recommendations for later investigation, not implemented behavior. Review: docs/research/watercolor-playground-review.md.

- Book cover/leaf rendering ownership (Eric, 2026-11-02): Keep the revealed spread hidden while the cover hinge is moving. Present flattened cached artwork on the hinged inside-cover face and stationary opposing page, then hand over to live scenery only after the cover is laid flat. Closing folds the live scene before hinge motion. Use the same page dimensions, gutter offset and readable UV orientation for cached planes and travelling leaves, preserving the continuous authored camera sweep. Pin snapshots for the animation's lifetime so invalidation or LRU eviction cannot free displayed textures; compare paint-state revisions before reusing snapshots and flush current paint uniforms when capturing. Bake each spread with its own atmosphere/lighting and avoid applying fog twice to cached surfaces. Keep editor guides out of captures and release snapshot leases after presentation hands back to the live scene.

- Viewport-aware page insertion (Eric, 2026-11-02): New text boxes, popup props and paint regions are placed on the visible part of the current page nearest the unobstructed camera-view center, accounting for editor panels, aspect previews and mobile drawers. Fit their initial dimensions to that visible area when zoomed in, keep the camera posture unchanged and retain page-edge margins. If no usable paper area is visible, explain that the user must pan back or zoom out rather than creating an off-screen control.

- Book editor object placement (Eric, 2026-11-03): select an object type and drag its preview from the page editor onto the paper to place it at the drop point. Retain snapping, undo and page-document persistence; off-paper releases and Escape cancel insertion. See docs/book-page-editor.md.

- Forest camera clearance (Eric, 2026-11-03): hide an entire scenery relief, including both transitioning poses, when the camera enters or approaches its oriented extruded bounds. The forest lab uses a 1 m entry clearance and restores beyond 1.5 m to avoid boundary flicker, including flight/tilted views. Keep source paintings and object-local pose clocks intact; this is visibility handling in the isolated lab.

- Forest character relief trial (Eric, 2026-11-03): include static Hero and adult Demi props on the forest lab's starting path, using cropped battle sprite frames and independently estimated depth maps. Share depth strength, fog, object-local pose phasing and camera-clearance hiding. These are relief props, with no battle or follower behavior.

- Forest character orientation (Eric, 2026-11-03): Hero and Demi's battle relief props face inward toward each other with fixed upright world orientations. Mirror Hero's painted frame and its depth sampling together. Camera orbit, flight, Upright and tree blend controls must not rotate these character props; retain fog, depth strength and proximity hiding. This supersedes camera-following pose phasing for the two characters only.

- Forest directional combat actors (Eric, 2026-11-03): replace Demi with the dark slime; stage Hero and slime six metres apart facing each other. Use combat's heading-relative, hysteretic low/high/overhead sprite selection and billboard posture, with matching independently estimated depth per authored frame. Crossfade outgoing/incoming sprite views using the tree blend duration and equal-power object-local opacity, preserving the outgoing view and clock during rapid camera travel. Keep proximity hiding for both views, fog and depth controls. This supersedes fixed character planes and the Demi prop in this isolated lab; no combat simulation is added.

## Village slice (Eric, 2026-11-23)

- `village.html` (`src/village/`) uses movable Mochi, Hero, Banks and Demi tokens. Stack tokens to form a party and bring it to a biome node to work there. Forest defaults to Aspen Grove. Tap or click an outlined aspen to zoom in on it and reveal its own seeded seven-column tableau and a foundation per deployed kin. Each new tree rolls a stable maximum of 9–14 HP and retains its own puzzle progress. Show its health bar when selected or damaged. Use Proto’s Khad Roots Exposed translucent watercolor card treatment with readable dark rank plaques and continuous scenery behind the cards. A legal receipt deals exactly one damage. A felled tree stays highlighted in the grove until clicked or tapped, then adds its lumber to the city inventory with a pickup animation. Berry Patch and Quarry retain their existing puzzle rules.
- Daylight is the only day limiter and is tied to receipts: tableau plays cost very little daylight, stock draws cost much more (balance pass in `docs/research/village-daylight-balance.md`, `tools/village-daylight-sim.ts`).
- Look: polished, modern and bespoke (clean panels, hairlines, dense tables, Melvor-like information density) with material playing cards inside. It replaces the wood/felt/brass tabletop for this page. No legal-move highlights.
- Validation: `node tools/village-layout-playwright.cjs <baseUrl>` covers the map, drawer panes or sheets, drag and tap placing with its ghost preview (valid and blocked), inspect/take down, minimap + puzzle for each surface and the night summary, running the shared layout check (with `forbidScroll`) at 1912x914, 1280x720, Galaxy Z Fold 7 unfolded (884x1000) and folded cover (412x915), and both screens in landscape. Nothing scrolls: smaller screens open Build/Kin/Hollow as sheets from a tab bar instead.
- Village grid map (Eric, 2026-11-24; design in `docs/research/village-grid-map.md`): the map replaces the left rail. One 9×7 grid of square tiles holds the Forest at (−2, 2), Berry Patch at (2, 2), Curious Sapling at (0, 0) and the gate at (−4, −1). Each occupies one square. The Curious Sapling bears the Aldan Seed. The Quarry is removed from the village. World references use signed (x, y) offsets from the sapling, with east and north positive. The displayed grid is the central patch. On mouse hover, show the square’s reference below the lower-right corner. The grid fits whole, and portrait cover screens rotate the view while preserving every square’s reference. Adjacency is edge-only.
- No walking (Eric, 2026-11-24): the player is a deity-like presence. Tapping a feature works it at once (no walk-out daylight cost) and the map shrinks into a top-left minimap beside the puzzle; tapping the minimap or Leave grows it back. One run at a time: working another feature banks the open run.
- Position rules (Eric, 2026-11-24): building combos stay, and buildings touching a feature earn site bonuses (Woodshed by the Forest +1 lumber per felled tree; Drying Rack by the Berry Patch stops spoilage, elsewhere halves it; Lantern Post by the Quarry +1 on deep rock). A Campfire may not touch the Forest. Placement shows a ghost preview of appeal, combos, bonuses or the reason it's blocked.
- Unbuild (Eric, 2026-11-24): any building can be taken down for a partial refund (`REFUND_RATE` 0.5, rounded down; future upgrades raise it to full) and rebuilt elsewhere, except shelter a resident needs.
- Map art (Eric, 2026-11-24): placeholder art until Eric supplies the authored set (`docs/art/village-art-assets.md`). The map ground is painted with the `portals.html?tree` grove-floor method per ART.md (shared radial-dab washes, painted grass and flecks), baked once per size and cached.
- Isometric village view (Eric, 2026-11-27; convention in `docs/art/village-isometric-sprites.md`): a toggle in the HUD switches `village.html` between the top-down map and an isometric map (`?iso`, `?iso=0`); the minimap and the `?book` spread stay top-down. One fixed camera: 45° heading, true-isometric pitch (35.26°), so a tile diamond is √3 : 1. Sprites are transparent PNGs in 256 × 256 cells; Hero's ground anchor is bottom centre near (128, 239), and Hero has eight screen-relative headings from five painted views plus mirroring. Footprints are placement metadata (width × depth in tiles), kept apart from each sprite's declared pivot. Flat terrain carries painted shading; tiles rise only where elevation matters for movement or occlusion, with the height step set in world units and projected by the renderer. Generated placeholder blocks stand in until art lands; Hero's sheets go in `public/assets/runtime/kin/hero/sprites/world/animal-forward-world-v1/`.
